# PART VII: MEMORIAL SATURDAY, GREAT FEAST OF HOLY PENTECOST, KNEELING PRAYERS AT VESPERS, AND SUNDAY OF ALL SAINTS

## GREAT FEAST OF HOLY PENTECOST AND ALL SAINTS[^2267]

## ON THE SUNDAY OF HOLY PENTECOST, ON SATURDAY EVENING, AT LITTLE VESPERS

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 4 stichera, and sing the stichera of the Feast.*[^2268]  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*  
Today the nations are renewed to declare more clearly the marvelous deeds of God in words of other tongues, as in the form of fire, for the strengthening of all who dwell upon the earth: today for a sign unto the nations in truth hast Thou come, O Salvation.[^2269]

*Sticheron:*  
Fulfilling the power from on high which Thou didst promise to send down unto Thy Disciples, O Christ, Thou sendest Thy Holy Spirit, showing forth unto us that Thou art the Truth, and those who hope in Thee, O God, are truly not put to shame.[^2270]

*Sticheron:*  
Fulfilling the promise of Thy Comforter, Thou hast sent Him unto the world as in fiery tongues, to consume the sins of all with divine fire, and to grant unto us true communion with Him, as we faithfully glorify Him as God.[^2271]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 7:*  
Having Christ God as Comforter with the Father, today, as another Comforter hath come unto us upon the earth, let us with faith worship the Holy Spirit.[^2272]

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
Having their minds enlightened by the divine-originating radiance, Thy Disciples, O Word, received the Holy Spirit essentially.[^2273]

*Verse:* Create in me a clean heart, O God: and renew a right spirit within me.[^2274]  
*Sticheron:*  
Tongues with fire, visibly seen from on high, descended upon the Apostles, being divided, enlightening, not burning.

*Verse:* Cast me not away from Thy presence: and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me.[^2275]  
*Sticheron:*  
Having come from on high unto us, O Comforter, as formerly unto the Apostles: sanctify and save those who proclaim Thee as God.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 1:*  
The Holy Spirit provides all things: He gushes forth prophecies, He perfects priests, He taught wisdom to the unlettered, He showed forth fishermen as theologians, He gathers together the whole assembly of the Church: O Consubstantial and Co-Enthroned with the Father and the Son, O Comforter, glory to Thee.[^2276]

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... Trisagion... Most Holy Trinity... Our Father... For Thine is the Kingdom...*  
*Dismissal Troparion, Tone 8:*  
Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast revealed the fishermen to be all-wise, having sent down upon them the Holy Spirit, and through them caught the universe in a net: O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee.[^2277]  
*And the rest customarily. And the dismissal.*

## ON THE GREAT FEAST OF PENTECOST, AT GREAT VESPERS[^2278]

*The customary stichology (Blessed is the man).*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 stichera, and sing 3 idiomela stichera of the first tone, repeating the first; then 5 of the second tone, repeating the first.*[^2279]  
*Tone 1:*  
*Sticheron (sung twice):*  
We celebrate Pentecost, and the Coming of the Spirit, and the appointing of the promise, and the fulfillment of hope, and the mystery, how great and precious it is! Therefore we cry unto Thee: O Creator of all, Lord, glory to Thee.[^2280]

*Sticheron:*  
With the tongues of other nations Thou hast renewed Thy Disciples, O Christ, that through them they might proclaim Thee, the Immortal Word and God, Who grantest great mercy unto our souls.[^2281]

*Sticheron:*  
The Holy Spirit provides all things: He gushes forth prophecies, He perfects priests, He taught wisdom to the unlettered, He showed forth fishermen as theologians, He gathers together the whole assembly of the Church: O Consubstantial and Co-Enthroned with the Father and the Son, O Comforter, glory to Thee.[^2282]

*Tone 2:*  
*Sticheron (sung twice):*  
We have seen the true Light; we have received the Heavenly Spirit; we have found the true Faith, worshipping the Undivided Trinity: for the Same hath saved us.[^2283]

*Sticheron:*  
In the Prophets Thou didst make known unto us the way of salvation, and in the Apostles the grace of Thy Spirit hath shone forth, O our Savior: Thou art God first, Thou art also after these, and unto the ages Thou art our God.[^2284]

*Sticheron:*  
In Thy courts I will hymn Thee, the Savior of the world, and bowing the knees, I will worship Thine invincible power, at evening, and morning, and at midday: and at every time I will bless Thee, O Lord.[^2285]

*Sticheron:*  
In Thy courts, O Lord, we, the faithful, bowing the knees of souls and bodies, hymn Thee, the Unoriginate Father, and the Co-unoriginate Son, and the Co-everlasting and Most Holy Spirit, Who enlightens and sanctifies our souls.[^2286]

*Sticheron:*  
We praise in hymnody the Consubstantial Trinity: the Father and the Son, with the Holy Spirit: for thus all the Prophets and the Apostles with the Martyrs proclaimed.[^2287]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
*Composition of Emperor Leo:*  
Come, ye peoples, let us worship the Thrice-Hypostatic Godhead: the Son in the Father, with the Holy Spirit: for timelessly the Father begat the Son, co-everlasting and co-enthroned, and the Holy Spirit was in the Father, glorified with the Son: one power, one essence, one Godhead: Whom worshipping, we all say: Holy God, Who madest all things through the Son, with the cooperation of the Holy Spirit; Holy Mighty, through Whom we knew the Father, and through Whom the Holy Spirit came into the world; Holy Immortal, the Comforting Spirit, proceeding from the Father and resting in the Son: O Holy Trinity, glory to Thee.[^2288]

*Entrance with the censer.*  
*O Joyful Light:*  
*Prokeimenon of the day, and the readings.*[^2289]  
*The Reading from Numbers (Num. 11:16-17, 24-29). Tone 1:*  
The Lord said unto Moses: Gather unto Me seventy men of the elders of Israel, whom thou thyself knowest to be the elders of the people, and their scribes; and thou shalt bring them unto the tabernacle of witness, and they shall stand there with thee. And I will come down, and talk with thee there, and I will take of the Spirit Which is upon thee, and will put It upon them; and they shall bear with thee the burden of the people, and thou shalt not bear them thyself alone. And Moses gathered seventy men of the elders of the people, and set them round about the tabernacle. And the Lord came down in a cloud, and spake unto Moses, and took of the Spirit that was upon him, and put It upon the seventy men that were elders; and when the Spirit rested upon them, they prophesied in the camp, and after that did not cease. But there remained two men in the camp, the name of the one was Eldad, and the name of the other Medad; and the Spirit rested upon them, and these were of them that were written, but went not unto the tabernacle, and they prophesied in the camp. And there ran a young man, and told Moses, and spake, saying: Eldad and Medad do prophesy in the camp. And Joshua the son of Nun, who stood before Moses, his chosen one, answered and said: My lord Moses, forbid them. And Moses said unto him: Enviest thou for my sake? Would that all the Lord's people were prophets, when the Lord shall put His Spirit upon them.[^2290]

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Joel (Joel 2:23-32). Chapter 2:*  
Thus says the Lord: Rejoice, ye children of Zion, and be glad in the Lord your God: for He hath given you food unto righteousness, and He will cause to rain down for you the early and the latter rain, as aforetime. And the threshing-floors shall be full of wheat, and the vats shall overflow with wine and oil. And I will restore to you the years that the locust hath eaten, and the caterpillar, and the blight, and the cankerworm, My great army which I sent upon you; and eating ye shall eat and be satisfied, and praise the Name of the Lord your God, Who hath dealt marvelously with you; and My people shall not be put to shame unto the age. And ye shall know that I am in the midst of Israel, and that I am the Lord your God, and there is none else beside Me; and My people shall no more be put to shame unto the age. And it shall come to pass afterward, that I will pour out of My Spirit upon all flesh; and your sons and your daughters shall prophesy, and your old men shall dream dreams, and your young men shall see visions. And also upon My bondmen and upon My bondmaidens in those days will I pour out of My Spirit, and they shall prophesy. And I will show wonders in heaven above, and signs on the earth beneath: blood, and fire, and vapor of smoke; the sun shall be turned into darkness, and the moon into blood, before the coming of the great and terrible day of the Lord. And it shall come to pass, that whosoever shall call on the Name of the Lord shall be saved.[^2291]

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Ezek. 36:24-28). Chapter 36:*  
Thus says the Lord: I will take you from among the nations, and gather you out of all the strange lands, and bring you into your own land; and I will sprinkle clean water upon you, and ye shall be cleansed from all your uncleannesses, and from all your idols will I cleanse you. And I will give you a new heart, and a new Spirit will I put within you; and I will take away the stony heart out of your flesh, and I will give you a heart of flesh; and I will put My Spirit within you, and cause you to walk in My statutes, and ye shall keep My judgments and do them. And ye shall dwell in the land that I gave to your fathers; and ye shall be My people, and I will be your God.[^2292]

*At the Litija, 3 idiomela stichera.*  
*Tone 2:*  
In the Prophets Thou didst make known unto us the way of salvation, and in the Apostles the grace of Thy Spirit hath shone forth, O our Savior: Thou art God first, Thou art also after these, and unto the ages Thou art our God.[^2293]

*Sticheron:*  
In Thy courts I will hymn Thee, the Savior of the world, and bowing the knees, I will worship Thine invincible power: at evening, and morning, and at midday, and at every time I will bless Thee, O Lord.

*Sticheron:*  
In Thy courts, O Lord, we, the faithful, bowing the knees of souls and bodies, hymn Thee, the Unoriginate Father, and the Co-unoriginate Son, and the Co-everlasting and Most Holy Spirit, Who enlightens and sanctifies our souls.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
When Thou didst send down Thy Spirit, O Lord, as the Apostles were sitting, then the Hebrew children, beholding it, were seized with amazement: for they heard them speaking in other, strange tongues, as the Spirit gave them utterance; for being unlearned, they were made wise, and having caught the nations unto the faith, they proclaimed divine things; therefore we also cry unto Thee: O Thou Who didst appear upon the earth, and didst save us from delusion: Lord, glory to Thee.[^2294]

*At the Aposticha, idiomela stichera.*  
*Tone 6:*  
The nations, not understanding the power of the Most Holy Spirit that came upon Thine Apostles, O Lord, deemed the diversity of tongues to be drunkenness: but we, having been strengthened by them, unceasingly say thus: Take not Thy Holy Spirit from us, we pray Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^2295]

*Verse:* Create in me a clean heart, O God: and renew a right spirit within me.[^2296]  
*Sticheron:*  
O Lord, the descent of the Holy Spirit, which filled Thine Apostles, caused them to speak in other tongues; therefore this marvelous wonder seemed unto the unbelieving to be drunkenness, but unto the faithful a cause of salvation: account us also worthy of His illumination, we pray Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Verse:* Cast me not away from Thy presence: and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me.[^2297]  
*Sticheron:*  
O Heavenly King, O Comforter, the Spirit of Truth, Who art everywhere and fillest all things, Treasury of blessings and Giver of life: come and dwell in us, and cleanse us from every impurity, and save our souls, O Good One.

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 8:*  
Of old the tongues were confused on account of the audacity of the tower-building; but now the tongues are made wise for the sake of the glory of the knowledge of God: there He condemned the ungodly through transgression; here Christ hath enlightened fishermen by the Spirit: then discord was wrought unto punishment; now harmony is renewed unto the salvation of our souls.[^2298]

*Dismissal Troparion, Tone 8:*  
Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast revealed the fishermen to be all-wise, having sent down upon them the Holy Spirit, and through them caught the universe in a net: O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee. *(Thrice)*[^2299]

*Then the blessing of the loaves. And the reading from the Acts of the Holy Apostles.*  
*If, however, there be no All-Night Vigil: After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," the troparion of the feast once.*  
*At Compline, after "It is truly meet," and after the Trisagion, the kontakion of the feast.*  
*We chant the Midnight Office according to custom.*  
*We chant the Triadicon Canon of the Octoechos, in the tone of the week.*[^2300]

## ON THE SUNDAY OF PENTECOST, AT MATINS[^2301]

*At "God is the Lord," the troparion of the feast, thrice.*  
*After the 1st Stichology, the Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*  
Let us, the faithful, brightly celebrate the feast after the feast, and the final feast: this is Pentecost, the fulfillment and setting forth of the promise; for on this day the fire of the Comforter came down upon the earth, as in the form of tongues, and enlightened the Disciples, and showed them forth as heavenly initiates of the mysteries: the light of the Comforter hath come, and enlightened the world.[^2302]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*  
*And there is a reading from the Commentary of John: Homily 50: "On the last day..." In two sections.*[^2303]

*After the 2nd Kathisma, the Sessional Hymn, in the same Tone and Podoben:*  
The Fountain of the Spirit, having come upon the earth, dividing Himself noetically into fiery rivers, bedewed the Apostles, enlightening them; and the cloud became unto them bedewing fire, enlightening them and raining down flame, through which we have received grace, by fire and by water: the light of the Comforter hath come, and enlightened the world.[^2304]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*  
*Then the Polyeleos; and we sing the*  
*Megalynarion:*  
We magnify Thee, O Life-Giving Christ, and we honor Thy Most Holy Spirit, Whom Thou didst send from the Father unto Thy Divine Disciples.[^2305]

*Verses:*  
*1. The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands (Ps. 18:2 LXX).*[^2306]  
*2. By the breath of His mouth is all their power (Ps. 32:6 LXX).*  
*3. Fire shall go before Him, and burn up His enemies round about (Ps. 96:3 LXX).*  
*4. Thou shalt send forth Thy Spirit, and they shall be created, and Thou shalt renew the face of the earth (Ps. 103:30 LXX).*  
*5. Thy good Spirit shall guide me in the land of uprightness (Ps. 142:10 LXX).*  
*6. Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*  
*7. Cast me not away from Thy presence, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*  
*8. The Lord will give strength unto His people; the Lord will bless His people with peace (Ps. 28:11 LXX).*  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*  
*And the reading.*

*And a reading in the Theologian.*[^2307]  
*After the Polyeleos, the Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded secretly" (Повелѣ́нное та́йно):*[^2308]  
After Thine Arising from the tomb, O Christ, and Thy Divine Ascension unto the heavenly height, Thou didst send down Thy glory upon the God-beholders, O Compassionate One, renewing a right Spirit within the Disciples; therefore, as a musical harp, they mystically resounded unto all, through the divine plectrum, Thy sayings and Thy dispensation, O Savior.[^2309]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*  
*And a reading in the Theologian.*[^2310]  
*The Songs of Ascents: The 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^2311]  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*  
Thy good Spirit shall guide me in the land of uprightness (Ps. 142:10 LXX).[^2312]  
*Verse:* O Lord, hear my prayer: give ear unto my supplication (Ps. 142:1 LXX).[^2313]  
*Let every breath.*  
*The Gospel according to John, Pericope 65 (John 20:19-23):* When it was late on that same day... *The End:* Whosesoever sins ye remit, they are remitted.[^2314]  
*"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ" is not said, but straightway Psalm 50.*[^2315]  
*Then, Glory:* Through the prayers of the Apostles... *Both now:* Through the prayers of the Theotokos... *Then the Verse:* Have mercy on me, O God...  
*Sticheron, Tone 6:* O Heavenly King...[^2316]  
*Two canons of the feast. The heirmoi of both canons are sung twice, and the troparia to 12.*  
*One choir sings the first heirmos, and the other choir sings the other heirmos.*[^2317]  
*The first canon, whose acrostic is:* "We celebrate Pentecost" (Πεντηκοστὴν ἑορτάζομεν).  
*The composition of Kosmas the Monk.*[^2318]  
*Tone 7. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
He Who broke battles with a lofty arm covered Pharaoh with chariots in the deep: let us sing unto Him, for He hath been glorified.[^2319]

*Troparion:*  
Having in deeds, as of old Thou didst promise unto the Disciples, sent the Comforter, the Spirit, O Christ, Thou hast shone forth light unto the world, O Lover of mankind.[^2320]

*Troparion:*  
That which was of old proclaimed by the Law and the Prophets is fulfilled: for today the grace of the Divine Spirit hath been poured out upon all the faithful.[^2321]

*Another canon, iambic, whose acrostic is:*  
*Θειογενὲς Λόγε, Πνεῦμα Παράκλητον πάλιν ἄλλον,*  
*Ἐκ Γενέτου κόλπων ἧκας ἐπιχθονίοις,*  
*Οἷα πυρὸς γλώσσῃσι φέρον Θεότητος ἀΰλου,*  
*Σῆμα τεῆς φύτλης, καὶ χάριν ὑμνοπόλοις.*  
*Which is:*  
*O Word, Born of God, another Comforter, the Spirit,*  
*From the Father's bosom Thou hast sent unto those upon the earth,*  
*Bearing as in fiery tongues the sign of Thine immaterial Godhead's*  
*Lineage, and grace unto the hymn-singers.*[^2322]  
*The composition of Kyr John of Arklai.*[^2323]

*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Covered by the divine darkness, the slow-tongued one enunciated the God-written Law; for having shaken off the mire from the eye of the mind, he beholds Him Who Is, and is instructed in the knowledge of the Spirit, praising with divine songs.[^2324]

*Troparion:*  
The pure and venerable lips spake: There shall be no separation for you, O friends; for I, sitting upon the Father's lofty Throne, will pour out the Spirit, that ungrudging grace may shine forth upon those who desire It.[^2325]

*Troparion:*  
The most true Word, Who had passed beyond the boundary, gently makes perfect the heart; for having finished the work, Christ gladdened His friends, granting the Spirit with a violent breath and tongues of fire, even as He had promised.[^2326]

*Katavasia:*  
He Who broke battles... Covered by the divine darkness...[^2327]  
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Thou didst say unto the Disciples, O Christ: Tarry ye in Jerusalem until ye be endued with power from on high; and I will send another Comforter like unto Myself, My Spirit and the Father's, in Whom ye shall be established.[^2328]

*Troparion:*  
The power of the Divine Spirit, having come, divinely united into one harmony the tongues that of old had been divided when they evilly agreed together, instructing the faithful through the knowledge of the Trinity, in Whom we are established.[^2329]

*Another Heirmos:*  
The solitary prayer of the Prophetess Anna of old, bearing a contrite spirit unto the Mighty One and the God of knowledge, loosed the bonds of the barren womb, and the intolerable reproach of the mother blessed with children.[^2330]

*Troparion:*  
Incomprehensible is the Sovereign Godhead; for by the flashing of the Spirit, He revealed unlettered men to be orators, wise fishermen muzzling the wise with their word, and delivering countless peoples from profound night.[^2331]

*Troparion:*  
The all-powerful, shining Light was proceeding from the Unbegotten Light, Whom through the Son of the Father's dominion, the united radiance, the fiery voice in Zion now reveals unto the nations.[^2332]

*Then the Sessional Hymn.*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded secretly" (Повелѣ́нное та́йно):*  
The lovers of the Savior were filled with joy, and they who formerly were afraid received boldness; for today the Holy Spirit came down from on high upon the house of the Disciples, and one spake one thing and another another unto the peoples; for the tongues, visibly divided as fire, rested upon them, and did not consume them, but rather bedewed them.[^2333]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*  
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
Observing Thy coming in the latter days, O Christ, the Prophet cried aloud: I have heard of Thy power, O Lord, that Thou art come to save all Thine anointed ones.[^2334]

*Troparion:*  
He Who spake in the Prophets, and was proclaimed beforehand by the Law unto the imperfect of old, the true God, the Comforter, is made known today unto the ministers and witnesses of the Word.[^2335]

*Troparion:*  
Bearing the sign of the Godhead, the Spirit was divided in fire unto the Apostles, and appeared in strange tongues: for the Father's Divine Power that comes is sovereign.[^2336]

*Another Heirmos:*  
O King of kings, Who art One of the One, O Word Who didst proceed from the Uncaused Father, Thou hast truly sent unto the Apostles Thine equally powerful Spirit, as the Benefactor, unto those who sing: Glory to Thy dominion, O Lord.[^2337]

*Troparion:*  
Having prepared the divine bath of regeneration by the Word, Thou dost rain down upon me, O Word of God, a stream of composite nature from Thy non-corruptly pierced Side, sealing it with the warmth of the Spirit.[^2338]

*Troparion:*  
All bend the knee unto the Comforter, and unto the Son of the Father, and unto the Co-originate Father; for in three Persons they have beheld the true, intangible, timeless, one Essence: for the light, the grace of the Spirit, hath shone forth.[^2339]

*Troparion:*  
May all who are ministers of the Thrice-Radiant Essence be filled with the Sovereign Godhead; for beyond nature Christ makes perfect as the Benefactor, and illuminates with fire unto salvation, granting all the grace of the Spirit.[^2340]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
The Spirit of salvation, conceived through Thy fear in the womb of the Prophets, O Lord, and brought forth upon the earth, creates the hearts of the Apostles clean, and is renewed right within the faithful: for Thy commandments are light and peace.[^2341]

*Troparion:*  
This power which hath come today is the Good Spirit, the Spirit of God's wisdom, the Spirit Who proceeds from the Father, and through the Son is revealed unto us, the faithful, as the Giver, in whom He dwells by nature of holiness, in which He is beheld.[^2342]

*Another Heirmos:*  
Receive the fire-inspired dew of the Spirit, a liberating purification of sins, O radiant children of the Church; for now the Law hath gone forth from Zion, the fire-formed grace of the Spirit in tongues.[^2343]

*Troparion:*  
Even as He was sovereignly well-pleased, the unmastered Spirit proceeds from the Father, making the Apostles wise in tongues, sealing the life-bearing, paternal, conformed Word, Which the Savior spake.[^2344]

*Troparion:*  
That He might heal the minds from sin, God the Word, the Supreme Origin, prepared the Apostles as a most pure house, in which the light of the Spirit, equally powerful and consubstantial, now makes His abode.[^2345]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Sailing in the surge of worldly cares, overwhelmed with the ship by sins, and cast unto the soul-corrupting beast, like Jonah, O Christ, I cry unto Thee: Lead me up from the death-bearing deep.[^2346]

*Troparion:*  
From Thy Spirit upon all flesh, even as Thou didst say, Thou hast richly poured out, and all things are filled with the knowledge of Thee, O Lord: for from the Father the Son was incorruptly born, and the Undivided Spirit proceeded.[^2347]

*Another Heirmos:*  
A cleansing and salvation for us, O Master Christ, Thou didst shine forth from the Virgin, that like the Prophet Jonah from the belly of the sea beast, Thou mightest snatch the whole fallen Adam of all humanity from corruption.[^2348]

*Troparion:*  
Renew within us who have received Him eternally a true and right Spirit in our inward parts, O Almighty One, Who proceedest from the Father, and wholly unitest, burning the matter of the hateful one, and cleansing the stains of our minds.[^2349]

*Troparion:*  
Unto the Apostles, the citizens of Zion who awaited Thy coming, Thou, O Spirit, dost confirm with fire-breathed inspiration the longed-for inheritance, the knowledge of the Word born of the Father, swiftly showing forth the harsh speech of the flattery of the nations.[^2350]

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*  
When the Most High came down and confused the tongues, He divided the nations; but when He distributed the tongues of fire, He called all into unity: and with one accord we glorify the All-Holy Spirit.[^2351]

*Ikos:*  
Grant swift and steadfast consolation unto Thy servants, O Jesus, when our spirits are despondent; separate not Thyself from our souls in afflictions; depart not from our minds in distresses; but ever anticipate us: draw near unto us, draw near, Thou Who art everywhere present; even as Thou art ever with Thine Apostles, so also unite Thyself unto us who desire Thee, O Compassionate One: that being united, we may hymn Thee and glorify the All-Holy Spirit.[^2352]

*Synaxarion for the Sunday of Holy Pentecost.*[^2353]  
*Verses:*  
With violent breath Christ imparts in tongues of fire  
The Divine Spirit unto the Apostles.  
The Spirit was poured out in a great day upon the fishermen.  

On this day, on the seventh Sunday after Pascha, we celebrate Holy Pentecost, and this also we received from the Hebrew books.[^2354] For even as they celebrate the Pentecost among them, honoring the number seven, and because, fifty days having passed after the Passover, they received the Law: so also we, celebrating fifty days after Pascha, receive the All-Holy Spirit, Who gives the Law, and guides unto all truth, and commands what is pleasing unto God. It should be known that among the Hebrews there were three feasts: the Passover, Pentecost, and Scenopegia, that is, the Feast of Tabernacles.[^2355] The Passover indeed they celebrated in remembrance of the crossing of the Red Sea: for Passover is interpreted as "passing over." And that feast pointed to our passing over and return from dark sin back unto Paradise. Pentecost they celebrated in remembrance of their affliction in the wilderness, and how through many sorrows they were led into the promised land: for then also they enjoyed the fruit of wheat and wine. And this feast pointed to our entering into the Church from unbelief: for then also we partake of the Master's Body and Blood. Some indeed say that Pentecost is celebrated among the Hebrews for this cause; but others say it was in honor of the fifty days during which Moses fasted and received the God-written Law, together remembering the slaughter of the calf and the other things that took place when Moses went up into the mountain and came down. Others considered Pentecost to have been devised among the Hebrews for the sake of the honor of the number seven, as was said: for that number, multiplied by itself, makes fifty, lacking one day. The honor of the number seven among the Hebrews is not only in days, but also extends unto years, from which years they also observe what among them is called the Jubilee, which means remission. For when the years are septupled, this takes place: when they leave the land unsown, and give rest unto the beasts, and command purchased slaves to go free.[^2356] The third feast, Scenopegia, that is, the Feast of Tabernacles, celebrated after the gathering of the fruits, which is five months after the feast of Passover: this was celebrated in memory of the day on which Moses first pitched the tabernacle seen by him in a cloud on Mount Sinai and prepared by Bezaleel, the chief craftsman; for making booths, they celebrated this feast, dwelling in the fields and thanking God as they gathered the fruits of their labors. Unto this feast David seems also to have inscribed the Psalms "Concerning the Winepresses." And this was an image of our Resurrection from the dead, when, our bodily tabernacles having been dissolved and again reconstructed, we shall receive the fruit of our labors, keeping festival in the eternal tabernacles.[^2357] It should also be known that on this day, as Pentecost was being celebrated, the Holy Spirit came upon the Disciples. The Holy Fathers themselves resolved to divide the feasts, on account of the majesty of the All-Holy and Life-Creating Spirit, forasmuch as He is One of the Holy and Life-Originating Trinity. This also we shall tell tomorrow: how the Holy Spirit came.[^2358]

*Verse:* Through the prayers of the Apostles, O Christ, have mercy on us, amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
Cast into the fiery furnace, the venerable children changed the fire into dew by their exclamation, crying aloud thus: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers.[^2359]

*Troparion:*  
When the Apostles were proclaiming the marvelous things of God, the working of the Spirit was deemed drunkenness by the unbelieving, through Whom the Trinity is made known, the One God of our fathers.[^2360]

*Troparion:*  
In Orthodox manner we theologize the Undivided Nature: God the Unoriginate Father, the Word and the Spirit of the same dominion, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^2361]

*Another Heirmos:*  
The symphonious song of instruments sounded forth to honor the golden, lifeless idol; but the light-bearing grace of the Comforter inspires reverence to cry aloud: O Trinity, One, Equal in power, Unoriginate, blessed art Thou.[^2362]

*Troparion:*  
Not understanding the prophetic utterance, the foolish ones spake of drunkenness caused by wine when they heard the strange speeches of the Apostles: but we, the pious, cry unto Thee divinely: O Benefactor of all, blessed art Thou.[^2363]

*Troparion:*  
The divine Joel, who beheld visions, thundered forth the divine teaching of the Sovereign Godhead, through Whom He said: I will pour out of My Spirit, as of the Word, upon those who cry aloud together: O Nature thrice-radiantly shining, blessed art Thou.[^2364]

*Troparion:*  
The grace indeed divided the threefold beam, so that It might reveal three Hypostases, to be honored in the simplicity of dominion; but now, in the one Lord's day, blessed is the Son, the Father, and the Spirit.[^2365]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The bush that partook of the fire on Sinai without being consumed revealed God unto the slow-tongued and stammering Moses; and the zeal of God showed forth the three children in the fire to be unconquerable singers: All ye works of the Lord, praise the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages.[^2366]

*Troparion:*  
When the violent, life-bearing blast of the All-Holy Spirit from on high was borne upon the fishermen in the form of tongues of fire, they eloquently proclaimed the marvelous things of God: All ye works of the Lord, praise the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages.[^2367]

*Troparion:*  
Ascending the mountain that is not to be touched, not fearing the terrible fire, come, let us stand upon Mount Zion, in the city of the Living God, now joining in choir with the spirit-bearing Disciples: All ye works of the Lord, praise the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages.[^2368]

*Another Heirmos:*  
The three-radiant image of the Godhead looses the bonds and bedews the flame; the children sing, and all created nature blesses the One Savior and Creator of all as the Benefactor.[^2369]

*Troparion:*  
The remembrance of the good, saving words which Christ spake unto the Apostles, having heard them from the Father, the Spirit arranges in the fiery appearance of tongues, establishing that which was blessed and made His own; and alienated creation sings of Thee.[^2370]

*Troparion:*  
Thou Who alone art savingly sovereign, the self-shining Light, and the Giver of light, art come, filling the Apostles, honored as the Crown of Thy servants, and Thou dispensest the bountiful Spirit.[^2371]

*Troparion:*  
The spirit-filled mouths of the Prophets sang of Thy bodily coming, O King, and of the Spirit Who proceeded from the Father's bosom, uncreated, Creator together with Thee, co-enthroned with Thee, an honor unto the faithful of Thine only Incarnation.[^2372]

*We do not sing the "More honorable than the Cherubim," but we sing the Refrain:*  
Magnify, O my soul, the Holy Spirit, Who proceeds from the Father and the Son.[^2373]  
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Thou who without experience of corruption didst bring forth, and didst lend flesh unto the Word, the Fashioner of all, O Mother who knewest not man, O Virgin Theotokos, receptacle of the Uncontainable, dwelling-place of Thine Illimitable Maker: thee we magnify.[^2374]

*Troparion:*  
Borne rejoicing of old upon the burning, fiery chariot, the zealot, inspired by fire, revealed the breath that hath now shone forth from on high upon the Apostles; illuminated by Which, they made known the Trinity unto all.[^2375]

*Troparion:*  
A strange thing was heard of the Disciples contrary to the law of nature: for while one voice was speaking, by the grace of the Spirit, peoples, tribes, and nations differently responded, being instructed in the knowledge of the Trinity by the marvelous works of God.[^2376]

*Another Refrain:*  
Magnify, O my soul, the One Godhead existing in three Persons.[^2377]  
*Another Heirmos:*  
Rejoice, O Queen, Virgin-motherly glory: for every facile and eloquent mouth cannot oratorically hymn thee worthily, and every mind is confounded in comprehending thy Childbearing: therefore with one accord we glorify thee.[^2378]

*Troparion:*  
It was meet to sing of the Maiden naturally endued with life; for she alone concealed within her womb the Word, the healing medicine of diseased human nature, Who, now sitting on the right hand of the Father, sent the grace of the Spirit.[^2379]

*Troparion:*  
Upon as many as the God-flowing grace hath breathed, shining, flashing, transformed by a strange, most majestic transformation, having recognized the equally powerful, indivisible wisdom, we glorify the Thrice-Radiant Essence.[^2380]

*Then the Katavasia: both Heirmoi together.*[^2381]  
*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Having adorned the heavens with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*[^2382]  
All-Holy Spirit, Who proceedest from the Father, and through the Son art come unto the unlettered Disciples: save those who have acknowledged Thee as God, and have mercy upon all. *(Twice)*[^2383]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Another podoben:*  
The Father is Light, the Word is Light, and the Holy Spirit is Light, Who was sent unto the Apostles in fiery tongues, and through Him the whole world is enlightened to honor the Holy Trinity.[^2384]

*At the Praises, Let every breath... We set 6 stichera, and sing the following idiomela, repeating the first 2.*  
*Tone 2:*  
*Sticheron (sung twice):*  
Marvelous things all the nations beheld today in the city of David, when the Holy Spirit descended in fiery tongues, as Luke, the speaker of God, relates; for he says: When the Disciples of Christ were gathered together, there was a sound as of a rushing violent breath, and it filled the house where they were sitting; and they all began to speak with strange words, with strange doctrines, and with strange commands of the Holy Trinity.[^2385]

*Sticheron (sung twice):*  
The Holy Spirit was ever, and is, and shall be: neither beginning nor ceasing, but ever ranked and numbered with the Father and the Son: Life, and Life-Creating; Light, and Giver of light; very Good, and Fountain of goodness; through Whom the Father is made known, and the Son is glorified, and by all is acknowledged: one power, one union, one worship of the Holy Trinity.[^2386]

*Sticheron (sung twice):*  
The Holy Spirit: Light, and Life, and living Noetic Fountain; Spirit of wisdom, Spirit of understanding; Good, Right, Noetic; ruling, cleansing offenses; God, and deifying; Fire proceeding from Fire; speaking, acting, distributing gifts; through Whom all the Prophets and the Divine Apostles, together with the Martyrs, were crowned: strange hearing, strange sight! fire divided for the imparting of gifts.[^2387]

*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. Tone 6:*  
O Heavenly King, O Comforter, Spirit of Truth, Who art everywhere present and fillest all things, Treasury of blessings and Giver of life: come and dwell in us, and cleanse us from every impurity, and save our souls, O Good One.[^2388]

*The Great Doxology. The dismissal troparion of the feast. Then, the litanies.*  
*And the priest makes the dismissal thus:*  
He Who in the appearance of fiery tongues sent down the All-Holy Spirit from heaven upon His holy Disciples and Apostles: Christ our true God, through the intercessions of His all-pure Mother, of the holy, glorious, and all-laudable Apostles, and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, as He is good and the Lover of mankind.[^2389]

*And the First Hour. At the First Hour, the troparion of the feast.*  
*After the Trisagion, the kontakion of the feast. And the final dismissal.*[^2390]

*At the Liturgy, we sing these antiphons:*[^2391]  
*Antiphon 1:*  
*Psalm 18 (Ps. 18 LXX), Tone 2.*  
*Verse 1:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands (Ps. 18:2 LXX).[^2392]  
*Refrain:* Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.  
*Verse 2:* Day unto day utters speech, and night unto night proclaims knowledge (Ps. 18:3 LXX).  
*Refrain:* Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.  
*Verse 3:* Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:5 LXX).  
*Refrain:* Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Refrain:* Through the intercessions of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Antiphon 2:*  
*Psalm 19 (Ps. 19 LXX), the same Tone.*  
*Verse 1:* The Lord hear Thee in the day of affliction; the Name of the God of Jacob defend Thee (Ps. 19:2 LXX).[^2393]  
*Refrain:* Save us, O Good Comforter, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia.  
*Verse 2:* May He send Thee help from the sanctuary, and strengthen Thee out of Zion (Ps. 19:3 LXX).  
*Refrain:* Save us, O Good Comforter, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia.  
*Verse 3:* The Lord grant Thee according to Thy heart, and fulfill all Thy counsel (Ps. 19:5 LXX).  
*Refrain:* Save us, O Good Comforter, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia.  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Only-Begotten Son...*[^2394]

*Antiphon 3:*  
*Psalm 20 (Ps. 20 LXX), Tone 8.*  
*Verse 1:* O Lord, in Thy strength the king shall be glad, and in Thy salvation he shall rejoice exceedingly (Ps. 20:2 LXX).[^2395]  
*Troparion, Tone 8:*  
Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast revealed the fishermen to be all-wise, having sent down upon them the Holy Spirit, and through them caught the universe in a net: O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee.[^2396]  
*Verse 2:* Thou hast given him the desire of his heart, and hast not withheld the request of his lips (Ps. 20:3 LXX).  
*Troparion:* Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...  
*Verse 3:* For Thou hast anticipated him with blessings of goodness; Thou hast set upon his head a crown of precious stone (Ps. 20:4 LXX).  
*Troparion:* Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...

*Entrance Hymn:*  
Be Thou exalted, O Lord, in Thy strength; we will hymn and praise Thy mighty acts (Ps. 20:14 LXX).[^2397]  
*Then the Troparion:* Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...  
*Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*  
*Kontakion, Tone 8:*  
When the Most High came down and confused the tongues, He divided the nations; but when He distributed the tongues of fire, He called all into unity: and with one accord we glorify the All-Holy Spirit.[^2398]

*Instead of the Trisagion:*  
As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ: Alleluia (Gal. 3:27).[^2399]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*  
Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world (Ps. 18:5 LXX).[^2400]  
*And until the Apodosis.*  
*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands (Ps. 18:2 LXX).  
*The Epistle: In the Acts of the Apostles, Pericope 3 (Acts 2:1-11):* When the days of Pentecost were being fulfilled...[^2401]  
*Alleluia, Tone 1:* By the Word of the Lord were the heavens established, and all the power of them by the Spirit of His mouth (Ps. 32:6 LXX).[^2402]  
*Verse:* The Lord looked down from heaven; He beheld all the sons of men (Ps. 32:13 LXX).  
*The Gospel according to John, Pericope 27 (John 7:37-52; 8:12):* On the last great day of the feast...[^2403]  
*Instead of "It is truly meet," we sing until the Apodosis the Heirmos:* Rejoice, O Queen...[^2404]  
*Communion Verse:* Thy good Spirit shall guide me in the land of uprightness (Ps. 142:10 LXX): Alleluia, thrice.[^2405]  
*And after the dismissal at the Liturgy, the 9th Hour is said according to custom.*[^2406]

## ON THE SUNDAY OF HOLY PENTECOST, AT VESPERS[^2407]

*A fast bell is rung on account of the sequence of the kneeling.*[^2408]  
*The priest begins:* Blessed is our God...  
*And the appointed reader sings:* O Heavenly King... *and the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX).*[^2409]  
*Then the deacon, if there be one, or if not, the priest says the Great Litany:*[^2410]  
In peace let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For the peace from above, and the salvation of our souls, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For the peace of the whole world, the good estate of the holy churches of God, and the union of all, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For this holy temple, and for those who enter into it with faith, reverence, and the fear of God, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For our Most Reverend Archbishop, Metropolitan [Name], for our God-loving Bishop [Name], for the honorable presbytery, the diaconate in Christ, for all the clergy and the people, let us pray to the Lord.[^2411]  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For our Most Devout Emperor [Name], for all his Court and Army, let us pray to the Lord.[^2412]  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.

That He will subdue and put under his feet every enemy and adversary, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For this city (or: this village), or: this holy monastery, [and for every monastery], and for every city, village, and country, and for the faithful who dwell therein, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For seasonable weather, abundance of the fruits of the earth, and peaceful times, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For travelers by water and land, the sick, the suffering, those in captivity, and for their salvation, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For the people standing here and awaiting the grace of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.[^2413]  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For those who have bowed their hearts and their knees before the Lord, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For the sanctification of the souls and bodies of all of us who have righteously with faith and piety gathered together into this most honorable temple, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That we may be accounted worthy of the imperishable Kingdom by the grace of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That we may be a temple and dwelling-place of the Holy and Life-Creating Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That we may preserve our faith and unashamed love by the grace of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That we may find grace and mercy of the All-Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For the revelation, so that we may be partakers of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That we may receive the remission of sins and the knowledge of spiritual grace, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That we may not be alien from the Holy Spirit at the dread and righteous judgment-seat of Christ, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For the granting of rich mercy and compassions and the grace of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That the Holy Spirit may loose us from every bond of the adversary, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For the salvation from on high of us who have bowed our hearts' and bodies' knees in faith on this fiftieth day, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That the Lord our God will hear us sinners, and send down upon us the Holy Spirit, and sanctify us, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That we may ever worship in soul and body, and hymn the Holy Consubstantial Trinity in Unity, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That the Lord God will be well pleased toward us who have bowed our knees, and send forth upon us His Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That our kneeling and our prayers before Him may be acceptable, as the incense of a spiritual fragrance, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
That we may be communicants of the radiance of the Divine, True, and Life-Creating Trinity, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
For our deliverance from all affliction, wrath, and necessity, let us pray to the Lord.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.  
*Choir:* Lord, have mercy.  
Commemorating our most holy, most pure, most blessed and glorious Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and all our life unto Christ our God.[^2414]  
*Choir:* To Thee, O Lord.

*Then the priest exclaims:*[^2415]  
**Priest:** For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.  
**Choir:** Amen.  
*Then we sing "Lord, I have cried," and we set 6 verses.*[^2416]  
*And we sing idiomelic stichera. Tone 4:*[^2417]  
Glorious things all the nations beheld today in the city of David, when the Holy Spirit came down in fiery tongues, even as Luke the proclaimer of God recounts; for he saith: When the disciples of Christ were gathered together, there came a sound as of a rushing violent wind, and filled the house where they were sitting; and they all began to speak with strange words, strange doctrines, strange ordinances of the Holy Trinity.[^2418]  
The Holy Spirit was ever, and is, and shall be...[^2419]  
The Holy Spirit, Light and Life...[^2420]  
*Repeating them.*[^2421]  
*Glory, both now:*  
*Tone 6:*  
O Heavenly King, Comforter, Spirit of truth, Who art everywhere present and fillest all things, Treasury of good gifts and Giver of life: come and abide in us, and cleanse us from every stain, and save our souls, O Good One.[^2422]  
*Written above at the Praises.*[^2423]  
*Entrance with the censer.*[^2424]  
*O Gladsome Light.*[^2425]  
*Great Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*[^2426]  
Who is a god great as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders (Ps. 76:14 LXX).[^2427]  
*Verse 1:* Thou hast made Thy power known among the peoples (Ps. 76:15 LXX).[^2428]  
*Verse 2:* And I said: Now have I begun; this is the change of the right hand of the Most High (Ps. 76:11 LXX).[^2429]  
*Verse 3:* I remembered the works of the Lord; for I will remember Thy wonders from the beginning (Ps. 76:12 LXX).[^2430]  
*And again:* Who is a god great as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders.  

## FIRST KNEELING PRAYER STATION
*Then the priest or deacon exclaims:*[^2431]  
**Deacon:** Again and again, having bowed our knees, let us pray to the Lord.[^2432]  
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy *(thrice)*.  
*And as we bow our knees upon the earth and are uncovered, the priest reads the prayers at the altar aloud facing the people.*[^2433]

O Pure, undefiled, unoriginate, invisible, incomprehensible, unsearchable, immutable, unconquerable, innumerable, forbearing Lord,[^2434] Who alone hast immortality, dwelling in unapproachable light (1 Tim. 6:16);[^2435] Who madest the heaven and the earth, and the sea, and all things that are therein (Acts 4:24);[^2436] Who, before all ask, dost grant their requests; unto Thee do we pray and of Thee do we beseech, O Master, Lover of mankind, the Father of our Lord and God and Savior Jesus Christ,[^2437] Who for us men and for our salvation came down from heaven, and was incarnate of the Holy Spirit and Mary the Ever-Virgin and all-glorious Theotokos;[^2438] Who first indeed teaching by words, and afterward also showing by deeds, when He endured the saving Passion, gave unto us, Thy humble, sinful, and unworthy servants, an example to offer supplications unto Thee with bending of the neck and knees, for our own sins and for the ignorance of the people.[^2439] Do Thou Thyself, O Plenteous-in-mercy and Lover of mankind, give ear unto us in whatsoever day we call upon Thee, but especially on this fiftieth day, wherein, after the Ascension of our Lord Jesus Christ into the heavens and His sitting at the right hand of Thee, the God and Father, He sent down the Holy Spirit upon His holy disciples and Apostles, Who also sat upon each one of them, and they were all filled with His inexhaustible grace, and spake with other tongues Thy magnificent works, and prophesied.[^2440] Now therefore, give ear unto us who pray unto Thee, and remember us, humble and condemned, and turn back the captivity of our souls, having Thine own mercy interceding for us: receive us who fall down before Thee and cry: We have sinned; unto Thee were we cast from the womb, from the belly of our mother Thou art our God (Ps. 21:11 LXX).[^2441] But since our days have vanished away in vanity (Ps. 77:33 LXX), we are stripped of Thy help, we are deprived of all defense: but taking courage in Thy compassions, we cry: Remember not the sins of our youth and our ignorance (Ps. 24:7 LXX), and cleanse us from our secret faults (Ps. 18:13 LXX);[^2442] and cast us not off in the time of old age, when our strength fails: forsake us not (Ps. 70:9 LXX);[^2443] before we return unto the earth, account us worthy to turn unto Thee, and attend unto us with favor and grace; measure our iniquities against Thy compassions; set the abyss of Thy compassions against the multitude of our sins; look down from Thy holy height, O Lord,[^2444] upon Thy people standing here and awaiting rich mercy from Thee. Visit us with Thy goodness; deliver us from the oppression of the devil; establish our life by Thy holy and sacred laws. Appoint an angel, a faithful guardian, unto Thy people; gather us all into Thy Kingdom; grant forgiveness unto them that hope in Thee; remit unto them and unto us our sins; cleanse us by the operation of Thy Holy Spirit; destroy the wiles of the enemy that are against us.[^2445]
*He adds also this prayer:*[^2446]  
Blessed art Thou, O Lord, Master, Almighty, Who hast illumined the day with the light of the sun, and brightened the night with rays of fire; Who hast accounted us worthy to pass through the length of the day and to draw near to the beginnings of the night: give ear unto our supplications and those of all Thy people, and, having forgiven us all our voluntary and involuntary sins, receive our evening supplications, and send down the multitude of Thy mercy and Thy compassions upon Thine inheritance.[^2447] Shield us with Thy holy angels; arm us with the armor of Thy righteousness; encompass us with Thy truth; keep us by Thy power; deliver us from every adversity, from every snare of the adversary. And grant unto us this present evening with the coming night, perfect, holy, peaceful, sinless, free from offenses, free from illusions, and all the days of our life: by the prayers of the holy Theotokos, and of all the saints who from the ages have been well-pleasing unto Thee.[^2448]  
*Then the deacon says:*[^2449]  
**Deacon:** Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.[^2450]  
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy *(once)*.  
**Deacon:** Commemorating our most holy, most pure, most blessed and glorious Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and all our life unto Christ our God.[^2451]  
*Then the priest, exclamation:*[^2452]  
**Priest:** For Thine it is to show mercy and to save us, O Lord our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.[^2453]  
**Choir:** Amen.  

*After this, the priest or the deacon says the Litany:*[^2454]  
**Deacon:** Let us all say with our whole soul and with our whole mind, let us say:[^2455]  
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.  
**Deacon:** And the rest.[^2456]  
*Then the priest, exclamation:*[^2457]  
**Priest:** For a merciful God art Thou, and the Lover of mankind, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.[^2458]  
**Choir:** Amen.  

## SECOND KNEELING PRAYER STATION
*Then the deacon says:*[^2459]  
**Deacon:** Again and again, having bowed our knees, let us pray to the Lord.[^2460]  
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy *(thrice)*.  
*And we bow our knees according to what was previously prescribed.*[^2461]  
*The priest reads the second prayer in the hearing of all:*[^2462]  
O Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who didst grant Thy peace unto men, and the gift of the All-Holy Spirit, while yet in this life and being with us, dost ever grant unto the faithful as an inalienable inheritance; and more visibly didst send down this grace today upon Thy disciples and Apostles, and didst strengthen their lips with fiery tongues, whereby the whole human race, receiving the knowledge of God in our own tongue unto the hearing of the ear, hath been enlightened with the light of the Spirit, and delivered from delusion as from darkness, and through the distribution of sensible and fiery tongues and by supernatural operation were instructed in the faith in Thee, and illuminated to glorify Thee as God together with the Father and the Holy Spirit in one Godhead and power and authority.[^2463] Do Thou Thyself, therefore, O Radiance of the Father, the immutable and immovable imprint of His substance and nature, O Fountain of wisdom and grace: open also the lips of me, a sinner, and teach me how it is meet and for what things it is needful to pray; for Thou art He that knows the great multitude of my sins, but Thy loving-kindness will overcome their immeasurableness; for lo, with fear I stand before Thee, casting the despair of my soul into the deep of Thy mercy: steer my life, O Thou Who rulest all creation by Thy word with the ineffable power of wisdom, O quiet Haven of the tempest-tossed: and make known unto me the way[^2464] wherein I should walk (Ps. 142:8 LXX). Grant the Spirit of Thy wisdom unto my thoughts; bestow the Spirit of understanding upon my foolishness; with the Spirit of Thy fear overshadow my deeds; and a right Spirit renew Thou in my inward parts (Ps. 50:12 LXX); and with the Sovereign Spirit establish the instability of my thoughts: that day by day, being guided by Thy good Spirit unto what is profitable, I may be accounted worthy to fulfill Thy commandments, and ever to remember Thy glorious Coming and the scrutiny of what hath been done by us.[^2465] And suffer me not to be deceived by the corruptible beauties of this world, but strengthen me to desire the enjoyment of future treasures; for Thou hast said, O Master: Whatsoever anyone shall ask in Thy Name, he receives freely from Thy co-eternal God and Father; therefore I also, a sinner, in the coming of Thy Holy Spirit, entreat Thy goodness: whatsoever I have asked, render unto me for salvation.[^2466] Yea, O Lord, rich Giver of every good benefaction, and good Bestower, for Thou art He that gives superabundantly what we ask: Thou art compassionate and merciful, Who without sin becamest a Partaker of our flesh, and unto them that bend their knees unto Thee dost bow down in tender compassion, having become the cleansing of our sins.[^2467] Grant therefore, O Lord, Thy compassions unto Thy people: give ear unto us from the heaven of Thy sanctuary; shelter us with the power of Thy saving right hand; cover us under the shelter of Thy wings; despise not the works of Thy hands.[^2468] Unto Thee alone do we sin, yet Thee alone do we serve; we know not how to worship a strange god, nor to stretch out our hands, O Master, unto any other god (Ps. 43:21 LXX). Remit unto us our transgressions, and, having received our kneeling supplications, stretch forth unto us all a helping hand; receive the prayer of all as acceptable incense, taken up before Thy most good Kingdom.[^2469]
*He adds also this prayer:*[^2470]  
O Lord, O Lord, Who deliverest us from every arrow that flies by day, deliver us also from every thing that walks in darkness (Ps. 90:5-6 LXX). Receive the lifting up of our hands as an evening sacrifice (Ps. 140:2 LXX). Vouchsafe us also to pass through the course of the night blamelessly, untempted by evil things; and deliver us from every disturbance and fear that comes unto us from the devil. Grant compunction unto our souls, and unto our thoughts care concerning the examination at Thy dread and righteous Judgment[^2471] seat. Nail our flesh to Thy fear (Ps. 118:120 LXX), and mortify our members which are upon the earth (Col. 3:5), that even in the quiet of sleep we may be illuminated by the contemplation of Thy judgments. Take away from us every unseemly fantasy and harmful concupiscence. And raise us up at the time of prayer, established in the faith and progressing in Thy commandments.[^2472]
*Then the deacon says:*[^2473]  
**Deacon:** Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.  
Commemorating our most holy, most pure, most blessed...[^2474]  
*Priest, exclamation to the prayer:*[^2475]  
**Priest:** Through the goodwill and grace of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine All-Holy, Good, and Life-Creating Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.  
**Choir:** Amen.[^2476]  
*Then: "Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening..."*[^2477]  

## THIRD KNEELING PRAYER STATION
*After this, the deacon says:*[^2478]  
**Deacon:** Again and again, having bowed our knees, let us pray to the Lord.  
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy *(thrice)*.  
*And as we likewise bow our knees,*[^2479]  
*The priest reads the third prayer:*[^2480]  
O Ever-flowing, living, and illuminating Fountain, creative Power co-eternal with the Father, Who hast most excellently fulfilled the whole dispensation for the salvation of mankind, O Christ our God: Who didst tear asunder the indissoluble bonds of death and the bolts of Hades, and didst trample down the multitude of evil spirits; Who offeredst Thyself as a blameless sacrifice for us, giving Thy most pure Body as an offering, untouchable and impervious to any sin, and through this dread and unutterable sacred action didst bestow eternal life upon us; Who descendedst into Hades and didst shatter the eternal bars, and didst show the ascent unto them that were sitting in darkness; and having ensnared the author of evil, the deep serpent, with God-wise lure, and bound him with chains of gloom in Tartarus and in the unquenchable fire, and in outer darkness, by Thine immeasurably powerful[^2481] might didst shut him fast: O renowned Wisdom of the Father, Who didst appear as a great Helper unto the afflicted, and didst enlighten them that sit in darkness and in the shadow of death (Luke 1:79). Thou, O Lord of everlasting glory, and beloved Son of the Most High Father, everlasting Light of everlasting Light, Sun of Righteousness: give ear unto us who pray unto Thee, and give rest unto the souls of Thy servants, our fathers and brethren who have previously fallen asleep, and our other kinsmen according to the flesh, and all of the household of faith, of whom we now make commemoration; for in Thee is the dominion of all, and in Thy hand Thou holdest all the ends of the earth.[^2482] O Master Almighty, God of our fathers, and Lord of mercy, Creator of the mortal and immortal race and of all human nature, which is compounded and again dissolved, of life and of decease, of dwelling here and of departure thither: Who measurest the years for the living, and appointest the times of death; Who bringest down unto Hades and bringest up; Who bindest in infirmity and loosest in power; Who orderest the present as is needful, and directest what is to come unto profit; Who cheerest with the hopes of resurrection them that are wounded by the sting of death:[^2483] Do Thou Thyself, therefore, O Master of all, God our Savior, the Hope of all the ends of the earth and of them that are afar off upon the sea (Ps. 64:6 LXX); Who also on this last and great saving day of the feast of Pentecost didst reveal unto us the mystery of the Holy, Consubstantial, Co-eternal, Undivided, and Unconfused Trinity, and didst pour out the descent and coming of Thy Holy and Life-Creating Spirit in the form of fiery tongues upon Thy holy Apostles, and didst establish them as heralds of our pious faith, and didst show them forth as confessors and preachers of true theology;[^2484] Who also on this all-perfect and saving feast hast vouchsafed to accept prayerful propitiations for them that are held bound in Hades, and grantest unto us great hopes of alleviation for them that are gripped by the griefs gripping them, and that comfort may be sent down by Thee: Give ear unto us, Thy humble servants who pray unto Thee, and give rest unto the souls of Thy servants who have previously fallen asleep, in a place of light, in a place of green pasture, in a place of refreshment, whence all pain, sorrow, and sighing have fled away; and assign their spirits to dwell in the habitations of the righteous, and account them worthy of peace and relief: for not the dead[^2485] shall praise Thee, O Lord, nor do they that are in Hades dare to offer confession unto Thee (Ps. 113:25 LXX): but we, the living, bless Thee and pray, and offer unto Thee propitiatory prayers and sacrifices for their souls.[^2486]
*He adds also this prayer:*[^2487]  
O God, great and eternal, holy, Lover of mankind, Who hast accounted us worthy to stand at this hour before Thine unapproachable glory, for the singing and praise of Thy wonders: cleanse us, Thine unworthy servants, and grant grace, that with a contrite heart and without presumption we may offer unto Thee the thrice-holy doxology and thanksgiving for Thy great gifts, which Thou hast done toward us and dost ever do in us.[^2488] Remember, O Lord, our weakness, and destroy us not with our iniquities: but show great mercy with our humility, that having escaped the darkness of sin, we may walk in the day of righteousness, and having put on the armor of light (Rom. 13:12), we may abide unharmed by every wile of the evil one, and with boldness may glorify in all things Thee, the only true God and Lover of mankind.[^2489] For Thine, O Master of all and Creator, is truly both a great and magnificent mystery: the temporary dissolution of Thy creatures, and after this their re-uniting and eternal resting. We acknowledge Thy grace concerning all things: concerning our entries into this world, and our exits, which by Thine unfailing promise betroth unto us the hopes of Resurrection and of incorruptible life, which we shall receive in Thy future Second Coming; for Thou art both the Prince of our Resurrection, and the unbribable and man-loving Judge of those who have lived, and the Master and Lord of retribution, Who didst also genuinely partake with us in flesh and blood (Heb. 2:14), because of utmost condescension; and our blameless passions, in willingly submitting Thyself to trial, Thou didst accept through the tender mercy of Thy compassions; and wherein Thou Thyself hast suffered, being tempted, Thou hast become a self-pledged Helper unto us who are tempted (Heb. 2:18): therefore Thou hast also raised us up together into Thine impassibility.[^2490] Receive therefore, O Master, our supplications and prayers, and give rest unto all each one's fathers, and mothers, and children, and brethren, and sisters of one race and of kindred race, and all souls that have previously fallen asleep in the hope of the resurrection unto life eternal; assign their spirits and their names in the book of life, in[^2491] the bosoms of Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, in the land of the living, in the Kingdom of the heavens, in the paradise of delight, through Thy radiant angels bringing all into Thy holy abodes; raising up also our bodies in the day which Thou hast appointed, according to Thy holy and true promises.[^2492] There is therefore, O Lord, no death unto Thy servants when we depart from the body and come unto Thee our God, but a passing from things more sorrowful unto things more profitable and more sweet, and unto rest and joy. And if we have sinned in anything against Thee, be merciful unto us and unto them: for none is pure from stains before Thee, not even if his life be but one day (Job 14:4-5 LXX): save only Thou alone, Who didst appear sinless upon earth, our Lord Jesus Christ, through Whom we all hope to obtain mercy and remission of sins.[^2493] Therefore unto us and unto them, as a good God and Lover of mankind, remit, forgive, and pardon our offenses, voluntary and involuntary, known and unknown, manifest and forgotten: whether in deed, or in thought, or in word; whether in all our conversations and movements. And unto them that have departed grant freedom and release, and bless us that are here: granting a good and peaceful end unto us and unto all Thy people, and opening unto us the bowels of mercy and love for mankind at Thy dread and fearsome Coming, and make us worthy of Thy Kingdom.[^2494]
*He adds also this prayer:*[^2495]  
O God great and most high, Who alone hast immortality, dwelling in light unapproachable (1 Tim. 6:16), Who createdst all creation in wisdom, Who dividedst between the light and between the darkness (Gen. 1:4), and didst appoint the sun for the ruling of the day, and the moon and stars for the ruling of the night (Ps. 135:8-9 LXX); Who hast accounted us sinners worthy also in this present day to anticipate Thy presence with thanksgiving, and to offer unto Thee evening worship: Do Thou Thyself, O Lord, Lover of mankind, direct our prayer as incense before Thee, and receive it for a savor of sweet-smelling fragrance (Ps. 140:2 LXX). Grant unto us this present evening and the coming night peaceful; clothe us with the armor of light (Rom. 13:12); deliver us from the fear of the night, and from the thing that walks in darkness (Ps. 90:5-6 LXX). And grant unto us sleep, which Thou hast bestowed for the rest of our weakness, free from every[^2496] diabolical fantasy. Yea, O Master of all, Bestower of good things, that even upon our beds being moved with compunction, we may remember in the night Thy most holy Name. And being illuminated by meditation on Thy commandments, in joy of soul may we rise up unto the doxology of Thy goodness, offering supplications and prayers unto Thy tender compassion for our sins and for all Thy people: whom, by the prayers of the Most Holy Theotokos, visit in mercy.[^2497]
*Then the deacon says:*[^2498]  
**Deacon:** Help us, save us, have mercy on us, raise us up, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.  
Commemorating our most holy, most pure, most blessed and glorious Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and all our life unto Christ our God.[^2499]  
*The priest exclaims:*[^2500]  
**Priest:** For Thou art the resting of our souls and bodies, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.  
**Choir:** Amen.  
*Then the deacon says the Litany:*[^2501]  
**Deacon:** Let us complete our evening prayer [unto the Lord]:[^2502]  
Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.  
That the whole evening may be perfect... An angel of peace... Pardon and remission... Things good and profitable... The remaining time of our life... A Christian ending...[^2503]  
Commemorating our most holy, most pure, most blessed and glorious Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and all our life unto Christ our God.[^2504]  
*The priest exclaims:*[^2505]  
**Priest:** For a good God art Thou, and the Lover of mankind, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.[^2506]  
**Choir:** Amen.  
**Priest:** Peace be unto all.[^2507]  
**Choir:** And to thy spirit.  
**Deacon:** Let us bow our heads unto the Lord.[^2508]

*The priest secretly reads the customary prayer:*[^2509]  
O Lord our God, Who didst bow the heavens and come down for the salvation of the human race, look upon Thy servants and upon Thine inheritance; for unto Thee, the fearsome Judge and Lover of mankind, Thy servants have bowed their heads and bent their necks, not expecting help from men, but hoping in Thy mercy and awaiting Thy salvation; whom do Thou preserve at all times, and during this present evening and the approaching night, from every enemy, from every adverse working of the devil, from vain thoughts, and evil recollections.[^2510]  
*Then he exclaims:*  
**Priest:** Blessed and glorified be the majesty of Thy Kingdom, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, both now and for ever and unto ages of ages.[^2511]  
**Choir:** Amen.  
*And after this, both choirs having come together, we sing:*  
*At the Aposticha, idiomelic stichera, Tone 3:*[^2512]  
Now unto all for a sign the tongues have clearly become; for the Jews, of whom is Christ according to the flesh, being diseased with unbelief, have fallen away from the grace of God; and of the divine light we who are of the Gentiles have been accounted worthy, being established by the words of the Disciples, who proclaim the glory of God, the Benefactor of all; with whom having bowed our hearts together with our knees, in faith let us worship the Holy Spirit, being established by the Savior of our souls.[^2513]  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*[^2514]  
Now the Comforting Spirit has been poured out upon all flesh; for having begun from the choir of the Apostles, from them He extended grace by participation unto the faithful, and confirms His sovereign descent, in fiery appearance distributing tongues unto the Disciples for hymnody and the glory of God; therefore, being noetically enlightened in our hearts, established in the faith by the Holy Spirit, we pray that our souls may be saved.[^2515]  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*[^2516]  
Now the Apostles are clothed with power from on high with Christ's power; for the Comforter renews them, being Himself renewed in them by a mystical renewal of the mind, that in strange and high-sounding voices they may preach the co-eternal Nature, both simple and three-hypostatic, to revere God, the Benefactor of all; therefore, having been enlightened by their teachings, let us worship the Father with the Son and the Spirit, praying that our souls may be saved.[^2517]  
*Glory, both now, Tone 8:*  
Come, O ye peoples, let us worship the Godhead in three Hypostases, the Son in the Father, with the Holy Spirit; for the Father timelessly begot the Son, co-eternal and co-enthroned, and the Holy Spirit was in the Father, glorified with the Son: one Power, one Essence, one Godhead; Whom all of us worshiping, do say: Holy God, Who didst make all things through the Son, with the cooperation of the Holy Spirit; Holy Mighty, through Whom we knew the Father, and the Holy Spirit came into the world; Holy Immortal, O Comforting Spirit, Who proceedest from the Father and restest in the Son: O Holy Trinity, glory to Thee.[^2518]  
*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart. Trisagion. O Most Holy Trinity. Our Father. Troparion, Tone 8:*[^2519]  
Daring cherubically to glorify the Father unoriginate, the Son co-unoriginate, the Spirit co-eternal, one Godhead, we say: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, our God, praised in the Trinity, have mercy on us.[^2520]

*Glory, both now, Tone 8:*  
Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast revealed the fishermen most wise, having sent down upon them the Holy Spirit, and through them caught the universe: O Lover of mankind, glory to Thee.[^2521]  
*Then the priest makes the dismissal thus:*[^2522]  
**Priest:** May He Who emptied Himself from the Father's and divine bosoms, and came down from heaven upon the earth, and took upon Himself all our nature, and deified it; and after these things ascended again into the heavens, and sat at the right hand of God and the Father; and sent down the divine and Holy, consubstantial, equal in power, co-glorious, and co-eternal Spirit upon His holy Disciples and Apostles, and thereby enlightened them, and through them the whole universe: Christ, our true God, through the intercessions of His most pure and all-blameless holy Mother; of the holy, glorious, all-praised God-heralds and spirit-bearing Apostles; and of all the saints, have mercy on us and save us, as He is good and the Lover of mankind.

## ON THE SUNDAY OF HOLY PENTECOST, AT COMPLINE[^2523]

*We sing the Canon to the Most Holy Spirit, the composition of Theophanes. Heirmoi twice, troparia to 6, whereof the acrostic is: "I sing the Spirit Who created all creation."*[^2524]  
*Ode 1, Tone 1. Heirmos:*  
Israel, having been delivered from bitter slavery, passed through the impassable as dry land, beholding the enemy drowned, and sings a song unto God as Benefactor, Who works wonders with an exalted arm, for He is glorified.[^2525]  
*Refrain: Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.*[^2526]  
*Troparion:*  
O divine Holy Spirit, Who distributest gifts unto all, and dost all things by Thy will: breathe into me Thy light-bearing gift, that I may glorify Thee as united with the Father and the Son.[^2527]  
*Troparion:*  
O Comforter, Who givest the grace of Thy holy inbreathing unto the noetic powers: having cleansed the defilement of my mind as Thou art good, show it filled with Thy holiness.[^2528]  
*Glory:*  
O Believed Fountain of life, and stream of natural goodness, O divine Holy Spirit: having quickened my deadened mind by Thy divine working, raise it up to hymn Thy Godhead.[^2529]

*Both now, Theotokion:*  
As thou wast the temple of God, by the descent of the Spirit, by the effective power upon thee of Him Who grants generative power, O Full of grace, through which thou didst bear in the flesh the Word unoriginate.[^2530]  
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Unto the Son begotten of the Father incorruptibly before the ages, and in these latter days incarnate of the Virgin without seed, unto Christ God let us cry aloud: Thou Who hast exalted our horn, holy art Thou, O Lord.[^2531]  
*Troparion:*  
Having by nature a holy power of will, working as God among the supramundane noetic beings, the Holy Spirit teaches unceasingly to cry: Holy art Thou, O Lord.[^2532]  
*Troparion:*  
With unceasing songs we harmoniously glorify the grace of the Spirit that shone forth upon the God-speaking Apostles in the violent wind, crying out with the bodiless choirs: Holy art Thou, O Lord.[^2533]  
*Glory:*  
Wise in mind, we hymn one Authority, and one Godhead, and Power, one Origin and Kingdom of the Holy Trinity, crying in a threefold voice: Holy art Thou, O Lord.[^2534]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
A light-bearing chariot and radiant dwelling-place, O Most Pure, thou wast higher than the Cherubim, having borne God upon thine arms; therefore we all cry unto thee, O Pure One: Rejoice, O All-Blessed.[^2535]  
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
A rod from the root of Jesse, and a flower from it, O Christ, Thou didst sprout from the Virgin; from the mountain overshadowed, praised, and dense with woods, Thou hast come, incarnate of her that knew not man, O Immaterial One and God: glory to Thy power, O Lord.[^2536]  
*Troparion:*  
Bestowing great things upon us, the All-Holy Spirit came down upon the Apostles as divine, as good, as filling all things, as deifying, as sanctifying, as the Creator of all, sovereign, and self-governing.[^2537]  
*Troparion:*  
Having sat upon the Father's throne, O Christ, Thou didst send down the Comforter unto Thy Disciples, as Thou didst promise, O Savior, as God coming, sending Him not as opposed, but as Creator of all, proceeding from the Father.[^2538]  
*Glory:*  
The All-Holy Spirit taught the tongue of the Prophets of old to speak of things to come, and now co-abiding substantially in the sound of a more violent wind, He proclaims the majesty of God through the tongues of the all-wise Apostles.[^2539]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The gate of noetic light we name thee, O Mother of God, through which Christ entered unto us, and appeared fair with the lightning-flashes of the Godhead, covered in a bodily garment, invisible as God, but now seen according to our likeness.[^2540]  
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Being the God of peace, the Father of mercies, Thou hast sent unto us the Angel of Thy great counsel, granting peace; therefore, having been guided unto the light of the knowledge of God, watching early from the night, we glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^2541]  
*Troparion:*
