O Spirit of wisdom and fear of God, of truth, counsel, and understanding, Who grantest peace, dwell within us, that being cleansed by Thy dwelling, watching early from the night, we may glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^2542]

*Troparion:*  
O Thou Who upholdest all things, and art Lord of all, preserve without falling those who honor Thee; grant holiness and enlightenment, that being filled with Thy light-giving, watching early from the night, we may glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^2543]  
*Glory:*  
He Who inscribed the Law of old for Moses, sets forth clearly the commandments of the New Covenant and the law of grace, having written them upon the hearts of the Apostles, the Divine Comforter having come upon them, as He is the Lover of mankind.[^2544]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
By thy childbearing, O Virgin, thou hast abolished the curse of Eve, the mother of all, having shone forth blessing unto the world, Christ; therefore rejoicing, with lips and mind confessing thee to be truly the Mother of God, we bless thee.[^2545]  
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
From the belly the sea monster vomited forth Jonah as an infant, just as it received him; and the Word, having dwelt in the Virgin and taken flesh, came forth, preserving her incorrupt; for of that which He suffered no corruption, He preserved her that bore Him uninjured.[^2546]  
*Troparion:*  
Fulfilling the promise unto Thy Disciples, O Christ, Thou didst send them the Spirit, bestowing the working of great wonders, and granting fire-like tongues, that they may fill the flocks of the Gentiles with Thy knowledge.[^2547]  
*Troparion:*  
Come unto us, O Holy Spirit, making Thy partakers of holiness, of unwaning light, of divine life, and of most sweet-smelling distribution; for Thou art the River of the Godhead, proceeding from the Father through the Son.[^2548]  
*Glory:*  
Save, O Comforter, those who faithfully hymn Thy God-befitting coming, and cleanse from every defilement, as Thou art compassionate; and show them worthy of Thine overshadowing, and by Thy most God-beholding light make them undefiled mirrors.[^2549]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The whole choir of the prophets, mystically taught by God, foretold the mystery of the ineffable and divine Incarnation of God the Word from thee, O Virgin Mother; for thou didst make manifest the most true and ancient counsel.[^2550]  
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва):*  
The All-Holy Spirit, having now descended upon the Apostles in fiery form, filled the gatherings of the nations with awe; for as they spoke with fiery tongues, O Lover of mankind, each heard his own language; therefore the wonder was known unto the unbelieving as drunkenness, but unto the faithful truly as saving; for this cause we glorify Thy dominion, O Christ God, asking that remission of offenses be richly sent down upon Thy servants.[^2551]  
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Children brought up together in piety, disregarding the ungodly command, were not afraid of the threat of fire, but standing in the midst of the flame, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of the fathers.[^2552]

*Troparion:*  
Now the proposed promise of Christ is fulfilled; for the coming of the Spirit unto the Disciples showed the division of tongues, having shone forth from the Trinity, the One Pre-divine.[^2553]  
*Troparion:*  
Of old, indeed, the senseless accord of languages was divided, but now they have clearly gathered into one communion, under the self-working, all-venerable, and Divine Spirit, One of the pre-divine Trinity.[^2554]  
*Glory:*  
At the wind of the Holy Spirit borne from on high, the Apostles of Christ gloriously proclaimed the majesty of God, singing in harmony: Blessed art Thou, O God of the fathers.[^2555]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The three Children in the furnace showed forth the image of thy childbirth; for they remained unharmed by the fire: for thou wast preserved, O Pure One, having received into thy womb the unendurable Fire, the blessed God of our fathers.[^2556]  
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The dew-shedding furnace depicted the image of a preternatural wonder; for it burned not the youths whom it received, just as the Fire of the Godhead burned not the womb of the Virgin into which It entered; therefore singing let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever.[^2557]  
*Troparion:*  
O Holy Spirit Who proceedest from God, grant light unto all that believe in Thee; for Thou art holy, and the Bestower of holiness unto men; therefore singing let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever.[^2558]  
*Troparion:*  
As Benefactor, O Giver, Thou grantest the gift of goodness unto those who hymn Thee, O Comforter; for Thou art the Giver of good things and the abyss of goodness; therefore singing let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever.[^2559]  
*Glory:*  
The Lord and Life-Creator is the Spirit, self-moved, self-governing, dividing the distributions of gifts as He wills, co-eternal, self-commanding, unoriginate; therefore singing let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever.[^2560]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Who marvels not at the multitude of Thy tender compassions, O Unoriginate Word? For for our sakes Thou didst become poor, being rich, and didst dwell in the womb of the holy Virgin; therefore singing let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all for ever.[^2561]  
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Rejoice, virgin glory; rejoice, most pure Mother, whom all creation magnifies with divine songs.[^2562]  
*Troparion:*  
Lo, Thou hast sent down unto us another Comforter, consubstantial with Thee, O Word, and co-enthroned with Thy Father.[^2563]  
*Troparion:*  
Save from temptation, O Comforter, those who acknowledge Thee to be God, and glorify Thine eternal existence.[^2564]

*Glory:*  
Come unto us, O Comforter, filling with Thy comfort those who theologically speak of Thine ineffable glory.[^2565]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
O Bride of God, All-Blameless, deliver from temptations by thy prayers those who glorify thee and honor thee worthily.[^2566]  
*In place of "It is truly meet," we sing the same Heirmos.*  
*After the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the feast.*[^2567]  
*On Monday of the Holy Spirit (and according to others: of the Life-Creating Trinity), at the Midnight Office:*[^2568]  
*After the 1st Trisagion, we say the Troparion of the feast.*  
*After the 2nd Trisagion, we say the Kontakion of the feast. Lord, have mercy (12 times).*  
*And the Dismissal. But the prayers for the departed we do not say.*

## ON MONDAY OF THE HOLY SPIRIT, AT MATINS[^2569]

*At "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the feast, thrice.*[^2570]  
*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*  
The post-festal, O ye faithful, and final feast let us celebrate brightly; this is Pentecost, the fulfillment of the promise and of the pledge; for on this day the Fire of the Comforter came down upon the earth, as in the appearance of tongues, and enlightened the Disciples, and revealed them to be initiates of heavenly mysteries: the light of the Comforter has come, and enlightened the world.[^2571]  
*Glory, both now, the same.*  
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone and Podoben the same:*  
The Fountain of the Spirit, having come upon the earth, dividing Itself noetically into fiery rivers, bedewed the Apostles, enlightening them; and the fire became for them a bedewing cloud, enlightening them and raining down flame, through which we have received grace by fire and by water: the light of the Comforter has come, and enlightened the world. (Twice)[^2572] *Then both canons of the feast: that of Tone 1 with the heirmos to 8, and that of Tone 4 with the heirmos to 6.*
*Katavasia: "Covered by divine cloud," both choirs together.*[^2573]  
*After Ode 3, Kontakion to the Holy Trinity, Tone 3:*  
O Trinity consubstantial and indivisible, Unity three-hypostatic and co-eternal: unto Thee as God we cry the angelic song: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, our God, praised in the Trinity, have mercy on us.[^2574]  
*Then, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва):*  
The All-Holy Spirit, having now descended upon the Apostles in fiery form, filled the gatherings of the nations with awe; for as they spoke with fiery tongues, O Lover of mankind, each heard his own language; therefore the wonder was known unto the unbelieving as drunkenness, but unto the faithful truly as saving; for this cause we glorify Thy dominion, O Christ God, asking that remission of offenses be richly sent down upon Thy servants.  
*Glory, both now, the same.*[^2575]  
*After Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of the feast.*[^2576]  
*Synaxarion on Monday after Pentecost, that is, of the Holy Spirit:*[^2577]  
*Verses:*  
*Let every breath glorify the Spirit of the Lord,*  
*By Whom the audacity of the evil spirits fell.*[^2578]  
On this day, Monday of Pentecost, we celebrate that All-Holy, and Life-Creating, and All-Powerful Spirit, One of the Trinity, God, equal in honor, consubstantial, and co-glorious with the Father and the Son. On this day of Pentecost, indeed, He came upon the Holy Apostles substantially, in the form of fiery tongues, sitting upon each one of them in the upper room, wherein they were abiding. And for the sake of honor toward the All-Holy Spirit, and separately, also on this Pentecost the divine Fathers, who understood all things well, ordained to celebrate Him.[^2579] For the Savior, before the Passion, promising the coming of the Comforter, said: "It is expedient for you that I go away; for if I go not away, the Comforter will not come" (John 16:7). And again: "When He is come, He will teach you, and guide you into all truth" (John 16:13). And again: "I will pray the Father, and He shall give you another Comforter, the Spirit of truth, Who proceeds from the Father" (John 14:16, 15:26). And after the Passion also, when He was ascending into heaven, He said: "Tarry ye in Jerusalem, until ye be endued with power from on high" (Luke 24:49). He sends Him, therefore, unto them who were abiding in His Name; and when the day of Pentecost was fully come, at the third hour of the day in the upper room, suddenly there was a thunder from heaven, such that even a multitude of the inhabited earth hastened together; and in the form of fiery tongues the Holy Spirit appeared, unto each one of them, not only to the Twelve, but also to the Seventy.[^2580] And they spoke with strange tongues, that is, each one of the Apostles spoke with all the languages of the nations. Not indeed that while the Apostle spoke in his own language, the foreigner heard, but each language of a nation the Apostle heard and spoke; whence also unto the gathered multitudes they seemed to be drunk. For each not knowing how the Apostle particularly spoke in all languages, supposed that he was drunk. And others marveled, saying: "What then will this be?" For these were gathered from all the earth for the sake of the feast: Parthians, Medes, and Elamites, who not long before had been taken captive by Antiochus.[^2581] Ten days, then, having passed after the Ascension, the Holy Spirit comes, and not immediately when Christ ascended, that He might make the Disciples who were awaiting Him all the more fervent. And some say that on each of the days, each one of the angelic ranks, coming, bowed down before that deified flesh. Ten days then having been completed, on the tenth day the Comforter came, reconciliation having been made by the Son.[^2582] And fifty days after Pascha, in remembrance of the Old Law; for Israel, from the time it passed through the Red Sea, fifty days having elapsed, received the Ten Commandments. Behold also the signs: there a mountain, here an upper room; there fire, here fiery tongues; and in place of thunders and darkness, here a violent wind.[^2583] In the form of tongues, therefore, the Holy Spirit came down, showing that He has affinity with the living Word; or also because the Apostles were to teach and bring the nations, having it by tongue. Of fire, moreover, because God is a consuming fire (Deut. 4:24, Heb. 12:29), and for the sake of purification. Divided, again, for the sake of gifts. And as of old He divided into many the one language of them that were building [the tower],[^2584] and divided it: so now, He divided into many the one language of them that knew it, that He might gather from those nations scattered throughout the ends of the inhabited earth.[^2585] And at the feast it takes place, that many being gathered together, the matter might be proclaimed everywhere, and that those who were present at Pascha and had heard of the wonders concerning Christ, might have cause to wonder.[^2586] And at Pentecost, because it was fitting that at the very time when the Law was given of old, after it the grace of the Spirit should also be poured out; even as Christ did at the legal Passover, having accomplished His own Pascha, the true Pascha. Not upon the eyes did the Holy Spirit sit, but upon the heads of the Apostles, embracing that which is qualitative, and the highest part of the body, and the mind itself, from which the tongue also has the faculty of speaking. Or because the Spirit pours out a certain voice as an image by the tongues, so as to ordain the Apostles through their heads to be active teachers of the whole world; for ordination takes place upon the crown of the head.[^2587] And the sound and the fire, because these also were at Sinai: that the same Spirit may be shown to be the Lawgiver and the Disposer of all, both then and now. And the multitude was confounded by the noise of the wind, because they thought that the end had come, which Christ had foretold unto the Jews concerning their destruction. And like fiery ones, it says, that no one should think anything bodily concerning the Holy Spirit. By drunkenness, then, the Apostles are accused; but Peter, standing up in their midst, then refuted this thing that was not truly held, having adduced the prophecy of Joel to his speech, and converted about three thousand from among them (Acts 2:14-41).[^2588] And the Holy Spirit is called the Comforter, as being able to comfort and give rest unto us: for in place of Christ we have received Him, having Him also by this: for He also intercedes for us with ineffable groanings unto God (Rom. 8:26), standing before us as the Lover of mankind, even as Christ also does: for the Son is also a Comforter. For this reason the Holy Spirit is called "another Comforter." For the Apostle says: "We have an Advocate with the Father, Jesus Christ" (1 John 2:1). And "another" is said for consubstantiality; for to say "another and another" [*allos kai allos*] is said of those that are of the same essence and divine; but to say "another thing and another thing" [*allo kai allo*] we know of different natures.[^2589] This Holy Spirit is one with the Father and the Son in all things; therefore He also works all things together with Them: and that very resurrection which is to come. And whatever He wills, He does: He sanctifies, He divides, He renews, He sends together, He makes wise, He anoints the Prophets; simply to speak, He is self-governing in all things: all-powerful, good, upright, sovereign. Therefore from Him is all wisdom, life, movement, sharing in every way in holiness and in every life. Simply, He has all things that the Father and the Son have, except unbegottenness and begottenness: proceeding from the Father and the Son.[^2590] The Holy Spirit then having been poured out upon all flesh, the world was filled with all kinds of gifts, and through them all the nations were guided unto the knowledge of God, and every sickness and every plague was driven away. And threefoldly was the Holy Spirit given by Christ unto the Disciples: before the Passion, not very manifestly; after the Resurrection, through breathing, more manifestly; and now He sent Him substantially: or rather, He Himself descended perfect, enlightening and sanctifying them, and through them again bringing the ends of the universe, by the coming of the Holy Spirit.[^2591]
By the prayers of the Holy Apostles, O Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.[^2592]  
*We do not sing "More honorable."*  
*After Ode 9, the Exapostilarion of the feast, twice; the other, once.*[^2593]  
*At the Praises, "Let every breath praise the Lord," we set 6 verses, and sing idiomelic stichera, Tone 2:*[^2594]  
In the Prophets Thou didst make known unto us the way of salvation, and in the Apostles shone forth, O our Savior, the grace of Thy Spirit: Thou art God the First, Thou art also after these things, and unto the ages Thou art our God.[^2595]  
In Thy courts I will hymn Thee, the Savior of the world, and bending my knees I will worship Thine unconquerable power, evening, and morning, and at midday, and at every time I will bless Thee, O Lord.[^2596]  
In Thy courts, O Lord, we the faithful, having bent the knees of our souls and bodies, hymn Thee, the Unoriginate Father, and the Co-unoriginate Son, and the Co-eternal and All-Holy Spirit, Who enlightens and sanctifies our souls.[^2597]  
*Glory, both now, Tone 8:*  
The tongues of old were confounded because of the audacity of the tower-building; but the tongues now were made wise because of the glory of the knowledge of God; there He condemned the ungodly by their error, here Christ enlightened the fishermen by the Spirit; then dumbness was abolished unto punishment, now accord is renewed unto the salvation of our souls.[^2598]  
*Great Doxology. Troparion of the feast, litanies. And we say the dismissal of the feast: "May He Who in the form of fiery tongues..." and the First Hour.*[^2599]

*At the Liturgy: The Typika. And from the canon of the feast, Odes 3 and 6.*[^2600]  
*Then the priest or deacon:*  
**Deacon:** Wisdom, let us attend!  
*The Entrance Hymn:*  
Be Thou exalted, O Lord, in Thy strength; we will sing and praise Thy mighty acts (Ps. 20:14 LXX).[^2601]  
*Troparion, Tone 8: "Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God..."*[^2602]  
*Glory, both now:*  
*Kontakion, Tone 8:*  
When the Most High came down and confused the tongues, He divided the nations; but when He distributed the tongues of fire, He called all into unity: and with one accord we glorify the All-Holy Spirit.[^2603]  
*The Trisagion.*  
*Prokeimenon of the Epistle, Tone 6:*  
Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance (Ps. 27:9 LXX).  
*Verse: Unto Thee, O Lord, will I cry: O my God, be not silent unto me (Ps. 27:1 LXX).*[^2604]  
*Epistle to the Ephesians, section 229 (Eph. 5:8b-19):*  
"Walk as children of light..."[^2605]  
*Alleluia, Tone 2:*  
Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy (Ps. 50:3 LXX).  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*[^2606]  
*Gospel according to Matthew, section 75 (Matt. 18:10-20):*  
"Take heed that ye despise not one of these little ones..."[^2607]  
*Communion Hymn:*  
Thy good Spirit shall lead me in the right land (Ps. 142:10 LXX).  
Alleluia, thrice.[^2608]  
*Be it known: That this week of the Holy Pentecost is fast-free with dispensation for cheese, fish, wine, and oil.*[^2609]  
*The Mid-Hours of the Hours are also omitted, as is the Canon of the Most Holy Theotokos usually sung at Compline.*[^2610]

## ON MONDAY OF HOLY PENTECOST, IN THE EVENING[^2611]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and sing prosomoia stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^2612]  
Now the tongues are renewed to declare the majesty of God in other-speaking words, as in fiery form, for the confirmation of all that are upon earth, to relate them most clearly; now for a sign unto the nations in truth, Thou hast joined salvation.[^2613]  
The power from on high which Thou didst promise to send down unto Thy Disciples, O Christ, having fulfilled it, Thou sendest Thy Holy Spirit, having shown unto us that Thou art the Truth, and they that trust in Thee, O Good One, truly are not put to shame.[^2614]  
Having fulfilled the pre-announcement, Thou hast sent Thy Comforter to the world as in fiery tongues, to burn up the sins of all by divine Fire, and to grant unto us true communion with Him, who faithfully speak theologically of Him.[^2615]  
*And 3 from the Menaion.*[^2616]  
*Glory, both now, Tone 1:*  
We celebrate Pentecost, and the coming of the Spirit, and the pledge of the promise, and the fulfillment of hope; and a mystery how great, as it is both great and venerable! Therefore we cry unto Thee: Creator of all, O Lord, glory to Thee.[^2617]  
*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^2618]  
In deed unto the Disciples, as Thou didst promise, O Word, Thou hast sent Thy divine and Holy Spirit, and hast enlightened them.[^2619]  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*[^2620]  
Suddenly from heaven the power of the Divine Comforter showed all the Apostles to be all-wise and God-speaking.[^2621]  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*[^2622]  
The race of the foreigners, hearing in their own languages, were perplexed at the divine Apostles magnifying the Trinity.[^2623]  
*Glory, both now, Tone 1:*  
With the tongues of foreigners Thou hast renewed Thy Disciples, O Christ, that through them they might proclaim Thee, the immortal Word and God, Who grantest great mercy unto our souls.[^2624]  
*On Tuesday.*[^2625]

## ON TUESDAY AFTER PENTECOST, AT MATINS[^2626]

*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^2627]  
I number together, I sing together, I glorify together, and I honor together with the Father and the Son the all-holy Spirit, uniting [Them] in Godhead, yet dividing [Them] in personal properties; for having now clothed Christ's Apostles, He made them most wise to preach in all the world.  
*Glory, both now: the same.*  
*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^2628]  
The grace of the Spirit, having dreadfully descended from heaven in the form of fire, enlightened Christ's Disciples and showed them forth as luminaries, proclaiming the one power and one Lordship of the Holy Trinity, Which we glorify by faith.  
*Glory, both now: the same.*  
*Psalm 50. Canon of the feast, Tone 4, with the heirmos to 8, and of the Menaion to 4.*[^2629]  
*Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the feast.*  
*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^2630]  
Having enlightened their mind with the God-originating radiance, Thy Disciples, O Word, essentially received the Holy Spirit.  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*  
Tongues with fire, seen from on high, came down upon the Apostles, being divided, enlightening, not consuming.[^2631]  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*  
Having come to us from on high, O Comforter, as formerly unto the Apostles, sanctify and save those who proclaim Thee as God.  
*Glory, both now, Tone 1:*  
The Holy Spirit provides all things: He pours forth prophecies, He perfects priests, He taught wisdom to the unlearned, He revealed fishermen as theologians, He gathers the whole assembly of the Church. O Comforter, consubstantial and co-enthroned with the Father and the Son, glory to Thee![^2632]

*On Tuesday of Holy Pentecost, the Triodion:*[^2633]  
*Ode 2, Tone 2. Heirmos:*  
God: His works are true, and all His ways are judgments (Deut. 32:4 LXX).[^2634]  
*Troparion:*  
O Right Spirit, guide onto right ways those who speak theologically of Thee.  
*Troparion:*  
O Comforter Who camest from heaven, save those who praise Thee.  
*Troparion:*  
Being Life that makes alive and Light, O Comforter, save those who praise Thee.  
*Glory:*  
Being true God and Benefactor, O Comforter, save those who praise Thee.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
O Virgin Mother, Thou gavest birth to true God unto our salvation; pray Him on behalf of us all.  
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Praise ye God, Who descended into the fiery furnace unto the Hebrew youths and turned the flame into dew, as works of the Lord, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages.[^2635]  
*Troparion:*  
Unto the all-wise Disciples gathered in the upper room, tongues suddenly appeared divided, as of fire, and the sound of a violent wind was heard, while they exalted Christ supreme unto the ages.  
*Troparion:*  
Another Comforter, Christ our Comforter, sent down unto us by grace, even the Spirit Who spake in the Prophets and Who spoke through fiery tongues of the God-proclaiming and all-wise Apostles.[^2636]  
*Glory:*  
The Wellspring of gifts, the Giver of good things, the other Comforter, having come unto Christ's Disciples, renewed their glory, having shone forth through them the light of piety.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Him Who sits inseparably together in the bosom of the Father, Who dwelt within thy womb, O Pure One, we praise as Bride of God, and we exalt Him supreme unto the ages.  
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam, who by eating fell grievously into corruption, and Who for our sake was ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin, we the faithful magnify with one accord in hymns.[^2637]  
*Troparion:*  
He that sanctifies the angelic choirs and pilots their ranks as God, He hath now come, having descended from heaven, and having made the all-wise Apostles most wise with divine lightnings, He sent them forth to the inhabited earth.  
*Troparion:*  
The Holy Spirit manifestly distributes the wealth of the Godhead, abiding and untakeable, unto the Apostles in fiery tongues, and He made them stronger than fire against deceit, burning up the mire of idols.  
*Glory:*  
O Spirit of truth and understanding, Spirit of wisdom, of might, and of counsel, Who proceeds from the Father and, for the sake of the Son, is richly given unto creation, save us who by faith worship Thy dominion.[^2638]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
O Virgin blessed of God, all my hope do I earnestly set upon thee: save me, O Mother of Life itself, and vouchsafe me, O Pure One, to be satisfied with eternal sustenance, that in faith and love I may magnify thee in hymns.

## ON TUESDAY AFTER PENTECOST, AT VESPERS[^2639]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses, and sing prosomoia stichera: Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^2640]  
As innate, as proper to rational beings, [was it given] to the dumb with fire-formed tongues to proclaim unto the nations the glory of God, Who adorned the heavens with fire and illuminated the sensible world, together with the Son and the Spirit.  
Unto Christ's Disciples gathered in Zion, as was the promise, the Holy Spirit, having come upon them in fiery form, showed them forth as fire-inspired, speaking with mystical utterances, eloquently declaring the doctrines of the Trinity.  
Unforged swords, the sacred Disciples, the Holy Spirit having come perfected; and through them they purified the earth for God the Creator, burning up the unsanctified, so that the weapons of the evil one might vanish and our souls be saved.[^2641]  
*And 3 from the Menaion.*  
*Glory, both now, Tone 2:*  
We have seen the true Light, we have received the heavenly Spirit, we have found the true faith, worshipping the undivided Trinity; for She hath saved us.[^2642]  
*At the Aposticha, prosomoia stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^2643]  
Today the operation of Thy most Holy Spirit came upon Thine Apostles, O Lord, and having shown them forth as all-wise through knowledge of God, He filled them with Thy blessed teaching; therefore we glorify Thy saving dispensation, O Jesus almighty, Savior of our souls.  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*  
Today Thy Spirit all-powerful, O Master, is sent from the Father, being consubstantial with Thee, distributed in fiery tongues, Who disposed Thine Apostles to speak Thy marvelous works; therefore we glorify Thy saving dispensation, O Jesus almighty, Savior of our souls.  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*  
Today Thou didst pour out, O Savior, the gifts of Thy Comforter Spirit, granting unto human nature to prophesy, even as Thou saidst, O Word, and Thou didst declare that we should worship the Trinity indivisibly; therefore we glorify Thy saving dispensation, O Jesus almighty, Savior of our souls.[^2644]

*Glory, both now, Tone 6:*  
O Lord, the descent of the Holy Spirit, which filled Thine Apostles, disposed them to speak in other tongues; therefore this most glorious event seemed drunkenness to the unbelieving, but to the faithful an intercession of salvation. Vouchsafe us also His illumination, we pray Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^2645]  
*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." Troparion of the feast, and the Dismissal.*  

## ON WEDNESDAY AFTER PENTECOST, AT MATINS[^2646]

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast, thrice.*  
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^2647]  
The spiritual grace having come, from poor fishermen Christ enlightened Thine Apostles as all-wise and princes, by whose teaching we also ever celebrate the bright feast of Pentecost, praising Thee together with the Father and the Divine Spirit.  
*Glory, both now: the same.*  
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^2648]  
The all-holy Spirit having now descended upon the Apostles in the appearance of fire, filled the assemblies of the nations with awe; for when they spake with fiery tongues, O Lover of mankind, each heard his own language; therefore the marvel was recognized by the unbelieving as drunkenness, but by the faithful truly as salvation. Therefore we glorify Thy dominion, O Christ God, asking that forgiveness of transgressions be richly sent down upon Thy servants.[^2649]  
*Glory, both now: the same.*  
*Canon of the feast, and of the Menaion.*  
*Kontakion and Exapostilarion of the feast.*  
*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^2650]  
O Comforter Who camest unto us from on high, even as formerly unto the Apostles, sanctify and save those who proclaim Thee as God.

*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*  
Suddenly from heaven the power of the divine Comforter showed all the Apostles to be all-wise and theologians.  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*  
Having enlightened their mind with the God-originating radiance, Thy Disciples, O Word, essentially received the Holy Spirit.  
*Glory, both now, Tone 6:*  
O Heavenly King, Comforter, Spirit of truth, Who art everywhere present and fillest all things, Treasury of good things and Giver of life: come and abide in us, and cleanse us from every impurity, and save our souls, O Good One.[^2651]  
*Then the 1st Hour, and the Dismissal.*  

*On Wednesday of Holy Pentecost, the Triodion:*  
*Ode 3, Tone 2. Heirmos:*  
Thou Who art the confirmation of them that trust in Thee, confirm, O Lord, the Church which Thou didst purchase with Thy precious Blood.[^2652]  
*Troparion:*  
He Who confirmed the heavenly powers, the Comforter Spirit, today showed the Apostles not to be dumb.[^2653]  
*Troparion:*  
The Spirit consubstantial, equal in power, co-enthroned, and of like form with the Father and the Word, worshipping piously we magnify.  
*Glory:*  
The undefiled, purest Spirit, the most pure Comforter, we speak of theologically, as ruling over all creation.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
A holy temple, and ark, and lampstand, O all-pure Lady, hast thou been, of the never-setting Light, having shone forth Christ unto those in darkness.  
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Joined unto the unbearable Fire, the youths standing for piety, unharmed by the flame, sang the divine hymn: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages.[^2654]  
*Troparion:*  
We worship the one indivisible divine Essence in three Persons, singing of the single Sovereignty and Kingdom with godly wisdom: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.  
*Troparion:*  
The life-originating and divine Spirit, and perfective, recognized in the Trinity, we praise orthodoxly with thrice-holy glorifications: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.  
*Glory:*  
Spirit intellectual, Spirit divine, imparting qualities, all-richly distributing operations of gifts unto those singing in faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The beauty of Jacob, even thee most comely in godly beauty, O all-pure Mother of God, He Who is fairer than all, God the Word, chose, unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^2655]  
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
In the shadow and the scriptures of the Law we see a type, O ye faithful: every male that opens the womb is holy unto God; therefore the firstborn Word of the unoriginate Father, the Son Who is the firstborn of a Mother that knew not man, we magnify.[^2656]  
*Troparion:*  
The most honorable and grace-moved Spirit we speak of theologically, Who of old in the Prophets pre-announced the present illumination and the all-rich outpouring of good gifts, distributed unto the Disciples and witnesses of the Word.[^2657]  
*Troparion:*  
The divine-originating, unharmed Spirit we glorify, Who pours forth and works divine gifts for all, the Spirit, the other Comforter, Who proceeds from the unoriginate Father and is understood together with the co-unoriginate Son.  
*Glory:*  
The beneficent and rich Spirit we praise in song, Who sanctifies all and illuminates them with intelligible light, the almighty, ever-hymned Dominion, honored together with the Father and glorified together with the Son.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The shadow of the Law hath passed away, and illumination hath shone forth upon them that sit in darkness, for He Who was incarnate of thee, O Virgin, by reason of His abundant tender mercy, was born of the unoriginate Father before the ages, consubstantial with Him that begat Him.  

## ON WEDNESDAY AFTER PENTECOST, AT VESPERS[^2658]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses: 3 of the feast, and 3 of the Menaion. Stichera of the feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^2659]  
This day of joy and gladness, from on high appeared unto the sacred Disciples the Holy Spirit today, from the Father in fiery form, and He enlightened all; He revealed fishermen as wise narrators, as the God-proclaiming Luke wrote, filling all things with a violent breath; therefore marveling, in faith we praise the abundant mercy and uttermost condescension of Christ our God.[^2660]

We celebrate Pentecost, the glory of the Holy Spirit, and the true joy, the fulfillment of divine hope, and of the mystery revealed unto us before the ages; therefore we also cry unto Thee: Creator of all, take not Thy Spirit away from us, O Word, but send Him from heaven, O Savior, and by the prayers of Thy holy and all-glorious, honorable Apostles, grant peace unto our souls, we pray Thee.[^2661]  
The heavens, being clothed, declare today the marvelous deeds of the Savior, and they traversed all the world, clearly to proclaim the divine power of the Comforter, and they enlightened all creation by their teachings, that we may hymn the undivided Trinity, one in three unconfused Persons; therefore by faith we celebrate the coming of the Holy Spirit, Which appeared from on high and illuminates the ends of the earth.  
*Glory, both now, Tone 7:*  
Having Christ God as our Advocate with the Father, unto the other Comforter Who hath come unto us upon earth today, even the Holy Spirit, let us bow down in faith.[^2662]  
*Then: "O Gladsome Light."*  
*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы):*[^2663]  
The Holy Spirit, Who proceeds from the Father and is glorified in the Son, by Whom all things are borne and upheld, and are sustained in life, and live, and abide, and are saved, we speak of theologically: O incomprehensible Comforter, grant peace unto Thy world![^2664]  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*  
The Holy Spirit, the Wellspring of every goodness, came essentially to fill the ends of the earth with heavenly power through the Apostles and with divine goodness; unto Him let us cry: O ineffable Comforter, grant peace unto Thy world!  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*  
The Holy Spirit is God, recognized as innate and co-enthroned with the Father and the Word, Light most perfect having shone forth from Light, from the unoriginate and perfect Father, and coming forth through the Son; unto Him let us cry: O Giver of life, Comforter, grant peace unto Thy world!  
*Glory, both now, Tone 8:*  
When Thou sentest Thy Spirit, O Lord, upon the sitting Apostles, then the children of the Hebrews, beholding it, were struck with awe; for they heard them speaking in other foreign tongues, even as the Spirit gave them utterance; for being unlearned, they were made wise, and having caught the nations into the faith, they declaimed divine things. Therefore we also cry unto Thee: O Thou Who didst appear on earth and didst save us from delusion, O Lord, glory to Thee![^2665]  
*Then: "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart..." and the Dismissal.*

## ON THURSDAY AFTER PENTECOST, AT MATINS[^2666]

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion of the feast, thrice.*  
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^2667]  
Now are fulfilled the prophetic foretellings; for He Who concealed these things with shadowy beams as a manifestation of things to come, as God the Comforter, hath now been poured out richly upon the Apostles, and through them upon those who faithfully worship the uncreated Trinity.  
*Glory, both now: the same.*  
*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*[^2668]  
The lovers of the Savior were filled with joy and received boldness, who formerly were fearful; for the Holy Spirit today came down from on high upon the house of the Disciples, and one spake one thing and another another unto the people; for tongues were visibly parted as fire, and consumed them not, but rather bedewed them.  
*Glory, both now: the same.*  
*Canon of the feast, Tone 7, with the heirmos to 8, and of the Saint in the Menaion to 4. After Ode 6, Exapostilarion of the feast, twice.*[^2669]  
*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́внѡ):*[^2670]  
The all-holy Spirit, consubstantial with the Father and the Word, having come upon the Disciples, made their earthen tongues to be of fire, enlightening them with divine grace.  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*  
Renew within us, O God, the all-holy Spirit Whom of old Thou sentest unto Thy Disciples, O Merciful One, and Whom Thou didst strengthen to fulfill Thy saving purposes in the world.  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*  
Thou Who spakest of old in the Prophets, O Comforter, hast now made the divine Apostles to utter the glory of God; through whom grant unto us cleansing and great mercy.

*Glory, both now, Tone 2:*  
In Thy courts, O Lord, we the faithful, bowing the knees of our souls and bodies, hymn Thee, the unoriginate Father, and the co-unoriginate Son, and the co-ever-existing and most Holy Spirit, Who illuminates and sanctifies our souls.[^2671]  
*Then the 1st Hour, and the Dismissal.*  

*On Thursday of Holy Pentecost, the Triodion:*  
*Ode 4, Tone 4. Heirmos:*  
He that sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the most divine, is come with incorrupt hand, and hath saved them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^2672]  
*Troparion:*  
The pre-ordained decree of the true promise is fulfilled: the light-bearing grace came by Pentecost; the glory of the Comforter appeared unto them that cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!  
*Troparion:*  
Incomprehensible is the all-holy Spirit: He surpasses every mind and the swiftness of thought, as being more ancient than all ages, as ranked together with the Father and the Only-Begotten Son.[^2673]  
*Glory:*  
Without beginning of being, and without ceasing afterward, but ever-existing, an immeasurable abyss of wealth, the almighty, co-eternal Spirit is glorified together with the Father and the Only-Begotten Son.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The all-holy Spirit overshadowed thee, O all-holy Maiden, having made thee a fair and divinely-befitting dwelling-place for the Incarnation of the Son, exalted above all.  
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
O Redeemer of all, Almighty One, descending into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew them that exercised piety, and didst teach them to sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, praise ye the Lord.[^2674]  
*Troparion:*  
Disperse the mist of sorrow, O All-Good One, by Thy consolation, from them that piously praise Thee and faithfully cry: Bless the Lord, ye works of the Lord.  
*Troparion:*  
Distributing gifts by Thy will unto whom Thou desirest, O Comforter, unto those who know Thee as God grant Thy grace, who in faith cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.  
*Glory:*  
As true God, the Comforter is faithfully worshipped and served by those who piously glorify and unceasingly cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Having given birth to God incarnate, assuage the tumult of my passions, and enlighten my soul, vouchsafing me to sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Eve, through the sickness of disobedience, brought in a curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the blossoming of childbearing, didst cause blessing to bloom forth unto the world; therefore we all magnify thee.[^2675]  
*Troparion:*  
The divine gift of the Spirit was poured out upon all flesh that believed, even as the Prophet of old, rejoicing as a seer, foretold; and we, beholding now the fulfillment of the things foretold, are enlightened.[^2676]  
*Troparion:*  
Thou filledst Thy God-proclaiming Disciples, O Savior, with the all-holy Spirit, and Thou didst send them forth into the world like rays illuminating with the light of piety, as Master and Lord; therefore we all magnify Thee.  
*Glory:*  
A cleansing fire bedewed the hearts of the Disciples: the dew of the Spirit descending from heaven enlightened the eyewitnesses of the Word, to shine forth unto the world the knowledge of God of the Trinity's illumination.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Of the Holy Spirit, O Pure One, thou didst conceive the Word Who shone forth agelessly from the unbegotten Father, and Thou gavest birth to Him incarnate, for the sake of His uttermost love of mankind and goodness, O Mother of God, Virgin Mother.  

## ON THURSDAY AFTER PENTECOST, AT VESPERS[^2677]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 6 verses: 3 of the feast, and 3 of the Menaion. Stichera of the feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^2678]  
Today the operation of Thy most Holy Spirit came upon Thine Apostles, O Lord, and having shown them forth as all-wise through knowledge of God, He filled them with Thy blessed teaching; therefore we glorify Thy saving dispensation, O Jesus almighty, Savior of our souls.  
Today Thy Spirit all-powerful, O Master, is sent from the Father, being consubstantial with Thee, distributed in fiery tongues, Who disposed Thine Apostles to speak Thy marvelous works; therefore we glorify Thy saving dispensation, O Jesus almighty, Savior of our souls.  
Today Thou didst pour out, O Savior, the gifts of Thy Comforter Spirit, granting unto human nature to prophesy, even as Thou saidst, O Word, and Thou didst declare that we should worship the Trinity indivisibly; therefore we glorify Thy saving dispensation, O Jesus almighty, Savior of our souls.  
*Glory, both now, Tone 2:*  
The consubstantial Trinity we praise in song: the Father, and the Son, with the Holy Spirit; for Her did all the Prophets and Apostles with the Martyrs proclaim.[^2679]

*On Thursday of Holy Pentecost at evening:*[^2680]  
*Entrance: "O Joyful Light."*  
*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^2681]  
Moses saw Him Who is upon the mountain by fire; but unto the God-beholders and wise Apostles also, the Spirit now descends in the appearance of fire, manifestly showing forth that it is one God Who spake then, and now, in the identity of essence.[^2682]  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*  
The God-proclaiming Apostles, with fire-formed tongues, calling [men] unto the water of divine baptism, burned up the babblings of the godless orators with the fire of the Spirit; but do Thou, O Comforter, enlighten us also, who faithfully speak theologically of Thee.  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*  
Those who served Thee in matter, O my God, having ineffably received the coarseness of matter,[^2683] as of old Thou didst the immaterial angels, Thou dost recreate fiery with the fire of the Spirit; for Thou art much-praised, for Thou art marvelous in Thy judgments, O Lover of mankind.  
*Glory, both now, Tone 7:*  
Having Christ our God as an Advocate with the Father, now that another Advocate hath come unto us upon the earth today, we worship the Holy Spirit in faith.  
*Then, "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart"; troparion of the feast, and dismissal.*  
*Know this also: that of the services of the saints occurring on the coming Saturday and on the Sunday of All Saints, we sing that of the one beforehand, and that of the other on this present Friday at Compline.*[^2684]  

## ON FRIDAY OF HOLY PENTECOST, AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," troparion of the feast, thrice.*  
*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^2685]  
All the prophecies of the Prophets are fulfilled; for He that covered these things with obscure rays, at the ends of the earth making His manifestation as God the Comforter, hath now been poured out richly upon the Apostles, and through them also upon those who faithfully worship the uncreated Trinity.  
*Glory, both now: the same.*  

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^2686] [^2687]  
The all-holy Spirit, having now descended upon the Apostles in the appearance of fire, filled the gatherings of the nations with amazement; for while they spake with fiery tongues, O Lover of mankind, each heard his own language. And so the wonder was recognized by the unbelieving as drunkenness, but by the faithful truly as salvation.[^2688] Therefore we glorify Thy dominion, O Christ God, [praying Thee] richly to send down remission of transgressions upon Thy servants.  
*Glory, both now: the same.*  
*Canon of the feast, Tone 7, with the heirmos to 8, and of the saint to 4.*  
*After Ode 6, Exapostilarion of the feast, twice.*  
*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ):*[^2689]  
The all-holy Spirit, Who is praised with the Father and with the Son, let us the faithful hymn, unceasingly bowing down, glorifying as God the all-holy Trinity, O ye peoples.  
*Verse: Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me (Ps. 50:12 LXX).*  
Thee, God the Comforter, do we faithfully hymn with the sacred and divine Apostles; for at the third hour, even as He promised, Christ sent Thee from the Father of lights.[^2690]  
*Verse: Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me (Ps. 50:13 LXX).*  
O Holy Trinity, glory to Thee! In hymns I worship in a Trinitarian manner the Father, and the Right Spirit, with the Son; and singing unto Thee, O divine Trinity, I worthily and faithfully honor Thee.  
*Glory, both now, Tone 8:*  
When Thou didst send down Thy Spirit, O Lord, upon the Apostles as they sat, then the Hebrew children beholding were struck with dread amazement; for they heard them speaking in other, strange tongues, even as the Spirit gave them utterance. For though unlearned, they were made wise, and having caught the nations unto faith, they eloquently spoke of divine things.[^2691] Therefore we also cry unto Thee: O Thou Who didst appear upon earth and hast saved us from deceit, O Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Then the First Hour, and dismissal.*  

*On Friday of Holy Pentecost, the Triodion:*[^2692]  
*Ode 5, Tone 5. Heirmos:*  
O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, unto Thee do I rise early and unto Thee do I cry: Enlighten my darkened soul, O Christ, as Thou alone art compassionate.[^2693]  
*Troparion:*  
The all-holy Spirit, Who proceeds unconfusedly from the unoriginate Father,[^2694] and rests upon the Son, let us the faithful glorify.  
*Troparion:*  
Being renewed in our hearts, and edifying and sanctifying those in whom He takes up His abode, let us with the Apostles radiantly receive Him.  
*Glory:*  
The ineffably mighty and almighty, all-holy Spirit, Who renews those who before had been corrupted and enlightens all, let us clearly speak of theologically.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
O manifest mountain, mountain overshadowed, mountain fat and curdled, which God hath loved:[^2695] O holy Virgin, save thy servants.  
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Unto Thee, the Creator of all, the youths in the furnace, having woven a universal choir, sang: All ye works, praise the Lord, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages.[^2696]  
*Troparion:*  
Thou art the preservation and keeping of all that have come to be, O Comforter; save those who sing unto Thee in faith.  
*Troparion:*  
Thou art the distribution of incorruptible gifts, and the deep of grace, and wealth unutterable, O divine and Holy Spirit; and Thee do we hymn and exalt supreme unto the ages.  
*Glory:*  
The life-originating and life-bearing grace of the divine and Holy Spirit, Which renews us by Its breathing, we hymn and exalt supreme unto the ages.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The Fashioner, the Wisdom of God, created an animate temple out of thee, O Virgin; save those who faithfully honor thee, O all-pure one, and exalt Him supreme unto all ages.  
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Rejoice, O Isaiah! The Virgin had in her womb and brought forth a Son, Emmanuel, both God and man; Dayspring is His name:[^2697] Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed.  
*Troparion:*  
Thou didst offer our first-fruits unto Thy Father, O Savior, and having bestowed upon us the riches of the divine nature, Thou sentest forth the good and life-creating Holy Spirit, Who is co-unoriginate with Thee, O Master.  

*Troparion:*  
The Spirit of the Father now grants the first-fruits of the Godhead unto earthly nature, not being estranged from the Only-begotten, but co-abiding as one in nature, consubstantial, of equal honor, and co-enthroned.[^2698] [^2699]  
*Glory:*  
Thou hast revealed Thy divine Disciples as a hymn, O Savior, speaking in fiery tongues of Thy fearful condescension unto us, now sending upon them from heaven the light-creating operation of the Comforter.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Thou wast the bridal chamber of the Holy Spirit, O Theotokos, from whom the Fashioner, the Father's Word, came forth as man; and having dwelt among us, He hath now revealed the knowledge of the Trinity.  

## ON FRIDAY OF HOLY PENTECOST, AT VESPERS[^2700]

*On Friday of Holy Pentecost at evening, regular Kathisma.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera and the entire service of the feast, without readings and entrance, as was directed for the Apodosis of Christ's Ascension.*  
*Likewise also on this 6th Saturday at Matins, the entire service of the feast, without Polyeleos, Songs of Ascents, and Gospel:*  
*At Matins:*  
*First canon of the feast with the heirmos to 8; the heirmos twice.*  
*Second canon of the feast with the heirmos to 6; the heirmos twice.*  
*Katavasia: the heirmos of the second canon, "Covered by the divine darkness," both choirs together.*[^2701]  
*Great Doxology, litanies, and dismissal.*  
*At the dismissal the priest commemorates the saint whose day it is.*  
*And the First Hour.*  
*At the Hours, Troparion and Kontakion of the feast.*  
*And final dismissal.*  

*On Saturday of Holy Pentecost, the Four-Ode Canon:*[^2702]  
*Ode 6, Tone 6. Heirmos:*  
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing to Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly-Merciful One.[^2703]  
*Troparion:*  
Thou didst operate first in the angelic hosts, granting radiance and enlightening them with the lightnings of the Godhead, O Holy Spirit, and keeping them by a unique sanctification.[^2704]  
*Troparion:*  
Thou hast revealed the Prophets, having power to foresee with foreknowledge things to come; having taken up Thine abode in their hearts as Good, O Comforter, Thou disposedst them to perceive things far off.  
*Glory:*  
Lifted up from the earth by reason of divine virtues, Christ's Apostles received the Master's Spirit Who came from heaven, and shone forth with the radiance of the Godhead.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The Holy Spirit, having come upon thee, made thee a pure and radiant temple of virginity, an all-pure dwelling for the Son of the Highest, O all-blessed one.  
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Angel made the furnace dew-yielding for the venerable youths, but the command of God burning up the Chaldeans persuaded the tormentor to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^2705]  
*Troparion:*  
The all-causing Father and Begetter, the only Son before the ages, and the proceeding Holy Spirit, let us the faithful concordantly glorify evermore: Blessed is the God of our fathers.  
*Troparion:*  
The Comforter, bearing the personal property of the Godhead, hath now come; for conversing with the Apostles in fiery tongues, He was manifestly shown forth as God: Blessed is the God of our fathers.[^2706]  
*Glory:*  
As immovable heavens by Thy Spirit of equal honor, Thou didst establish Thy Disciples, O Christ, to proclaim Thee, the great Sun Who clearly dawned upon us: Blessed is the God of our fathers.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
In the incarnation of the Word, the Holy Spirit, having overshadowed thee, O Pure One, made thee a divine vessel and radiant ark, O all-pure, blessed one, who gavest birth to God in the flesh.  
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Out of the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and the sacrifice of the righteous one Thou didst consume with water; for Thou doest all things, O Christ, simply by willing: Thee do we exalt supreme unto all ages.[^2707]  
*Troparion:*  
Incomprehensibly proceeding from the Father, and unconfusedly resting upon the Son, the honorable and almighty Spirit, Thee do we exalt supreme unto the ages.  

*Troparion:*[^2708]  
Cleanse us from defilements of soul, wash us from the filth of sin, and grant us, O Christ, a right Spirit, Who renews salvation for souls unto all ages.[^2709]  
*Glory:*  
O Thou Who callest those dwelling in the graves, and renewest the face of all the earth, mortify our earthly members, who worship Thee unto all ages.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Thou wast revealed as an earthly heaven, O Pure One, having contained within thy womb the heavenly Word, Who drew near in the flesh unto those upon the earth: Whom we exalt supreme unto the ages.  
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the angelic ranks dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word was revealed unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.[^2710]  
*Troparion:*  
Clearly speaking in fiery tongues, the Disciples burned up the delusion of idols, and illuminated the souls of the faithful with the most radiant divine operation of the Comforter, Who came manifestly.  
*Troparion:*  
Thou didst show forth Thy wise Disciples as pure receptacles of divine grace and undefiled mirrors of the divine radiance, through the God-originating shining of the Spirit, and Thou didst send them forth, O Good One, as Thou art Greatly-Merciful.  
*Glory:*  
Vouchsafe those who sing unto Thee to attain divine blessedness by Thy coming, O Comforter, as the Wellspring of good things, granting communion in panoply gifts as the Giver of good, O Thou alone Lover of mankind.  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
Unto God for men, O Pure One, thou didst effect reconciliation beyond nature, having become an intercessor unto Him on our behalf; for through thee, O all-pure one, He gathered into one the things previously scattered, and we were all accounted worthy of the heavenly divine inheritance and delight.  
*At the Liturgy:*[^2711]  
*After the entrance, Troparion of the feast; Glory, both now, Kontakion of the feast.*  
*Trisagion. Prokeimenon and Alleluia of the feast.*  
*Epistle of the day: to the Romans, Pericope 74 (Rom. 1:7-12):*  
*"Paul, a servant of Jesus Christ..."*  
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 5 (Matt. 5:42-48):*  
*"Give to him that asks thee..."*  
*Communion Verse of the feast: "Thy good Spirit shall guide me in the right land (Ps. 142:10 LXX). Alleluia."*[^2712]  

## SUNDAY OF ALL SAINTS: ON SATURDAY EVENING, AT SMALL VESPERS[^2713]

*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 4 verses, and sing resurrectional stichera, 4: Tone 8:*[^2714]  
*"An evening hymn..."*  
*"O Lord, Lord, cast us not away..."*  
*"Rejoice, O holy Zion..."*  
*"The Word begotten of God the Father..."*  
*Glory, Tone 6:*  
*"The divine choir of martyrs..."*  
*Seek all these at Great Vespers.*  
*Both now, Theotokion Dogmatikon, Tone 8:*[^2715]  
How shall we call thee blessed, O Theotokos? Or how shall we praise in hymn, O All-Blessed one, the incomprehensible mystery of thy childbearing? For the Creator of the ages and the Fashioner of our nature, taking pity on His own image, brought Himself down into unsearchable emptying: He Who is in the immaterial bosom of the Father took up His abode within thy womb, O Pure One, and became flesh without change from thee, O Thou that knewest not wedlock, remaining what He was, God by nature. Therefore we worship Him as perfect God and perfect man, Him in His own form; for both natures are truly in Him, and we proclaim all His twofold natural properties according to each essence, reverencing two operations and wills: for being consubstantial with God and the Father, He sovereignly wills and operates as God; and being consubstantial with us, He sovereignly wills and operates as man. Pray Him, O Pure, All-Blessed one, that our souls may be saved.  

*At the Aposticha, resurrectional sticheron: "Thou didst ascend the Cross, O Jesus..." Seek at the Aposticha at Great Vespers. Then Theotokia, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^2716] [^2717]  
*Verse: I will remember thy name in every generation and generation (Ps. 44:18 LXX).*  
Rejoice, O all-praised Theotokos! Rejoice, O Wellspring that pourest forth life unto the faithful! Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady of all and blessed Mistress of creation! Rejoice, O All-Immaculate, Most-Glorified one! Rejoice, O All-Pure one! Rejoice, O Palace! Rejoice, O Divine Tabernacle! Rejoice, O Pure One! Rejoice, O Virgin Mother! Rejoice, O Bride of God![^2718]  
*Verse: Hear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear (Ps. 44:11 LXX).*  
Rejoice, O all-pure Mother of God! Rejoice, O Hope of the faithful! Rejoice, O Cleansing of the world! Rejoice, thou that deliverest thy servants from every affliction! Rejoice, O life-bearing Consolation of men! Rejoice, O Protection! Rejoice, O Intercession of those who call upon thee! Rejoice, O divine Abode of God and Holy Mountain!  
*Verse: The rich among the people shall entreat thy face (Ps. 44:13 LXX).*  
Rejoice, O Theotokos, Mother of Christ! Rejoice, O only Hope of men and Protection! Rejoice, O Refuge! Rejoice, O radiant Candlestick of the Light! Rejoice, O sanctified Candle! Rejoice, O Palace! Rejoice, O Paradise! Rejoice, O Divine Tabernacle! Rejoice, O Wellspring that pourest forth waters unto those who hasten to thee!  
*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*  
He Whom the heaven could not contain, O Virgin Theotokos, was contained within thy womb, though uncontainable, and thou remainedst pure, by an ineffable word, thy virginity being defiled in nothing; for thou alone among women wast both Mother and Virgin, and thou alone, O All-Pure one, didst nurse the Life-giving Son, and in thine arms didst carry the slumberless Eye;[^2719] for He left not the Father's bosom, even as He was before all ages: on high all God with the Angels, below all of thee with men, and everywhere ineffably. Pray Him, O All-Holy Sovereign Lady, that those may be saved who in an Orthodox manner confess thee as the Pure Theotokos.  
*After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," and after "Our Father."*  
*Resurrectional troparion; Glory, both now, Theotokion; and litany, and dismissal.*  

## AT GREAT VESPERS[^2720]

*After the customary psalm, the first Kathisma, entire.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 10 verses, and sing resurrectional stichera, 3; and Eastern, 3; and of the saints, 4.*  
*Resurrectional stichera, Tone 8:*  
An evening hymn and rational worship do we offer unto Thee, O Christ, for Thou wast well pleased to have mercy upon us by Thy Resurrection.[^2721]  
O Lord, Lord, cast us not away from Thy face, but be well pleased to have mercy upon us by Thy Resurrection.  
Rejoice, O holy Zion, Mother of the Churches, Dwelling-place of God; for thou first receivedst remission of sins by His Resurrection.  
*Eastern Stichera:*[^2722]  
The Word begotten of God the Father before the ages, the very same in the last times voluntarily incarnate of her that knew not wedlock, endured the crucifixion of death, and saved man, slain of old, by His Resurrection.  
Thy Resurrection from the dead do we glorify, O Christ, whereby Thou didst set free the race of Adam from the tyranny of Hades, and as God didst grant unto the world eternal life and great mercy.  
Glory to Thee, O Christ Savior, Only-begotten Son of God, Who wast nailed upon the Cross, and didst rise from the tomb on the third day!  
*Stichera of All Saints, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^2723]  
Spiritual orators, Disciples of the Savior, having become instruments of the Spirit through faith, were scattered unto the ends of the earth, sowing the honorable preaching in an Orthodox manner; from whom, by divine husbandry and grace, sprang forth the armies of the Martyrs, imaging the honorable Passion by manifold dismemberments, wounds, and fire; and with boldness they pray for our souls.  
Enkindled by the fire of the Lord's love, they despised fire, and ignited like divine coals in Christ, the honorable Martyrs burned up the stealthy insolence of deceit; they muzzled the mouths of wild beasts by honorable invocation, and being beheaded, they cut down all the battle arrays of the enemy, and patiently pouring out streams of blood, they watered the Church that is enlightened by faith.  
Fighting with wild beasts, beheaded with the sword, torn with iron claws, having their hands severed, and tormented, the steadfast Martyrs, burned unsparingly with material fire, pierced, and dismembered in their joints, endured most firmly, foreseeing the future rest, the incorruptible crowns, and the glory of Christ, unto Whom with boldness they pray for our souls.[^2724]  

Those who suffered faithfully in all the ends [of the earth]—the Apostles, Martyrs, God-wise Priests, and the sacred gathering of honorable Women—let us worthily praise in sacred hymns; for the earthly were united with the heavenly, and through sufferings they received dispassion by Christ's grace, and now like radiant stars illuminating us, with boldness they pray for our souls.[^2725]  
*Glory, Tone 6:*  
O divine choir of Martyrs, foundation of the Church, fulfillment of the Gospel, ye have fulfilled the Savior's sayings by deed; for by you the gates of Hades opened against the Church were shut; the shedding of your blood dried up the idol sacrifices; your slaughter brought forth the plenitude of the Church; ye amazed the bodiless powers; as crown-bearers ye stand before God: Whom unceasingly pray for our souls![^2726]  
*Both now, Theotokion Dogmatikon, Tone 8:*  
The King of heaven, for His love of mankind, appeared on earth and dwelt among men; for having taken flesh of the pure Virgin and come forth from her with the assumption [of our nature], He is one Son, twofold in nature, but not in hypostasis.[^2727] Therefore, truly proclaiming Him perfect God and perfect man, we confess Christ our God: Whom pray, O unwedded Mother, that our souls may have mercy shown them.  
*Entrance: "O Joyful Light."*  
*Prokeimenon of the day: "The Lord is King" (Ps. 92:1 LXX), and the readings.*  
*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Isa. 43:9-14 LXX):*[^2728]  
Thus saith the Lord: All the nations are gathered together, and princes shall be gathered out of them. Who will declare these things, or who will make you hear things from the beginning? Let them bring forth their witnesses, and let them be justified, and let them hear, and let them speak the truth. Be ye My witnesses, and I too am a witness, saith the Lord God, and the Servant Whom I have chosen; that ye may know, and believe Me, and understand that I am He: before Me there was no other God, and after Me there shall be none. I am God, and besides Me there is no savior. I have declared, and have saved; I have reproached, and there was no strange god among you. Ye are My witnesses, and I am the Lord God; even from the beginning I am He, and there is none that can deliver out of My hands: I will work, and who shall turn it back? Thus saith the Lord God, Who delivers you, the Holy One of Israel.  
*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wis. 3:1-9):*[^2729]  
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was taken for misery, and their going from us to be utter destruction; but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, yet is their hope full of immortality; and having been a little chastened, they shall be greatly rewarded: for God proved them, and found them worthy of Himself. As gold in the furnace hath He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering sacrifice. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine, and run to and fro like sparks among the stubble. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over people, and their Lord shall reign over them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand the truth, and such as be faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy are in His saints, and visitation is among His elect.

*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wis. 5:15 - 6:3):*[^2730]  
The righteous live unto the ages, and in the Lord is their reward, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive the kingdom of majesty, and a crown of beauty from the hand of the Lord; for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take His jealousy as a panoply, and shall arm creation to avenge His enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall put on unfeigned judgment as a helmet. He shall take holiness as an invincible shield; He shall sharpen severe wrath for a sword; and the world shall fight with Him against the senseless. The shaft-hurled lightning arrows shall go forth, and as from a well-curved bow of the clouds they shall fly unto the mark. And from the stone-casting wrath hailstones shall fall full. The sea water shall rage against them, and the rivers shall inundate them sharply. A spirit of power shall stand against them, and like a whirlwind it shall fan them away; and lawlessness shall lay waste all the earth, and evildoing shall overturn the thrones of the mighty. Hear therefore, O kings, and understand; learn, ye judges of the ends of the earth! Give ear, ye that rule multitudes, and take pride in crowds of nations! For power is given unto you from the Lord, and strength from the Most High.  

*At the Litiya, the sticheron of the temple.*  
*Then idiomel stichera of All Saints.*  
*Tone 1:*  
With the harmony of faith, a cosmic festival, let us spiritually celebrate the memory of all the saints who from all ages have been well-pleasing unto God: the honorable assembly of the Patriarchs and the Prophets, the adornment of the Apostles, the congregation of the Martyrs, the praise of the Ascetics; for they unceasingly pray that peace be granted unto the world, and unto our souls great mercy.[^2731]  
Come, all ye faithful, let us praise in psalms, hymns, and spiritual songs the all-glorious memory of all the saints: honorably calling blessed the Savior's Baptist, the Apostles, the Prophets, and the Martyrs, the Hierarchs, the Teachers, and the Venerable ones, the Ascetics, and the Righteous, and the God-loving gathering of holy Women, let us concordantly cry aloud: O All-Good Christ our God, by their prayers grant peace unto Thy Churches, victories over enemies unto the Christ-loving Emperor, and unto our souls great mercy.[^2732]  
Come, all ye, let us spiritually rejoice in the memory of the saints; for behold, it hath arrived, bearing unto us enriching gifts. Therefore with a voice of rejoicing and a pure conscience let us cry aloud, saying: Rejoice, O assembly of the Prophets, who preached Christ's coming to the world, and foresaw from afar as near at hand! Rejoice, O choir of the Apostles, net-casters of the nations and fishers of men![^2733] Rejoice, O congregation of the Martyrs, gathered from the ends of the earth into the one faith, who for its sake endured the torments of afflictions and perfectly received the crown of suffering! Rejoice, O delight of the Fathers, who having withered your bodies by fasting and mortified the carnal passions, were winged by the divine Spirit with soaring, flew up unto the heavens, and dwelling with the Angels, receive eternal good things! But O ye Prophets, Apostles, and Martyrs with the Ascetics, fervently entreat Him Who crowned all, that those who ever celebrate your honorable memory in faith and love may be delivered from enemies visible and invisible.  
*Glory, Tone 5:*  
Let us assemble for the present festival, ye faithful; for there is set before us a spiritual banquet of sweet delicacies, and a mystical cup filled with rejoicing of the virtues of the martyrs: for these patient-hearted ones from the ends of the earth, having surrendered their whole body unto diverse wounds, unto God brought— —a rational sacrifice, of every age: some indeed beheaded at the head, others scraped along the back, and others by the severed amputation of hands and of all their limbs; together all the saints became partakers of Christ's sufferings: but grant crowns unto them as the recompense of torments, O Lord, and vouchsafe us to live in their footsteps, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^2734]
*Both now, Theotokion, the same Tone:*  
Thou art the temple and gate, the palace and the throne of the King, O all-honorable Virgin: through whom my Redeemer, Christ the Lord, appeared unto those sleeping in darkness, being the Sun of righteousness, willing to enlighten that which He fashioned according to His own image with His hand: therefore, O All-Praised one, as thou hast acquired maternal boldness toward Him, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved.  
*At the Aposticha, resurrectional stichera:*  
*Tone 8:*  
Thou didst ascend the Cross, O Jesus, Who camest down from heaven; Thou didst come unto death, O immortal Life, the true Light unto those who are in darkness, the Resurrection of all unto the fallen: our Enlightenment and Savior, glory to Thee!  
*Other stichera, Eastern, alphabetical:*[^2735]  
*Verse: The Lord is King, He is clothed with majesty (Ps. 92:1 LXX).*  
We praise in hymn Christ, Who is risen from the dead; for taking a soul and a body, He severed both from passion: His most pure soul indeed descended into Hades, which He despoiled, while in the tomb the holy body of the Redeemer of our souls saw no corruption.  
*Verse: For He established the world, which shall not be shaken (Ps. 92:1 LXX).*  
With psalms and songs we praise in hymn Thy Resurrection from the dead, O Christ, whereby Thou hast delivered us from the tyranny of Hades, and as God hast granted eternal life and great mercy.  
*Verse: Holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days (Ps. 92:5 LXX).*  
O incomprehensible Sovereign Master of all, Creator of heaven and earth, suffering through the Cross Thou didst pour forth dispassion for me; and receiving burial and rising in glory, Thou didst raise Adam up with Thyself by Thine almighty hand: glory to Thy third-day Arising, whereby Thou hast granted us eternal life and cleansing of sins, as Thou alone art compassionate.  
*Glory, Tone 6:*  
Come, ye faithful, having gathered a choir today let us piously celebrate: and gloriously let us honor the all-glorious and honorable memory of all the saints, saying: Rejoice, ye glorious Apostles, Prophets, Martyrs, and Hierarchs! Rejoice, O congregation of the Venerable ones and of the Righteous! Rejoice, O choir of honorable Women, and entreat Christ for the world, that He grant victories unto the Emperor over barbarians, and unto our souls great mercy.[^2736]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*[^2737]  
My Creator and Redeemer, O Most-Pure one, Christ the Lord, having come forth from thy womb, clothed Himself in me, and delivered Adam from the first curse: therefore unto thee, O All-Pure one, as to God's— —Mother and Virgin, we truly cry aloud unceasingly with the angelic: Rejoice, O Sovereign Lady, Intercession, and Protection, and Salvation of our souls!
*After the Trisagion, Troparion: "O Theotokos and Virgin, rejoice," twice; and of the saints, once.*  
*And the blessing of the loaves. And the reading of the General Epistle of James.*[^2738] [^2739]  
*If, however, a vigil is not held, at "Lord, I have cried," resurrectional stichera, 3; and Eastern, 1; and of the saints, for 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше).*  
*After the Trisagion, resurrectional troparion; Glory, of the saints; Both now, Theotokion, written at the beginning of Matins.*  
*And the dismissal.*  
*At the Midnight Office, the Triadicon Canon of the Octoechos, of the Tone.*  

## SUNDAY OF ALL SAINTS: AT MATINS

*At "God is the Lord," resurrectional Troparion:*  
*Tone 8:*  
Thou didst come down from on high, O Compassionate One; Thou didst accept three-day burial, that Thou mightest set us free from passions: O our Life and Resurrection, Lord, glory to Thee![^2740]  
*Glory, troparion of the saints, Tone 4:*  
Thy Church, adorned with the blood of Thy Martyrs throughout the whole world as with purple and fine linen, through them cries unto Thee, O Christ God: Send down Thy compassions upon Thy people, grant peace unto Thy commonwealth, and unto our souls great mercy.[^2741]  
*Both now, Theotokion:*  
The mystery hidden from all ages and unknown to Angels, through thee, O Theotokos, was revealed unto those upon earth: God incarnate in unconfused union, willingly accepting the Cross for our sake, whereby having resurrected the first-formed, He saved our souls from death.[^2742]  
*After the 1st Kathisma-reading, resurrectional Sessional Hymn:*  
*Tone 8:*  
Thou didst rise from the dead, O Life of all, and a radiant Angel cried unto the women: Cease from tears, proclaim the glad tidings unto the Apostles, cry aloud singing: That Christ the Lord is risen, Who was well pleased as God to save the human race.  

*Verse: Arise, O Lord my God, let Thy hand be lifted up (Ps. 9:33 LXX).*[^2743]  
The soldiers sealed Thy tomb, O Savior; an Angel rolled away the stone from the doors; the women beheld Thee risen from the dead, and they proclaimed the glad tidings unto Thy Disciples in Zion: That Thou art risen, O Life of all, and the bonds of death are loosed: Lord, glory to Thee!  
*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*  
O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin, and enduredst crucifixion, despoiling death by death, and manifesting the Resurrection as God: despise not those whom Thou hast fashioned with Thy hand; manifest Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Theotokos who gave Thee birth, who intercedes for us; and save, O our Savior, a despairing people.  
*And a reading from the Commentary on the Gospel.*  
*After the 2nd Kathisma-reading, Sessional Hymn:*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*[^2744]  
Gabriel, clad in white, radiant as in the appearance of lightning, stood before Christ's tomb, and rolled away the stone from the tomb; and great fear held the guards, and suddenly they all became as dead: by reason of the guards at the tomb and the seal upon the stone, be ashamed, ye most lawless ones! Understand that Christ is risen!  
*Another Sessional Hymn:*  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости Сло́ва):*[^2745]  
Having truly risen from the tomb, Thou didst command the venerable women to proclaim the Arising unto the Apostles, even as it is written. And swift Peter ran to the tomb, and beholding the light in the tomb, he was astonished. Therefore also seeing the winding-sheets lying alone within it without the divine Body, with fear he cried aloud: Glory to Thee, O Christ God, for Thou savest all, O our Savior, being the Father's Radiance.  
*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*  
*We sing this not sitting, but standing, and with fear and reverence we utter the present hymn:*[^2746]  
All creation rejoices in thee, O full of grace: the angelic choir and the human race; O hallowed Temple and rational Paradise, virginal Boast, from whom God became incarnate and became an Infant, Who is our God before the ages! For He made thy womb a Throne, and made thy belly more spacious than the heavens. All creation rejoices in thee, O full of grace: Glory to thee!  
*And a reading from the Commentary on the Gospel.*  
*After the Evlogitaria (Psalm 118), the troparia: "The angelic council..." And after them:*  
*Hypakoe, Tone 8:*  
The Myrrh-bearing women standing before the tomb of the Life-giver sought the immortal Master among the dead, and receiving the joy of the glad tidings from the Angel, they announced unto the Apostles: That Christ God is risen, granting great mercy unto the world.[^2747]  
*And immediately the Hymns of Ascents (Anabathmoi), Tone 8.*  

*Antiphon 1:*[^2748]  
From my youth the enemy tempts me, he burns me with pleasures; but trusting in Thee, O Lord, I vanquish him.
