Let them that hate Zion become as grass before it is plucked up; for Christ shall cut through their necks with the cutting of torments.[^2749]  
*Glory:*  
Through the Holy Spirit all things have life: Light of Light, great God, we praise Him with the Father and the Word.  
*Both now, the same.*  
*Antiphon 2:*  
Let my heart be covered with Thy fear, thinking in humility: lest being lifted up it fall away from Thee, O All-Compassionate One.[^2750]  
He that hath hope in the Lord shall not fear then, when He will judge all things with fire and torment.  
*Glory:*  
Through the Holy Spirit, everyone that is divine sees and foretells: he works wonders on high, singing of one God in Three: for though the Godhead shine in three lights, It rules as one Principle.  
*Both now, the same.*  
*Antiphon 3:*  
I cried unto Thee, O Lord: give heed, incline Thine ear unto me when I cry, and cleanse me before Thou takest me hence.  
Everyone that departs unto the earth his mother is again resolved, to receive either torments or rewards for what he hath lived.  
*Glory:*  
Through the Holy Spirit is theology, a thrice-holy Monad: for the Father is unoriginate, from Whom the Son was timelessly begotten, and the Spirit is co-enthroned, con-formal, having shone forth from the Father.[^2751]  
*Both now, the same.*  
*Antiphon 4:*  
Behold now, what is good or what is pleasant? But that brethren dwell together (Ps. 132:1 LXX); for in this the Lord promised life eternal.[^2752]  
Concerning his garment, He Who adorns the lilies of the field commands that it is not fitting to take thought.  
*Glory:*  
By the Holy Spirit, as the unitive Cause, all things are held together peace-bestowingly: for He is God, consubstantial in Godhead with the Father and the Son.  
*Both now, the same.*  

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*[^2753]  
The Lord shall reign for ever, Thy God, O Zion, unto generation and generation (Ps. 145:10 LXX).  
*Verse: Praise the Lord, O my soul; I will praise the Lord in my life (Ps. 145:1-2 LXX).*  
*Let every breath.*  
*Resurrection Gospel according to Matthew, 1st Matins Gospel, Pericope 116 (Matt. 28:16-20): "The eleven disciples..."*  
*From this day we begin to read the resurrectional Matins Gospels in order.*[^2754]  
*"Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ..." And Psalm 50.*  
*Glory: "Through the prayers of the holy Apostles..." Both now: "Through the prayers of the Theotokos..." Sticheron, Tone 6: "Jesus having risen from the tomb..."*  
*Canons, 4: Resurrectional with its heirmos, for 4; Cross-Resurrectional, for 2; of the Theotokos, for 2; and of All Saints, for 6.*  
*Resurrectional Canon.*  
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Striking crosswise and dividing the sea, the rod of Moses, working wonders of old, engulfed Pharaoh the charioteer; but it saved Israel the fugitive, who marched on foot, singing a hymn unto God.[^2755]  
*Troparion:*  
How shall we not marvel at Christ's almighty Godhead? Which pours forth dispassion and incorruption unto all the faithful from His sufferings, and from His holy side causes a wellspring of immortality to drop down, and from the tomb life everlasting.  
*Troparion:*  
As comely an Angel now appeared unto the women, bearing luminous natural images of immaterial purity, and by his appearance announcing the light of the resurrection, crying: The Lord is risen!  
*Theotokion:*  
Glorious things were spoken of thee throughout generations of generations, O thou that containedst God the Word within thy womb, yet remainedst pure, O Virgin Mary: therefore we all honor thee, our intercession after God.  
*Cross-Resurrectional Canon.*  
*Heirmos: Having traversed the water (Во́дꙋ проше́дъ):*[^2756]  
*Troparion:*  
The gates of pain were lifted up, and the gatekeepers of Hades were terrified, beholding Him Who descended unto the lowest regions, Who on high is beyond all nature.  
*Troparion:*  
The angelic ranks marveled, beholding seated upon the Father's throne fallen human nature, which had been shut up in the subterranean depths of the earth.  
*Another Canon, to the Most-Holy Theotokos.*  
*Heirmos: Let us sing unto the Lord (Пои́мъ Гдⷭ҇ви):*  
*Troparion:*  
O most-pure Theotokos, who gavest birth beyond nature to the incarnate, everlasting, and pre-essential Word, we sing unto thee.  
*Troparion:*  
The Virgin brought forth Christ, the life-bearing cluster of grapes, dropping down the sweetness of salvation for all the world.  

*Canon of All Saints, whose acrostic is: Tῶν ἁγίων πάντων πολυώνυμα τάγματα μέλπω, that is: I sing the multifold ranks of all the saints.*[^2757]  
*Tone 8. Heirmos: Pharaoh the charioteer (Колесницегони́телѧ Фараѡ́на):*  
*Troparion [T]:*  
Singing the ranks of Thy saints, I pray that my soul may be illumined with Thy light through their prayers: for Thou art unapproachable Light, driving away the gloom of ignorance by Thy splendors, O Word of God, Christ the Giver of Light. *(Twice)*  
*Troparion [Ω]:*  
When Thou wast lifted up upon the tree, Thou didst draw unto the knowledge of Thee the whole inheritance of the nations, O Sovereign Master; and with the light of the Holy Trinity Thou didst enlighten them through Thy holy Apostles, through whom Thou chasedst away the mist of deception.  
*Troparion [N]:*  
Strengthened by Thy lawgiving, O Christ, Thy glorious Apostles piously rejected all things upon the earth, and with the light of grace enlightened the whole inhabited world, preaching Thee in Gospel fashion.  
*Glory [Α]:*  
Rejoicing, the valiant Martyrs took up Thy Cross, and unswervingly imitating Thy honorable Passion, they feared not the tyrant's threat, neither fire, nor sword, nor wounds, nay, neither hunger, nor death.  
*Both now, Theotokion [Γ]:*  
The maidens, O All-Pure one, manifestly took up a manly resolve: for unswervingly imitating the sufferings of the martyrs, and in psalmic manner following after thee unto Thy Son, O Virgin, divinely rejoicing together, they were brought unto the King of all.  
*Katavasia:*[^2758]  
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen Mother, and I shall be seen radiantly celebrating, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.  
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^2759]  
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters, establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of the Church; for there is none holy save Thee, O Thou alone Lover of mankind.  
*Troparion:*  
Adam, who was condemned by the tasting of sin, Thou hast justified by the saving Passion of Thy flesh, O Christ: for Thou Thyself wast free from the trial of death, O Sinless One.  
*Troparion:*  
The light of the resurrection shone forth upon those sitting in darkness and the shadow of death, my God Jesus; and having bound the strong one by His Godhead, He plundered his vessels.  
*Theotokion:*  
Thou wast revealed higher than the Cherubim and Seraphim, O Theotokos; for thou alone didst receive the uncontainable God within thy womb, O Undefiled one: therefore all we faithful with hymns call thee blessed, O Pure One.  

*Another Canon [Cross-Resurrectional].*[^2760]  
*Heirmos: Fashioner of the vault of heaven (Нбⷭ҇нагѡ крꙋ́га верхотво́рче):*[^2761]  
*Troparion:*  
When I had rejected the commandment of old, O Lord, and was cast out from Thee, Thou didst make [Thyself] into that in which I was fashioned; and learning obedience, Thou didst fashion me anew unto Thyself by the Crucifixion.  
*Troparion:*  
Foreknowing all things in wisdom, O Lord, and having established the nethermost parts by Thine understanding, by Thy condescension Thou didst vouchsafe, O Word of God, to raise up that which is according to Thine image.  
*Another Canon [of the Theotokos].*  
*Heirmos: Thou art the establishment (Ты̀ є҆сѝ оу҆твержде́нїе):*  
*Troparion:*  
Grant us help by thy prayers, O All-Pure one, repelling the assaults of grievous afflictions.  
*Troparion:*  
Thou wast the restoration of Eve our foremother, having given birth, O Theotokos, unto the Author of life for the world, Christ.  
*Canon of All Saints.*[^2762]  
*Heirmos: Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens (Оу҆твержде́й въ нача́лѣ нб҃са̀):*  
*Troparion [I]:*  
Vested in sacred priesthood, Priests and Pastors, wisely shepherding it, O Christ, worthily adorned the word of teaching, being truly enriched from on high. *(Twice)*  
*Troparion [Ω]:*  
Adorned with the embellishments of primal virtue, and having appeared as unerring luminaries, ye made heavenly the Church of Christ, O Saints, wonderfully adorning her diversely on every side. *(Twice)*  
*Glory [N]:*  
Obeying Thy law, O Master, the assemblies of the blessed, enlightened by manifold virtues, rejoicing inherited the heavenly mansions: for some one, some another, they all worthily fulfilled them.  
*Both now, Theotokion [Π]:*  
From a virginal womb thou didst bear unto us God the Word, Who is from God, O Mother of God, Whom pure virgins divinely loved, and following after thee, they all manifestly followed Him.  
*Sessional Hymn.*[^2763]  
*Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва):*  
Celebrating the holy memory of Thy Forefathers, Fathers, Patriarchs, Apostles, Martyrs, Hierarchs, Prophets, and Thy Venerable ones, Ascetics and the Righteous, and of every name written in the book of life, O Christ God, we move all unto prayer, entreating Thee: Pacify Thy world through them, as Thou art the Lover of mankind, that we all may cry aloud unto Thee, O God Who art glorified in the council of Thy saints: Thou art He Who hast truly and worthily glorified their memory!  
*Glory, the same; Both now, Theotokion:*  
Let us hymn the Heavenly Gate and Ark, the All-Holy Mountain, the Radiant Cloud, the Unburnt Bush, the Rational Paradise, the Recall of Eve, the Great Treasury of all the inhabited world: for salvation was wrought in her for the world— —and remission of ancient transgressions; therefore we cry unto her: Pray to thy Son, that remission of transgressions be granted unto those who piously worship thine all-holy Nativity.
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^2764]  
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who leftest not the bosom of the Father and visitedst our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!  
*Troparion:*  
When I was an enemy, Thou didst love me exceedingly: by a marvelous emptying Thou camest down unto the earth, O compassionate Savior, not rejecting my uttermost humiliation, and remaining on the height of Thy most pure glory, Thou didst glorify him who before was dishonored.  
*Troparion:*  
Beholding this, O Sovereign Master, who is not astonished now that death is destroyed by the Passion, corruption put to flight by the Cross, and Hades despoiled of its riches by death, through the divine power of Thee, the Crucified: marvelous is the deed, O Lover of mankind!  
*Theotokion:*  
Thou art the boast of the faithful, O unwedded Bride; thou art intercessor, thou also the refuge of Christians, wall and haven: for thou bearest entreaties unto thy Son, O All-Immaculate one, and savest from perils those who with faith and love acknowledge thee as the pure Theotokos.  
*Another Canon [Cross-Resurrectional].*  
*Heirmos: I heard, O Lord (Оу҆слы́шахъ Гдⷭ҇и):*  
*Troparion:*  
The children of the lawless nailed Thee upon the Cross, O Christ God; whereby as Compassionate Thou savedst those who glorify Thy sufferings.  
*Troparion:*  
Risen from the tomb, Thou raisedst up with Thyself all the dead who were in Hades, and as Compassionate Thou enlightenedst those who glorify Thy Resurrection.  
*Another Canon [of the Theotokos].*  
*Heirmos, the same:*  
*Troparion:*  
O untilled field, that broughtest forth the life-bearing ear of wheat, Who grants life unto the world, O Theotokos: save those who sing unto thee.[^2765]  
*Troparion:*  
Being enlightened, we all proclaim thee as Theotokos, O All-Pure one; for thou gavest birth unto the Sun of righteousness, O Ever-Virgin.  
*Canon of All Saints.*[^2766]  
*Heirmos: Thou art my strength, O Lord (Ты̀ моѧ̀ крѣ́пость Гдⷭ҇и):*  
*Troparion [Α]:*  
The congregation of Thy saints hath gathered in love, and enjoying Thee truly and purely in gladness, an indissoluble choir joined in chorus with the Angels, in Thee, O All-Seeing One, God of all and Lord. *(Twice)*  
*Troparion [N]:*  
With youthful vigor, ye glorious Hieromartyrs, ye bound your brows with the martyr's crown, most richly adorned first with the God-effecting anointing of the priesthood; therefore worthily receiving twofold crowns, ye rejoice everlastingly with Christ.  
*Troparion [T]:*  
All wisdom of the flesh ye truly subjected unto the spirit, O God-bearers, ascetically withering its unrestrained impulses with fasting: and now enlightened with the light of dispassion, ye have received the rewards of your labors.  

*Glory [Ω]:*[^2767]  
As Martyrs of Christ the First Martyr, ye endured grievous torments: patiently and valiantly, as though suffering in alien bodies, O glorious ones; and now shown forth as heirs of the Kingdom, ye pour forth healings unto the faithful.  
*Both now, Theotokion [N]:*  
Thou wast the Bride of God, O Mother of God, for thou gavest birth unto His pre-eternal Word in the flesh for us: unto Whom also the women having lived holy lives, and through thee having adorned themselves with manifold sufferings, corrected the foremother's fall.  
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^2768]  
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O unsetting Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and guide my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.  
*Troparion:*  
Thou didst endure being clothed in purple, mocked before Thy Passion, O Savior, covering the unseemly nakedness of the first-formed, and naked wast Thou nailed upon the Cross in the flesh, stripping off, O Christ, the garment of mortification.[^2769]  
*Troparion:*  
From the dust of death, rising Thou didst fashion anew my fallen essence, and didst make it unaging, O Christ, having shown it forth again as a royal image, shining with the life of incorruption.  
*Theotokion:*  
Having maternal boldness toward thy Son, O All-Pure one, despise not the kindred providence for us, we pray thee: for thee alone do we Christians present unto the Sovereign Master as a propitiatory cleansing.  
*Another Canon [Cross-Resurrectional].*  
*Heirmos: Enlighten us (Просвѣти́ на́съ):*  
*Troparion:*  
Guide us in the light by the power of Thy Cross, O Christ: for thereby we fall down before Thee; grant us peace, O Lover of mankind.  
*Troparion:*  
Steer our life, as Thou art All-Good, who sing Thy Resurrection, and grant us peace, O Lover of mankind.  
*Another Canon [of the Theotokos].*  
*Heirmos: Rising early at dawn we cry unto Thee (Оу҆́тренююще вопїе́мъ тѝ):*  
*Troparion:*  
Calm the intolerable tempest of my passions, O thou that gavest birth unto God, the Steersman and Lord.  
*Troparion:*  
The angelic ranks and the assembly of men serve thy childbearing, O most-pure Theotokos.  
*Canon of All Saints.*[^2770]  
*Heirmos: Why hast Thou cast me off (Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ):*  
*Troparion [Π]:*  
Ye were accounted worthy to foresee things to come even as the divine Prophets, and desiring nobility of soul to be cleansed by a pure life, O God-bearers, ye were illuminated by spiritual power. *(Twice)*  
*Troparion [Ο]:*  
The choir of the Saints now shines with divine gifts: all the Patriarchs before the law, the Prophets, the Apostles, and the assemblies of the Martyrs, and of the Ascetics, and of the Teachers, and of the Righteous, together with the Hieromartyrs. *(Twice) Glory...*[^2771]
Beholding the assembly of Thy Saints today shining with Thy brightness, O Savior, and with unwaning lamps of grace, we unceasingly hymn Thy divine wealth of good things and abundance, O Lover of mankind.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Having loved Thy marvelous Offspring, most honorable Women, they accounted the pleasures of this life as nothing, O All-Pure One, desiring only the brightness and divine illumination of its Lover.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.*[^2772]

By the tree the author of evil powerfully cast me down; but Thou, O Christ, being lifted up upon the Cross, didst more powerfully cast him down, putting him to shame, and didst raise up fallen man.

Thou didst take pity on Sion, having shone forth from the tomb, making the new instead of the old, as Thou art compassionate, by Thy divine Blood; and now Thou reignest in it unto the ages, O Christ.

*Theotokion:*
That we may be delivered from grievous transgressions by thy supplications, O pure Birth-giver of God, and that we may obtain, O Most-Pure One, the divine radiance of the Son of God Who was ineffably incarnate of thee.

*Another Heirmos:*
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord:*
Thou didst stretch forth Thy palms upon the Cross, healing the unrestrained hand stretched forth in Eden by the first-created man; and of Thy will Thou didst taste of gall, and didst save, O Christ, as Thou art mighty, those who glorify Thy Passions.[^2773]

A Deliverer from death didst Thou appear, from the ancient condemnation, that He might destroy the kingdom of corruption; and He descended into Hades, and raised up and saved, as He is mighty, those who hymn His Resurrection.

*Another Heirmos:*
*Grant unto me a radiant robe:*
As the Temple of God and the Ark, the animate Bridal Chamber and the Heavenly Gate, do we, the faithful, proclaim thee, O Mother of God.

The Destroyer of idols, as God, Thy Offspring, O Mary, Bride of God, is adored together with the Father and the Spirit.

*Another Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior:*
As a precious, elect cornerstone laid in Sion, the Saints having found Thee, O Master, an unshakable foundation, built themselves upon Thee as elect stones. *(Twice)*[^2774] From Thy pierced Rib, drops of Blood trickled down with deifying water, renewing the world, and calling the divine assembly of All Saints unto the Benefactor. *(Twice)*[^2775]

*Glory...*
We piously hymn the divine cloud of the Martyrs, enlightened by grace, and having shone forth more brightly than the purple of blood and the scarlet of their steadfast suffering.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
We all know thee to be the true Mother of God, through whom the nature of women, being delivered, O All-Blameless One, suffered first for Christ, and was piously filled with every virtue.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
As the first-fruits of nature, the universe offers unto Thee, O Lord, the Planter of creation, the God-bearing Martyrs; by their supplications, through the Mother of God, preserve Thy Church, Thy commonwealth, in profound peace, O Most-Merciful One.[^2776]

*Ikos:*
Those who suffered across the whole earth, and have migrated into heaven, who imitated the Passions of Christ, and take away our passions, gather here today, displaying the Church of the firstborn, as possessing the image of the heavenly realm, and crying out unto Christ: Thou art my God; through the Mother of God preserve me, O Most-Merciful One.

*And the reading of the Prologue:*
*Synaxarion on the Sunday of All Saints:*
*Verses: All the friends of my Lord do I hymn:*
*If any man desires, let him go unto all.*

On this same day, on the first Sunday after Pentecost, we celebrate the feast of All Saints throughout the whole universe: in Asia, Libya, Syria, both north and south; which our divine Fathers instituted to celebrate after the Descent of the All-Holy Spirit, as it were showing a certain image, that the coming of the All-Holy Spirit worked such things, sanctifying and making wise those taken from our human mixture, to replenish that fallen Angelic rank, establishing them and bringing them unto God through Christ, some indeed through martyrdom and blood, and others through virtuous life and dwelling, which things He works above nature.[^2777] For the Holy Spirit descends in the form of fire, having by nature an upward tendency; and He leads dust upward unto the heavenly heights, which by nature is prone downward, and our human mixture, which was formerly assumed and deified by God the Word, was lifted up and seated at the right hand of the Father; and now He draws all who desire, according to the promise, as God the Word showed the reconciliation of evils unto those who have shown forth, and a certain most manifest end of His coming in the flesh unto us and His dispensation, as anciently the people from the nations who knew not God come into divine union and friendship, human nature offering as it were certain first-fruits unto God, who were variously proven good in His sight. According to one manner, then, we celebrate the feast of All Saints thus. But according to the second: because many indeed pleased God by reason of extreme virtue, and otherwise were nameless among men, or perhaps on account of certain human circumstances, yet having great glory from God. Or because many lived in Christ in India, and Egypt, and Thrace, Mesopotamia, and Phrygia, and those beyond the Euxine Sea; and furthermore throughout the whole west, even unto the British Isles, and simply to speak, in the east and the west. And it was not possible worthily to honor all these one by one on account of their numberlessness, according as the Church custom hath received: so that we may receive help from all these, wheresoever any on earth hath pleased God. And otherwise, for the sake of those who are yet to come, the divine Fathers instituted to celebrate the feast of All Saints, honoring and comprehending together all those both before and after, both manifest and unmanifest, in whomsoever the Holy Spirit dwelt and sanctified. Or thirdly, it was fitting that the Saints who are celebrated in part on each day should also be gathered into one day, that it may be made manifest that they strove for one Christ, and all ran the race of the same virtue. And so all, as servants of one God, were worthily crowned, and formed this Church, replenishing the heavenly world, and urging us also to accomplish the like struggle with them, diverse and multiform as it is, according to the strength which each possesses, striving with all diligence. For these from the ages...

Unto All the Saints, Leo the ever-memorable and most-wise Emperor built a most beautiful and excellent church, which is near the Church of the Holy Apostles, within the city of Constantine; previously indeed, as they say, building it first for Theophano his wife, who had pleased God unto the end.[^2778] And this glorious matter was discussed in their midst, and the imperial senate said: And since he appropriated his intention to the Church, he did not find it subject to his will, nor did she desire his presumption, saying: that it is not fitting for these, who yesterday and recently were corrupt with imperial fantasy and food, to be so suddenly honored, as to build for her such a magnificent and extraordinary church, and not giving her more time, even though she pleased God.[^2779] Then the most-wise Emperor, by the conciliar desire of the whole Church, dedicated the great church which he built unto All the Saints of the universe, saying: "Even if Theophano is holy, let her be numbered with All the Saints." And on this account I deem that from that time this feast received its beginning to be celebrated, though it existed also before. Therefore they placed it also after all the feasts, assembling and concluding all the feasts of the year as with an enclosure. For the ecclesiastical establishment and good order, even if it began long before, gradually arrived at the good establishment as was fitting. But by the days of this Emperor they were established unto perfection, and were configured in the order which exists today. The Triodion, then, to speak briefly, contains within itself with great care all that God wrought for our sake by ineffable words: the fall of the devil from heaven, the expulsion of Adam through the first parable, and the transgression, the whole dispensation of God the Word for our sake, and how we ascended again unto the heavens by the Holy Spirit, and replenished that fallen rank, which is known through All the Saints. And let it be known, that we celebrate all today whom the Holy Spirit hath sanctified by grace: I say, the highest and sanctifying minds, as nine ranks: Forefathers, and Patriarchs, Prophets, and holy Apostles; Martyrs, Hierarchs, Hieromartyrs, and Venerable Martyrs, Venerables, and Righteous, and all the assemblies of holy Women, and all other nameless Saints, with whom let those also be who are yet to be.[^2780] And before all, and unto all, and with all, the Holy of Holies, the All-Holy, and incomparably better than the Angelic ranks, our Lady and Sovereign, the Mother of God Mary, Ever-Virgin: by whose supplications, O Christ God, and of all Thy Saints from the ages, have mercy on us and save us, as Thou alone art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The fire of God's condescension in Babylon was formerly abashed; therefore the Children in the furnace, dancing with rejoicing foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.*[^2781]

Thy glorious emptying, the divine wealth of Thy poverty, O Christ, astonishes the Angels who behold Thee nailed upon the Cross, that Thou mightest save those who cry in faith: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

By Thy divine descent Thou didst fill the nethermost parts with light, and the darkness which formerly pursued was driven away; whence the prisoners from the ages arose, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Trinity:*
We proclaim Thee as Lord of all, and rightly theologize Thee as the one Father of the one Only-Begotten Son, and knowing the one upright Spirit proceeding from Thee, of the same essence and co-eternal.

*Another Heirmos:*
*From Judea:*
Thou hast wrought salvation in the midst of the earth, O God, as was prophesied; for being lifted up upon the Tree, Thou didst call all who cry in faith: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Risen from the tomb as from sleep, O Compassionate One, Thou hast delivered all from corruption; and creation is confirmed by the Apostles who proclaim the Rising: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*The same Heirmos:*
Incarnate of a virginal womb, Thou didst appear for our salvation; therefore knowing Thy Mother as the Mother of God, we cry in thanksgiving: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

Thou didst put forth a rod from the root of Jesse, O All-Blessed Virgin, bearing a saving blossoming Fruit for those who cry in faith unto Thy Son: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Another Heirmos:*
*The fire of God's condescension:*

The ranks of the Saints, unceasingly hymning Him Who rests in the Saints, now enjoy divine sweetness, and rejoicing they dance, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^2782]

Shining honorably with the divine radiance of the three-sun light-shedding, the assemblies of the Saints, we theologize the Trinity in three Persons: the unoriginate Father, and piously the Son, and the Holy Spirit.

In Thine ineffable glory, when Thou shalt appear, O God, in the midst of gods, rendering what is worthy, and distributing gifts, and giving crowns, then, O Savior, vouchsafe us to sing unto Thee: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Glory...*
Rejoicing, let us hymn the assembly of All the Saints gathered from everywhere, both races together, and dignities, and ways of life, and with them let us sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Mary, all-pure and most holy, Birth-giver of God, all ye maidens, having together formed a divine choir, cry out rejoicing with a loud voice: Rejoice, thou who gushest forth the fountain of joy unto us!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Chaldean tyrant furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the pious; but having seen them saved by a better power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages.*[^2783]

The divine and most-divine power of Jesus hath divinely shone forth in us; for having tasted of the death of the Cross in the flesh for all, He destroyed the power of Hades: Whom unceasingly, ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

The bones were scattered, the boastful one fell, he that had fallen and was broken is set upright, corruption was cast out, and incorruption flourished; for mortality was swallowed up by life: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Trinity:*
The Thrice-Radiant Godhead, shining in one ray from one three-hypostatic nature: the unoriginate Begetter, the Word co-essential with the Father, and the co-reigning Spirit of one essence, ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages![^2784]

*Another Heirmos:*
*Conquerors of the tyrant:*
Unto Him Who stretched forth His hands unto me upon the Tree, calling me in my nakedness, that He might warm my nakedness with His beauteous form, bless all ye works of the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto the ages!

Unto Him Who raised me up when fallen from nethermost Hades, and honored me with the high-throned glory of the Father, bless all ye works of the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto the ages!

*Another Heirmos:*
*The Heavenly King:*
Quench the ignited and flaming arrows of the adversaries against us, that we may hymn thee, O Pure One, unto all ages.[^2785]

Above nature hast thou given birth unto the Creator and Savior, God the Word, O Virgin: therefore we hymn thee unto all ages.

*Another Heirmos:*
*The furnace sevenfold:*

Rejoice, ye Martyrs, Prophets, Apostles, the venerable assembly of Hieromartyrs; the divine choir of the Righteous, the Venerables, and Teachers, sweetly singing with the Myrrh-bearing women: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages. *(Twice)*

Saints, who are filled with the brightness surpassing the mind, and filled with divine gladness and joy, ye become gods by drawing near unto God, partaking of deifying radiances, and enlightened by the beams of ineffable glory, supreme exalt Christ unto all ages. *(Twice)*[^2786][^2787]

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*

Having appeared, O Saints, as stars of multifold light, ye adorn the firmament of the Church with diverse gifts and diverse beauties, in righteousness, chastity, courage, and understanding: ye priests, hymn the Master; ye people, supreme exalt Him unto all ages!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

With gladness all the women who were divinely wise in Christ and finished their course, hymn through the Spirit the most pure Virgin, the true Mother of God, who delivered us from the curse of our foremother Eve, reverently standing before her: Hymn her Fruit unto the ages!

*We praise, we bless, we worship the Lord, hymning and supreme exalting Him unto all ages.*

*Heirmos:*
*The Child-bearing of the Mother of God saved the pious Children in the furnace, then indeed prefigured, but now realized, it stirs up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the works of the Lord, and supreme exalt Him unto all ages.*

*Then we sing: More honorable than the Cherubim.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Heaven was dismayed thereat, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens: therefore the Mother of God, the chief ranks of Angels and of men do magnify.*[^2788]

Being simple in Thy divine and unoriginate nature, Thou wast compounded by the assumption of flesh, having constituted it in bodily form, O Word of God; and having suffered as man, Thou didst remain without passions as God: therefore in two natures, undivided and unconfused, we magnify Thee.[^2789]

The Father according to divine essence, as having become man by nature, Thou didst call God, O Most High, condescending unto servants; risen from the tomb, by grace Thou didst make the Father of the earth-born Him Who by nature is both God and Master: with whom we all magnify Thee.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst appear, O Virgin Mother of God, beyond nature, having given birth in the flesh unto God the Word, Whom the Father brought forth from His heart before all ages, as He is good; Whom now we understand to be higher than bodies, even though He was clothed in a body.

*Another Heirmos:*
*Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from a Virgin's womb: therefore the faithful magnify the most pure Mother of God.*

We know Thee by nature as the Son of God, conceived in the womb of the Mother of God, and having become man for our sake; and beholding Thee upon the Cross suffering in human nature, but remaining impassible as God, we magnify Thee.[^2790]

The gloomy darkness was dispelled, for from Hades Christ the Sun of righteousness shone forth, enlightening all the ends of the earth, shining with the light of the Godhead, the new Man, an earthly God, Whom in two natures we magnify.

*Another Heirmos:*
*Truly the Mother of God:*
Full of joy and gladness is thy memory, pouring forth healings unto those who draw near, and who piously proclaim thee to be the Mother of God.

With psalms do we hymn thee, O Full of Grace, and unceasingly do we offer "Rejoice": for thou hast poured forth joy unto all.

*Another Heirmos:*
*Heaven was dismayed thereat:*

Armed with hope and love, and walled round with faith, the assembly of the elect rejoicingly endured all the threats of tyrants, torments, and wounds, being gloriously enriched by Christ, Who grants victory in sufferings, the first martyrs of truth.

The haven of salvation, the Baptist, the Apostles, Prophets, and Martyrs, with the Ascetics, the divine Teachers, and Priests, the gathering of Patriarchs, and the glorious Hieromartyrs, the God-loving Women, the Venerables, and the Righteous, let us now worthily praise. *(Twice)*

*Glory...*
Tried by the fire of temptations, and not seduced by pleasures, ye rejoice purely, standing before the radiant Throne of the Master, O glorious assemblies of the Saints in the heavens, the mirrors being dissolved, the shadow having passed away, in true manifestations.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Thou hast appeared as the uniter of those that were separated, for through thee men in truth became fellow-citizens with Angels in the heavens; and the ranks of All the Saints bear witness, now singing with them of thy Offspring, O Virgin Mother of God, in eternal hymns.

*Katavasia:*
*Let every earth-born mortal leap for joy, enlightened in spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless Minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let them cry: Rejoice, O all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin!*[^2791]

*Holy is the Lord our God. We sing according to the Tone:*

*Then the Resurrectional Exapostilarion:*
Let us go up with the disciples into the mountain of Galilee, in faith to behold Christ, speaking that He hath received authority over the heights and the depths; let us learn how He teaches to baptize all nations in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, and to abide with the initiates of the mysteries, as He promised, unto the consummation of the age.[^2792]

*Glory...*

*Exapostilarion of All Saints, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*[^2793][^2794]
The Baptist and Forerunner, the Apostles, Prophets, Martyrs, Hierarchs, Ascetics, and Venerable ones, Hieromartyrs, together with God-loving Women, and all the Righteous, and the ranks of Angels, let us duly bless in hymns, praying to obtain their glory from Christ the Savior.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
He Who is glorified on high divinely by the Angels, without departing from the Father's bosom, O Most Pure One, lived ineffably with those below; and thou wast the mediatress of His salvation, having lent flesh beyond speech unto Him from thy pure blood, O Pure One: Whom do thou beseech to grant remission of transgressions unto thy servants.

*At the Praises, let every breath... We set 8 stichera, and sing Resurrectional stichera.*

*Tone 8:*
O Lord, although Thou didst stand before the judgment seat, judged by Pilate, yet Thou didst not withdraw from Thy throne, sitting together with the Father; and having risen from the dead, Thou hast freed the world from the servitude of the stranger, as Thou art compassionate and the Lover of mankind.

O Lord, although the Judeans laid Thee in the tomb as dead, yet the soldiers watched Thee as a sleeping King, and as a treasure of life they sealed Thee with a seal: but Thou didst rise, and didst grant incorruption unto our souls.

O Lord, a weapon against the devil hast Thou given unto us, Thy Cross: for he trembles and shakes, not enduring to look upon its power, for it raises the dead, and hath destroyed death: on this account we worship Thy Burial and Thy Rising.

Thine Angel, O Lord, having proclaimed the Resurrection, struck terror into the guards, and cried unto the women, saying: Why seek ye the Living among the dead? Risen is He Who is God, and He hath granted life unto the universe.

*Another Eastern sticheron, of the same Tone:*
Thou didst suffer through the Cross, being impassible in Thy Godhead; Thou didst accept a three-day Burial, that Thou mightest free us from the servitude of the enemy, and having made us immortal, mightest quicken us, O Christ God, by Thy Resurrection, O Lover of mankind.

*Other stichera of All Saints.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^2795]
The Lord hath made marvelous the Saints who are upon the earth: for they received His wounds and sufferings in the flesh, glorying in these, and manifestly adorned with divine adornments: whom let us hymn as unfading blossoms, as unerring stars of the Church, as voluntary whole-burnt offerings.

*Verse: The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them [LXX Ps. 33:17].*
With the Prophets, let the Apostles; with the Venerable ones, the Teachers; with the Hieromartyrs, all the Righteous, and the multitude of holy Women who suffered and fasted in love, and the ranks of the righteous, be praised in sacred hymns, as heirs of the heavenly Kingdom, as dwellers of Paradise!

*Verse: Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:35].*

Those who heavenized the earth with the brightness of virtues, who imitated the death of Christ, who walked the path that mediats immortality, who cleansed the passions of men by the operation of grace, who throughout the whole world valiantly contended with one soul, let the Martyrs be praised![^2796]

*Glory...*
*First Morning Gospel Sticheron:*
*Tone 1:*
Unto the disciples going into the mountain for the earthly Ascension, the Lord presented Himself; and having worshiped Him, and having learned of the authority given everywhere, they are sent forth beneath heaven to proclaim the Resurrection from the dead and the Ascension into heaven: with whom Christ, the God and Savior of our souls, promised without deceit to abide unto the ages.[^2797]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Most blessed art thou, O Mother of God, Virgin:*

*The Great Doxology.*
*After the Trisagion, the Resurrectional troparion only, litanies. And dismissal, and the First Hour in the narthex.*
*And the Catechesis of the Studite. And the complete dismissal.*

## SUNDAY OF ALL SAINTS: AT THE DIVINE LITURGY
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone, on 4.*[^2798]
*Tone 8:*
Remember us, O Christ, Savior of the world, as Thou didst remember the thief upon the Tree, and vouchsafe all, O only Compassionate One, Thy heavenly Kingdom.

Nailed upon the Tree of Thy will, O our Savior, by the Tree Thou didst deliver Adam from the curse, restoring, as Thou art compassionate, that which was after Thine image, and the dwelling of Paradise.

Give ear, Adam, and rejoice with Eve, for he that anciently stripped you both, and took you all captive through deception, hath been brought to naught by the Cross of Christ.

Today Christ is risen from the tomb, granting incorruption unto all the faithful, and He renews joy unto the Myrrh-bearing women after His Passion and Rising.

*And from the Canon of All Saints, Ode 6, on 4.*

*Prokeimenon of the Apostle, Tone 8:*
*Pray ye, and render unto the Lord our God [LXX Ps. 75:11].*
*Verse: In Judah is God known; in Israel His name is great [LXX Ps. 75:1].*
*And of the Saints, Tone 4:*
*Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel [LXX Ps. 67:35].*

*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 330 [Heb. 11:33–12:2]: All Saints:*[^2799]
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*
*The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them [LXX Ps. 33:17].*

*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous, but out of them all the Lord will deliver them [LXX Ps. 33:19].*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 38 [Mt. 10:32–33, 37–38; 19:27–30]: "Every one therefore who shall confess Me before men..."*
*Communion Hymn: Praise the Lord from the heavens [LXX Ps. 148:1]. Another: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous [LXX Ps. 32:1].*
*At the table, consolation for the brethren, preparing themselves for the ascetic struggle of the fast.*[^2800][^2801]

*It is fitting to know: If the temple is dedicated to All Saints, at Small and Great Vespers, and at Matins, and at the Liturgy, we sing similarly, as if it should occur on the Sunday of a great saint for whom a vigil is kept.*[^2802]
*Only at Matins we sing the Polyeleos.*
*After the Polyeleos:*
*We magnify you, O Apostles, Martyrs, Prophets, and all Saints, and we honor your holy memory: for ye pray for us unto Christ our God.*
*Selected Psalm: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord [LXX Ps. 111:1].*
*And the Sessional Hymn of the Saints written in the Canon, after Ode 3.*
*The Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi] of the Tone.*
*The Prokeimenon, Let every breath..., and the Gospel of the Saints.*
*Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ... and Psalm 50.*

*And the rest, as was previously written.*

## OFFICE OF THE FEAST OF THE MOST HOLY MYSTERY OF THE EUCHARIST
*Celebrated on Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints.*[^2805]

*On Wednesday evening, at "Lord, I have cried", we set 8 stichera.*

*Tone 6:*
Revealing the great mystery of Thy Dispensation in the flesh, while reclining at the Supper with the initiates of Thy mysteries, O Lover of mankind, Thou didst say: Eat ye the Bread of life, this is My Body; and drink ye the Blood of incorruptible life. *(Twice)*[^2806]

A heavenly tabernacle was shown forth in the upper chamber, where Christ accomplished the Passover, and the unbloody Supper, and the spiritual service: and the table of the perfected Mysteries there present, a noetic altar of sacrifice. *(Twice)*

Having blessed the bread, being Himself the Heavenly Bread, having given thanks unto the Father Who begat Him; having taken also the cup, He gave it unto the Disciples, crying: Take, eat, this is My Body; and drink ye the Blood of incorruptible life. *(Twice)*

The Lord nourished His Disciples with heavenly food; before His Passion He gave them Angelic Bread, marvelous beyond manna: Your fathers, (saith the Lord) did eat manna in the wilderness, and died; this is the Bread which comes down from heaven, that if any man eat thereof, he shall not die, but shall live for ever: of which do Thou now vouchsafe us also to partake, for the sake of the supplications of Thy Mother of God. *(Twice)*[^2807]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*
Come, ye faithful lovers of the feasts, let us clap our hands today at the salvation-bearing feast for the world, of the Bread of life and the Lamb of God, Who takes away the sins of the world and our transgressions, not in the old leaven of malice...[^2808]

...of malice, but in the unleavened bread of purity and truth; having purified ourselves, therefore, let us worthily partake of Him, that we may obtain His eternal Kingdom.[^2809]

*Entrance.*
*Prokeimenon of the day. Then the Readings.*
*Reading from Exodus [Exodus 19:10–19]:*[^2810]
The Lord said unto Moses: Go down, testify unto this people, and purify them today and tomorrow; and let them wash their garments. And let them be ready against the third day; for on the third day the Lord will come down upon Mount Sinai before all the people. And thou shalt set bounds for the people round about, saying: Take heed to yourselves, that ye go not up into the mountain, nor touch anything thereof. Whoever touches the mountain shall surely die. No hand shall touch it, for he shall be stoned with stones, or shot through with a dart; whether it be man or beast, he shall not live. When the voices and the trumpets and the cloud depart from the mountain, they shall go up into the mountain. And Moses went down from the mountain unto the people, and sanctified them, and they washed their garments. And he said unto the people: Be ready; for three days come not near a woman. And it came to pass on the third day, when morning came, there were voices and lightnings, and dark clouds upon Mount Sinai, and the voice of a trumpet sounding loudly; and all the people that were in the camp were afraid. And Moses led the people forth out of the camp to meet God, and they stood before Mount Sinai. And Mount Sinai was altogether on a smoke, because God descended upon it in fire. And the smoke went up as the smoke of a furnace, and all the people were exceedingly amazed. And the voices of the trumpet sounded exceedingly strong. Moses spoke, and God answered him by a voice.

*Reading from the First Book of Kingdoms [1 Kingdoms / 1 Sam. 21:1–6]:*[^2811]
In those days, David came into the church unto Ahimelech the priest, and Ahimelech was amazed at his coming. And he said unto him: Why art thou thus alone, and there is no man with thee? And David said unto the priest: The king commanded me a matter today, and said unto me: Let no man know the matter for which I send thee, and concerning which I have charged thee. And I have left the young men in the place called Elmaun. And now, if there be under thy hand five loaves of bread, give me whatsoever thou hast. And the priest answered David, and said: There is no common bread under my hand, but only holy bread is under my hand, kept in the body, of those who are clean from women; and these may eat them. And David answered the priest, and said: Truly we have kept ourselves from women yesterday and the third day; since I came forth on the way, all the young men were purified, and though this way is unclean, yet it will be sanctified today for the sake of my vessels. And Ahimelech the priest gave him the Showbread, for there was no other bread there; for those were the only loaves of presentation that had been taken from before the face of the Lord, and he gave them unto David on that day.

*Reading from the Third Book of Kingdoms [3 Kingdoms / 1 Kings 17:1–9]:*[^2812]
The word of the Lord came unto Elijah, saying: Depart hence toward the east, and hide thyself by the brook Cherith, that is before the face of the Jordan. And it shall be that thou shalt drink water of the brook; and I have commanded the ravens to feed thee there. And Elijah did according to the word of the Lord, and sat down by the brook Cherith, before the face of the Jordan. And the ravens brought him bread in the morning, and flesh in the evening; and he drank water of the brook. And it came to pass after a while that the brook dried up, because there was no rain upon the earth. And the word of the Lord came unto Elijah, saying: Arise, and go to Zarephath of Sidon, and abide there; for behold, I have commanded a widow woman there to sustain thee. And he arose...

...and went to Zarephath to the gate of the city; and behold, a widow woman was there gathering sticks; and Elijah called unto her from behind, and said unto her: Bring me now a little water in a vessel, that I may drink. And as she went to take it, Elijah cried after her, and said: Bring me now also a morsel of bread in thine hand. And the woman said: As the Lord my God lives, if there is anything in my storehouse, but only a handful of meal in a jar, and a little oil in a cruse: and behold, I am gathering two sticks, that I may go in and prepare it for me and my children, that we may eat it, and die. And Elijah said unto her: Fear not, go in and do according to thy word; but make me thereof a little unleavened cake first, and bring it unto me; and afterward make for thee and thy children. For thus saith the Lord God of Israel: The jar of meal shall not fail, and the cruse of oil shall not diminish, until the day that the Lord God gives rain upon the face of all the earth. And the woman went and did according to the word of Elijah, and gave unto him, and ate herself, and her children. And from that day the jar of meal failed not, and the cruse of oil did not diminish, according to the word of the Lord, which He spoke by the hand of Elijah.[^2813]

*At the Litiya, stichera.*

*Tone 6:*
Before the ark of the shadow, David the Ancestor of God leaped and danced; but we today worship Him as God, Whose humanity the ark prefigured; for instead of the manna, the Godhead is incarnate; instead of the tables of stone, the Lawgiver Christ the Lord is seen hidden, saving our souls.[^2814]

The Old Testament had an ark overlaid on every side with gold; which, being captured by foreigners, endured not the coexistence with the idol in the temple of false gods, but cast it down to the earth, and shattered its head and both palms of its hands: but Thou, O Christ our God, Lover of mankind, how wilt Thou enter into the temple of the defiled soul of us wretched ones?[^2815]

O Thou Who alone hast immortality, and dwellest in unapproachable light, how wilt Thou unite Thyself with the idol of the darkness of sin? But O Light that enlightenest every man, shatter not, neither cast down into outer darkness the dark and sinful souls of us, but enlighten them by Thy grace, we pray Thee, O Abyss of compassion, O supernatural Bread![^2816]

O innocent Lamb that takest away the sins of the whole world, for the sake of man's salvation Thou didst change bread into Thy Body; and in the bread Thou didst conceal Godhead, Body, and Soul by divine power: unto Whom all we faithful today festively bow down with compunction and faith.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 2:*
Unto the Mystical Supper today, and the Divine Table, we faithful draw near with fear, desiring to taste of the immortal Spring; but trembling and fear seize us because of our many sins, from which we desire to be cleansed by Thee, through the prayers of the Sovereign Lady, our Queen.

*At the Aposticha, stichera.*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Let us hymn the heavenly Bread of life, descending unceasingly and ineffably from heaven, saying with one accord: Rejoice, O Body of Christ, whereby we all are delivered; and Divine and Holy Blood, whereby we are redeemed!

*Verse: Man ate the bread of angels; He sent them food to the full [LXX Ps. 77:25].*[^2817]
At the Supper Thou didst give Thyself unto Thy disciples and friends for food, (O wonder!) and: Eat bread, for this is My Body; and having taken the cup, Thou didst say: Drink ye all of My Blood.

*Verse: And He rained down manna upon them to eat, and gave them the Bread of heaven [LXX Ps. 77:24].*
O Divine, all-honorable, and most precious Body of the incarnate Word, save Thy servants from many afflictions and temptations, and from all tribulations, granting eternal Kingdom and life, for the sake of Thy mercy!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 8:*
O marvelous wonder! The Living and Divine Bread that came down from heaven is willingly given for food at the mystical evening; for having taken bread and blessed it, He gave it, saying unto the disciples: Take, eat, this is My Body; likewise also the cup; therefore let each examine himself, and so let him eat and drink, unto salvation and into life eternal.[^2818]

*Then:*
*Now dismissest Thou: Trisagion through Our Father:*
*Troparion, Tone 7:*[^2819]
The supernatural Bread, which Angels and Archangels desire to behold with trembling, today is seen festively upon the altar, and is given unto men for food: beholding Thy most rich mercy toward it, O Christ our God, we confess Thee as God with flesh hidden by the appearance of the species, saying: Vouchsafe us to be partakers, that we may also delight in life eternal.

*And the Dismissal.*
**Priest:** He Who for the sake of His exceeding great mercy, at His Mystical Supper, gave His own Flesh to eat and His true Blood to drink for our salvation, Christ our true God, through the intercessions of His all-pure Mother, of the holy twelve Apostles, and of all the Saints, have mercy upon us and save us, as He is good and the Lover of mankind.[^2820]

## FEAST OF THE MOST HOLY EUCHARIST: AT MATINS
*At "God is the Lord", troparion: "The supernatural Bread...", thrice.*
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn.*
*Tone 7:*
Christ, being our great and all-honorable Passover, was eaten as bread; and having been slain as a lamb, He was offered for us as an acceptable sacrifice unto God: Whose all-pure Body and precious Blood we piously worship, crying with compunction: Do Thou Thyself abide ever with us, and none shall prevail against us!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, Ever-Virgin Mary, behold the Flesh of Thy Son, Christ our God, which having taken from thy womb through the operation of the All-Holy Spirit, He gave unto His disciples at the Mystical Supper, saying: Take, eat, this is My Body; and drink, this is My Blood: Whom do thou beseech with maternal boldness, that He consume not us who partake, but save our souls.[^2821]

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^2822]
Having taken the heavenly bread and the cup, Christ blessed them, showing them unto the Father Who begat Him; and giving them unto the wondering disciples, He said: He that eats My Flesh and drinks My Blood hath eternal life, and I will raise him up at the last day: on this account we, unworthy though we be, today festively worship the all-pure Mysteries of Thy Flesh and Blood, boldly believing that Thou art Christ, the Son of the living God, Who camest into the world to save sinners.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Without seed, from the Holy Spirit thou didst conceive, and didst bear the Son, Christ our God: Whose most pure Flesh, as our God and Redeemer, we worship prostrate to the face of the earth, desiring to become partakers; and unto thee, as Mother, we flee, O all-pure Mother of God, and speaking with compunction, we pray thee: Receive our prayers, and bear them unto Thy Son and our God, that He may save our souls.

*Polyeleos, and*
*Megalynarion:*[^2823]
We magnify Thee, O Christ Giver of life, Who for our sake didst change bread into Thy Body and wine into Thy Blood: and these we today worship as God incarnate.

*Verse 1: Great is the Lord, and greatly to be praised, in the city of our God, in His holy mountain [LXX Ps. 47:2].*
*Verse 2: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me? [LXX Ps. 115:3].*
*Verse 3: I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord [LXX Ps. 115:4].*
*Verse 4: He hath made a remembrance of His wonders; merciful is the Lord and full of compassion; He hath given food unto them that fear Him [LXX Ps. 110:4–5].*
*Verse 5: And He rained down manna upon them to eat, and gave them the Bread of heaven [LXX Ps. 77:24].*
*Verse 6: Man ate the bread of angels; He sent them food to the full [LXX Ps. 77:25].*

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Alleluia, thrice.*
*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "The beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^2824]
O eternal and unoriginate God, having assumed human nature, Thou didst come into the prepared upper chamber with Thy disciples; where, having completed the old Passover, O our Savior, Thou didst institute the new mystical Passover: for having taken bread, Thou didst change it into Thy Body, and the cup into Blood, and didst give them unto us for food and drink: beholding this Divine Mystery today, we say with compunction: Glory to Thy dispensation, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*
*1st Antiphon of the Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], Tone 4.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*[^2825]
*He that eats My Flesh and drinks My Blood hath eternal life, and I will raise him up at the last day [Jn. 6:54].*
*Verse: Man ate the bread of angels; He sent them food to the full [LXX Ps. 77:25].*
*Let every breath...*
*Gospel according to John, Pericope 24 [Jn. 6:55–58]:*[^2826]
The Lord said unto the Judeans who came unto Him: My Flesh is food indeed, and My Blood is drink indeed. He that eats My Flesh and drinks My Blood abides in Me, and I in him. As the living Father hath sent Me, and I live because of the Father; so he that eats Me, even he shall live because of Me. This is the Bread that came down from heaven; not as your fathers did eat manna, and died; he that eats this Bread shall live for ever.

*Then, Psalm 50. And the Sticheron.*
*Tone 6:*[^2827]
Today the Angels are filled with awe, beholding Thee, the incarnate God, concealed within the Mysteries, worshiped with fear by men, and given for food: with them we also, mortals of the earth, hymn Thee, the Sovereign Lover of mankind, saying: Vouchsafe us to partake of Thy most pure Body and Blood, O compassionate Lord!

*CANON*
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^2828]
Come, O ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought out of Egyptian bondage, for He hath been glorified!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
May Thy holy Body, O compassionate Lord, and Thy precious Blood be unto me the Bread of everlasting life, and the healing of manifold diseases!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
Vouchsafe me, unworthy though I be, O Master, to receive Thy most pure Blood and Thy most holy Body: to eat and drink them with desire and faith!

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Defiled by improper deeds, wretched that I am, I am unworthy of the communion of Thy most pure Body and Divine Blood, O Christ: yet do Thou vouchsafe me this!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
O good earth, blessed Bride of God, who didst sprout the unplowed and saving Ear of grain for the world: vouchsafe me who eat this to be saved!

*Katavasia:*
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter speech unto the Queen and Mother; and I shall be seen radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^2829]
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou didst open wide my mouth against my enemies; for my spirit rejoiced in chanting: There is none holy as our God, and there is none righteous beside Thee, O Lord!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
Grant me drops of tears, O Christ, that cleanse the defilement of my heart; so that, purified with a good conscience, in faith and fear I may draw near, O Master, to the communion of Thy Divine Gifts!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
May Thy most pure Body and Divine Blood be unto me for the remission of transgressions, and for the communion of the Holy Spirit, and for life eternal, O Lover of mankind, and for the estrangement from passions and griefs!

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Vouchsafe me, O Lord, to partake without condemnation of Thy Body and of Thy precious Blood, and to glorify Thy goodness!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
O most holy Table of the Bread of life, Who for mercy's sake came down from on high and gives new life unto the world: vouchsafe me also now, unworthy though I be, to taste thereof with fear, and to be alive!

*Katavasia:*
O Mother of God, living and bountiful Fount, do thou spiritually establish the choir gathered in unity, who hymn thee; and in thy divine glory, vouchsafe them crowns of glory!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1, Podoben: "The stone being sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*[^2830]
Eating the lamb with Thy disciples, O Master, according to the commandment of Moses, Thou didst give Thyself, the spotless Lamb, mystically unto us for food, for the remission of sins, and for the sanctification of souls: nourished by which, we pray: Let not the communion of Thy Holy Mysteries be unto us for judgment or condemnation, because of our sins and iniquities!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
O Virgin Mother of God, entreat the Lamb and Shepherd, the Son Whom thou didst bear, that He may save and enlighten our souls!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^2831]
Thou didst come from the Virgin, neither an envoy nor an angel, but Thou Thyself incarnate, O Lord, and hast saved me, the whole man: therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
Having become incarnate for our sake, O Most Merciful One, Thou didst desire to be slain as a sheep for the sins of men: therefore I pray Thee, cleanse Thou also my transgressions!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
Heal the wounds of my soul, O Lord, and sanctify me wholly: and vouchsafe me, wretched that I am, O Master, that I may be a partaker of Thy mystical Divine Supper!

*Glory...*[^2832]

*Troparion:*
Standing and groaning with fear and trembling, I lift up the eyes of my heart on high unto Thee, O Savior, Who didst stretch out Thine open hands upon the Cross: with them grant me the food of Thy good pleasure, confirming me and strengthening me in Thy fear.[^2833]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Make Thy compassionate Son merciful unto me, O Sovereign Lady, and preserve me, thy servant, without spot and without blemish, so that, having received the noetic pearl within, I may be sanctified.

*Katavasia:*
He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine, hath come upon a light cloud, and with His incorrupt hand hath saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^2834]
O Illumination of them that lie in darkness, Salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior, unto Thee I rise early, O King of peace: enlighten me with Thy brightness, for beside Thee I know no other God!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
Even as Thou didst say beforehand, O Christ, let it be unto Thy humble servant, and abide in me, as Thou didst promise: for behold, I eat Thy Divine Body, and drink Thy Blood.

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
O Word of God and God, may Thy Divine Body be a burning coal unto me for illumination who am darkened; and may Thy Blood be for cleansing unto my defiled soul!

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Having an utterly defiled soul and unclean lips, I dare not now draw near unto Thee, O Christ, and receive Thy Body: but do Thou show me worthy!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
O Mary, Mother of God, pure dwelling of sweet fragrance, by thy prayers make me a chosen vessel, that I may partake of the Holy Mysteries of Thy Son!

*Katavasia:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God over all, and didst give birth to the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who hymn thee.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^2835]
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: bring me up from corruption, O God!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
Sanctify my mind, soul, heart, and body, O Savior, and vouchsafe me, O Master, to approach Thy awesome Mysteries without condemnation!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
May I be estranged from passions, and vouchsafed to receive the increase of Thy grace, and the confirmation of life, for the sake of communion of Thy Holy Mysteries, O Christ!

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
Grant me to approach Thy Divine Mysteries with fear and trembling, O Christ: and to receive Thy true and holy Body, and Thy true, holy, and precious Blood![^2836]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*
O God, holy Word of God, sanctify me wholly who now draw near unto Thy Divine Mysteries, by the entreaties of Thy holy Mother.[^2837]

*Katavasia:*
Celebrating this divine and all-honorable festival of the Mother of God, come, ye God-wise ones, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^2838]
Behold, Christ is set forth as food for all: come, and falling down let us worship Christ God, Who is concealed by these Mysteries, crying out with compunction: O Master, consume not us who are unworthy by our partaking: but do Thou burn away sins, and purify our souls!

*Ikos:*
Drawing near unto the Mystical Table with fear, all we, receiving the Divine Bread with pure souls, hoping for salvation, let us cleanse the depths of our conscience, that we may also delight in life eternal, through the prayers of the Mother of God!

*Synaxarion for Holy Thursday of the Eucharist:*[^2839]
On this Thursday, which is also the sixth Thursday after Pascha, and the first Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints, is named the greatest, most honorable, and surpassing all the Mysteries of the Catholic faith, the most perfect Mystery of the Eucharist, the most radiant feast which we have received from the Roman Church to celebrate in a God-pleasing and devout manner, by the local Council held in the city of Zamość. This feast was instituted in the beginning at Rome by the most holy Ecumenical Patriarch Urban, the fourth Pope of Rome of that name, in the year 1264, principally for this reason: because in those times certain heresies arose, impiously blaspheming this Mystery. For the confounding (saith Pope Urban) principally of the malicious faith and folly of the heretics, we have judged it worthy and righteous that we should keep a most solemn remembrance of this Mystery of the Eucharist; for although even before this, on the day of the Mystical Supper of the Lord, on which Christ Himself instituted this Mystery and delivered it unto the disciples, we make commemoration of it: yet because at that time the whole Church throughout the world was occupied with the reconciliation of penitents, the confection of the holy great chrism, the fulfillment of the commandment of Christ concerning the washing of feet, and very many other matters, it was not possible to establish a complete and perfect solemnity concerning this great Mystery: on which account expressly for that purpose the day which is the fifth after the Apodosis of the Feast of Pentecost, that is to say Thursday, was determined and decreed by the aforementioned most holy Pope, for the honorable veneration of Christ the Lord Himself in this Mystery by all men, and for the overthrow (as was said) of the shameless heretical boldness and blasphemy. And this (besides still other very many reasons) was the primary cause for the institution of this Feast from the beginning in the Roman Church. And in our Church, since the folly of the same heretics was multiplying in many places, and moreover lest the knowledge of this Mystery should utterly diminish among the simpler people, but rather through accurate inquiry into the matter, as to what it is, should be rooted in their souls and hearts, the Fathers of the aforementioned local Council wisely and expediently judged that this Feast should be received from the Roman Church.

For what? Is not that judged to be a worthy reason for this matter, unto which even John Chrysostom stirred up his hearers in his Liturgies at that time, saying: It is necessary, beloved, to understand the miracle of the Mysteries, what it is, and for what purpose it was given, and what profit comes of this matter; and he was not content with this, but added again, saying what this miracle is, and for what cause it was given, and what profit the matter hath: One body (he saith) we become, members of His Flesh and of His bones, (which is of Christ through the Mysteries), so that not only in love may we be this, but in very deed we partake of this Flesh: and this comes about through the food which He delivered unto us, wishing to show the longing which He hath toward us. And again he saith in the person of Christ: Parents often deliver their own children unto others to be nursed; but I (saith He) do not so, but I feed you with Mine own Flesh, and I offer Myself. Desiring to be your Brother, for your sake I partook of flesh and blood: and in turn I deliver unto you that same Flesh and Blood, whereby I became kin unto you. O wonders of the mystery! O profit of the matter! According to the word of Chrysostom, is it not worthy that such a benefaction of Christ the Lord should be honored by us with a most radiant Feast? Yea, it is worthy: and moreover carefully in every way, so that such His love toward us may be known by all even unto the least, and not covered with extreme ignorance among some. Hence it is that even the holy Ecumenical Council held at Trent greatly praises such a design, saying: Most piously and devoutly was this custom introduced into the Church of God, that every year this most great and saving Mystery should be honored with the most radiant commemoration and solemnity, and should be carried around with a litiya in a God-befitting and honorable manner through public squares and distinguished places.[^2840] Moreover, it did not hesitate to deliver unto anathema those who oppose this order, and who say: that the Mystery of the Eucharist should neither be honored with its own feast, nor carried around solemnly in litany-processions according to the pious and common order and custom of the Catholic Church. Let those who have loved the curse draw this down upon themselves, sons of disobedience...

...of disobedience; but we, being children of the Catholic Church, piously and devoutly honor Christ the Lord Who is secretly present in this Mystery, so that having inherited the blessing, we may be vouchsafed to behold Him openly, face to face in heaven: unto Whom be glory unto the ages. Amen.[^2841]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^2842]
When the golden image was worshiped on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the ungodly command; cast into the midst of the fire, they sang bedewed: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
May the communion of Thine immortal Mysteries now, I pray Thee, O Christ, be unto me a fount of good things, light, life, and dispassion, mediating the progress and multiplication of most divine virtue, O only Good One: that I may glorify Thee!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
That I may be delivered from passions, enemies, necessities, and all affliction, with trembling, love, and reverence, O Lover of mankind, I now approach Thy immortal and Divine Mysteries, singing unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
O wretched soul, O passionate soul, be dismayed beholding the most glorious Mysteries; sighing, weep, smiting thy breast, crying and saying: O God, cleanse me even as the prodigal!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bear Christ the Savior beyond comprehension, O full of God's grace, I, thine unclean servant, earnestly pray thee, O Pure One: as I now desire to approach the Most Pure Mysteries, cleanse me wholly from defilement of flesh and spirit!

*Katavasia:*
The God-wise youths worshiped not the creature rather than the Creator, but valiantly trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^2843]
Disregarding the golden image, the thrice-blessed youths, beholding the unchanging and living image of God, sang in the midst of the fire: Let all the creation that hath received being hymn the Lord, and exalt Him unto all ages!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
Vouchsafe me, O God my Savior, now to be a communicant of Thy heavenly, awesome, and holy Mysteries, O Christ, and of Thy Divine and Mystical Supper!

*Refrain: Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!*
*Troparion:*
Having fled unto Thy compassion, O Good One, with fear I cry unto Thee: Abide in me, O Savior, and I, as Thou didst say, in Thee: for behold, trusting in Thy mercy, I eat Thy Body and drink Thy Blood.

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
I shudder receiving Fire, lest I be burned as wax and as grass: O the compassion of God! O the awesome mystery! How do I, who am clay, partake of the Divine Body and Blood, and am made incorruptible?[^2844]

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
The Divine Bread of life was truly baked within thy womb, O Mother of God, preserving thy womb unharmed, O Spotless One: therefore, as our nourisher, we hymn thee unto all ages!

*Katavasia:*
The Childbearing of the Mother of God saved the pious youths in the furnace: then prefigured, but now in very deed fulfilled, it moves the whole world to sing unto Thee: Hymn the works of the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!

*At Ode 9, we chant this Refrain:*[^2845]
*Magnify, O my soul, the Lord Who gave His own Flesh for mystical food.*

*Another Refrain:*
*Magnify, O my soul, Christ our God, Who gave His Flesh for the salvation of men.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^2846]
Thou art all desire, Thou art all sweetness, O Word of God, Son of the Virgin, God of gods, O Lord, Most Holy of the holy: therefore Thee with her that bore Thee do all we magnify!

*Refrain: Magnify, O my soul, the Lord Who gave His own Flesh for mystical food.*
*Troparion:*
This is Christ; taste and see that the Lord Who existed anciently, having once for all offered Himself for our sake and instead of us as a sacrifice unto His Father, is ever slain, sanctifying them that partake!

*Refrain: Magnify, O my soul, Christ our God, Who gave His Flesh for the salvation of men.*
*Troparion:*
May I be sanctified in soul and body, O Master, may I be enlightened, may I be saved, may I become Thy dwelling by the communion of the Sacred Mysteries, having Thee dwelling within myself with the Father and the Spirit, O most merciful Benefactor!

*Glory...*
*Troparion:*
May Thy Body and Thy most precious Blood, O my Savior, be unto me as fire and as light, burning up the fuel of sin and consuming the thorns of passions, and wholly enlightening me who worship Thy Godhead!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
God was incarnate from thy pure blood, on which account every generation hymns thee, O Sovereign Lady, and the noetic multitudes glorify thee: for through thee they clearly beheld Him Who reigns over all, having taken substance in human nature.

*Katavasia:*
Let every earth-born mortal leap for joy, enlightened in spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless Intelligences keep festival, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let them cry: Rejoice, O all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Exapostilarion:*[^2847]
While the disciples were sitting at the Mystical Supper, and beholding Thee, O Lord, having taken bread into Thy holy hands, having lifted up Thine eyes to heaven unto God the Father, having given thanks, blessed, and broken it, Thou didst give it unto Thy holy disciples: grant it now also unto us, Thy sinful servants!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*[^2848]
This is He, O all-pure Virgin Mother of God, Who is worshiped in these Mysteries, Whom thou, O All-Pure One, didst bear before in thy maternal womb, and afterward upon thine arms: Him do thou entreat for us, that He may save our souls!

*At the Praises, Stichera:*[^2849]
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ ди́вное чꙋ́до):*[^2850]

*Sticheron:*
O marvelous wonder! The Fount of life is beheld upon the throne, and is given unto men for food: rejoice, O divine Church, adorned with the bread of the Flesh of Christ; let us cry aloud, ye faithful, having John the Theologian as leader: The Word was made Flesh, and dwelt among us, and we beheld His glory, the glory as of the Only-begotten of the Father!

*Sticheron:*
O marvelous wonder! He Who was prefigured in the manna, the True God, born of the True God, having assumed the nature of human flesh: rejoice this day, O human race, beholding God in the flesh giving Himself unto thee; cry aloud, rejoice, and fervently giving thanks unto God: The Word was made Flesh, and dwelt among us, and we beheld His glory, the glory as of the Only-begotten of the Father!

*Sticheron:*
O marvelous wonder! Wisdom hath slain Her beasts of sacrifice, and mingled Her wine in Her cup, and prepared Her table, summoning all unto Herself: Come, eat My Bread, and drink the wine which I have mingled for you; rejoice, ye kings; give thanks, ye hierarchs: The Word was made Flesh, and dwelt among us, and we beheld His glory, the glory as of the Only-begotten of the Father![^2851]

*Sticheron:*
O marvelous wonder! He Who upon Mount Sinai spoke with Moses in the unburnt bush, and wrote the tablets of the law, Whose voice of thunder, trumpets, and lightning terrified the sons of Israel, who crossed the sea on dry land: He this day is divinely concealed in the Bread, and is given unto all for food: the Lamb, God, and Savior!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 6:*[^2852]
*Doxasticon:*
I am the living Bread, the Bread that came down from heaven, said the Lord unto the Jews who came unto Him: If ye eat not the Flesh of the Son of man, and drink His Blood, ye have no life in you; he that eats My Flesh, and drinks My Blood, hath eternal life, and I will raise him up at the last day!

*Great Doxology.*
*After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast: "The supernatural Bread:" seek yesterday at Vespers.*
*Then the Litanies, and the Dismissal.*

## AT THE DIVINE LITURGY
*At the Liturgy we sing these Antiphons:*[^2853]

*Antiphon 1:*
*Psalm 22, Tone 2.*
*Verse 1:* The Lord is my shepherd, and nothing shall I lack; in a green place, there hath He made me to dwell.
*Refrain:* By the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!
*Verse 2:* For though I walk in the midst of the shadow of death, I will fear no evils, for Thou art with me.
*Refrain:* By the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!

*Verse 3:*[^2854] Thou hast prepared a table before me, against them that afflict me.
*Refrain:* By the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!
*Verse 4:* Thou hast anointed my head with oil, and Thy cup inebriats me, how excellent it is!
*Refrain:* By the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Refrain:* By the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us!

*Antiphon 2:*[^2855]
