*Verse 1:* I believed, therefore have I spoken; but I was humbled exceedingly.
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst feed us with Thy Flesh, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia (thrice).
*Verse 2:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst feed us with Thy Flesh, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia (thrice).
*Verse 3:* I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord.
*Refrain:* Save us, O Son of God, Who didst feed us with Thy Flesh, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia (thrice).

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Only-begotten Son...*

*Antiphon 3:*
*Verse 1:* Sacrifice a sacrifice of righteousness, and put your trust in the Lord.
*Troparion:* The supernatural Bread...
*Verse 2:* By the fruit of their corn, wine, and oil have they been multiplied.
*Troparion:* The supernatural Bread...
*Verse 3:* For He hath satisfied the empty soul, and the hungry soul hath He filled with good things.
*Troparion:* The supernatural Bread...

*Entrance Verse:*[^2856]
I will sacrifice unto Thee a sacrifice of praise, and will call upon the name of the Lord.

*Troparion:* The supernatural Bread...[^2857]
*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Behold, Christ is set forth as food for all...

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* He fed them with the fat of wheat, and with honey out of the rock did He satisfy them.
*Verse:* Rejoice in God our helper, shout with joy unto the God of Jacob!
*Epistle to the Corinthians, Pericope 149:*[^2858]

*Alleluia, Tone 1:*
*Verse:* The eyes of all look hope unto Thee, and Thou givest them their food in due season.
*Verse:* Thou openest Thine hand, and fillest every living thing with Thy favor.
*Gospel from John, Pericope 23:*[^2859]

*In place of "It is truly meet", we sing with the refrain the Heirmos:* Thou art all desire...[^2860]
*Communion Verse:* He that eats My Flesh, and drinks My Blood, abides in Me, and I in him. Alleluia (thrice).[^2861]
*Dismissal: seek at Vespers yesterday.*

## ON THE SAME THURSDAY EVENING[^2862]
*Without Kathisma, if there was a Vigil.*
*At "Lord, I have cried", we set 6 verses: Stichera Idiomela.*
*Tone 2:*

*Sticheron:*
The eternal High Priest according to the order of Melchizedek, our Lord Jesus Christ, at His Mystical Supper, offered bread and wine, Himself unto the Father, for the sake of the sins of the human race: unto Him this day as True God we bow down in festival. (Twice)

*Sticheron:*
Gracious and merciful, the Lord hath given food unto them that fear Him, saying unto His Disciples: Take, eat, this is My Body; drink of the cup, all of you, this is My Blood of the New Covenant, which is poured out for you for the remission of sins. (Twice) *Sticheron (continued):*[^2863]
The Lord hath made a remembrance of His wonders, for the sake of His mercy and loving-kindness; in these Mysteries of His Body and Blood, He gave His Body for food, and Blood for drink. O great power, and works of the Lord, sought out in all His desires! Holy and awesome is His name, which is above every name, that at the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of things in heaven, and things on earth, and things under the earth![^2864]

*Sticheron:*
Unto the Mystical Supper this day, and the Divine Table, let us draw near with fear, ye faithful, desiring to taste of the immortal Fount; but trembling and fear seize us, because of our many sins, from which we desire to be cleansed through Thee, by the prayers of the Mother of God our Lady.

*Glory... Tone 8:*
*Doxasticon:*
I am the living Bread that came down from heaven; if anyone eat, he shall not die, but shall live for ever; and the Bread which I will give is My true Flesh, which I gave for the life of the world; I am the living Bread, the Bread that comes down from heaven; if anyone eat of it, he shall not die, but hath eternal life.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion, the same Tone:*
He Whom thou didst bear without seed, O all-pure Virgin, He is seen mysteriously this day upon the throne in festival, and is given unto the faithful for food: Angels with trembling, and men as unto the True God bow down; Whom do thou entreat by thy maternal prayers, that He burn us not because of our sins, but have mercy and save our souls.

*Entrance with the censer.*
*O Gladsome Light.*

*Great Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*[^2865]
Who is so great a God as our God? Thou art the God Who doest wonders.
*Verse 1:* Thou hast made known Thy power among the peoples.
*Verse 2:* And I said: Now have I begun; this is the change of the right hand of the Most High.
*Verse 3:* I remembered the works of the Lord, for I will remember Thy wonders from the beginning.
*And again:* Who is so great a God as our God? Thou art the God Who doest wonders.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^2866]

*Sticheron:*
At the Mystical Supper, Christ God gave unto the Apostles to eat His Divine Flesh, for the healing of soul and body.

*Verse: Thou hast prepared a table before me, against them that afflict me:*
*Sticheron:*
Approach, all ye, not unto Mount Sinai, but unto God the Most High, concealed in these Mysteries, Christ our God.

*Verse: The Lord is my shepherd, and nothing shall I lack:*
*Sticheron:*
Christ is given unto all sinners for food, making bread His Body, and transforming wine into divine Blood.

*Glory... The same Tone and Podoben:*
*Doxasticon:*
We praise Thee, O Christ, our good Savior, Who hast poured out Thy Blood for the sake of human salvation.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion, the same:*
O Virgin Mary Mother of God, pray unto Him Who was born of thee, that He may make us worthy to be partakers of the Divine Mysteries.

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... Trisagion. After "Our Father:" Troparion of the Feast.*

## ON FRIDAY AT MATINS[^2867]
*Troparion of the Feast and of the Saint.*
*Sessional Hymns after the Kathismata of the Feast: (see fol. 617)[^2868].*
*Canon of the Feast, and of the Saint.*
*After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Saint.*
*After Ode 6, Kontakion of the Feast.*
*Exapostilarion of the Feast; Glory, of the Saint, if he have one; Both now: of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast.*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ воскре́слъ):*[^2869]

*Sticheron:*
This mystery is great and awesome, wherein the Creator God abides concealed, with Godhead, and Flesh, and Soul, for the sake of mercy toward the human race: unto Him let us all bow down as God, and the Savior of our souls.

*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*
*Sticheron:*
O marvelous wonder! That God, Who previously was uncontainable, was contained in the womb of the Virgin, and this day enclosed in a small crumb of bread, receives worship in festival from men: Whom let us hymn with Angels and Archangels, the Savior of our souls.[^2870]

*Verse: I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord:*[^2871]
*Sticheron:*
Thou art a High Priest according to the order of Melchizedek, offering unto God the Father bread and wine as a sacrifice of Thyself, for the sake of our sins, O Christ God our Savior; and unto Thee, the True God, with all thanksgiving we bow down.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 4:*[^2872]
*Doxasticon:*
Come, ye faithful lovers of the feasts, let us clap our hands this day for the world-saving triumph of the living Bread, and of the Lamb of God Who takes away the sins of the world and transgressions; not in the leaven of ancient malice, but in the unleavened bread of sincerity and truth; having cleansed ourselves, therefore, let us worthily partake of Him, that we may obtain His eternal Kingdom.

## ON FRIDAY EVENING[^2873]
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera of the Feast, Idiomela.*
*Tone 5:*

*Sticheron:*
Isaac, giving blessing unto his son Jacob the Patriarch, confirmed him with abundance of corn and wine, and unto this blessing the Patriarch joined also the primogeniture of sons: in like manner also did our Savior Christ the Lord, giving unto us the supernatural Bread, and wine from the true vine; for having taken bread into His divine hands, He said: Take, eat, this is My Body; drink of the cup, for this is My Blood, poured out for the whole world.[^2874]

*Sticheron:*
As the hart desires the fountains of waters, so desires my soul after Thee, O God, Who hast given us Thy Flesh for food, and dost feed us who hunger with Divine Bread, and dost give drink unto our thirsting souls with the wine of Thy most pure Blood: therefore this day bowing down in festival unto Thee, O Christ our God, Who becamest incarnate, didst suffer upon the Cross, and didst die for the sake of our salvation, didst rise on the third day, didst ascend into heaven, and sittest at the right hand of the Father, unto Thee we fall down and with compunction say: Save us for the sake of Thy tender mercy![^2875]

*Sticheron (continued):*[^2876]
While the Disciples were eating at the Mystical Supper, Christ, having taken bread into His hands, and having lifted up His eyes unto heaven, blessed, broke, and gave unto His Disciples, saying: Take, eat, this is My Body, which is broken for you for the remission of sins, and unto life eternal.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 3:*[^2877]
*Doxasticon:*
Come, all ye ends of the earth, and standing this day in the temple of the Lord, let us fall prostrate upon the face of the earth with humility, even as John the Theologian beheld the four and twenty elders falling down before Him Who sits upon the throne and Lives unto ages of ages: for this was the Lamb of God sitting upon the throne, Whom beholding this day in the Most Holy Mysteries, invisible, yet visible unto the faithful by faith: confessing Him as God and the Word incarnate, with fear and trembling let us bow down unto Him.

*O Gladsome Light.*
*Prokeimenon of the day.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela.*
*Tone 2:*[^2878]

*Sticheron:*
Shout with joy, ye people, unto God Who loves mankind, for the Mighty One hath done great things for us, at His Mystical Supper, giving unto all heavenly Bread for the salvation of our souls.

*Verse: He hath made a remembrance of His wonders:*
*Sticheron:*
Let all the earth bow down unto God Who lives in these Mysteries, thanking His tender mercy, that as supernatural Bread from the heavens, He gives food unto His beloved, whom He hath redeemed with His Blood.

*Verse: The Lord sent redemption unto His people:*
*Sticheron:*
Taste and see that the Lord is good, Who at the Mystical Supper gives His Flesh unto the Disciples, and unto all the faithful for food, and unto eternal salvation.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 6:*
*Doxasticon:*
I am the living Bread that came down from heaven; if anyone eat of it, he shall not die, but shall live for ever: and the Bread which I will give is My true Flesh, which I gave for the life of the world: I am the living Bread, the Bread that comes down from heaven; if anyone eat of it, he shall not die, but hath eternal life.

## ON SATURDAY AT MATINS[^2879]
*At the Aposticha, Stichera.*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^2880]

*Sticheron:*
Rejoice this day, Church of Christ, bowing down in festival unto Christ God, Who was born of the Virgin in the flesh, and is concealed in the Mysteries of bread, and is given unto men for food, and saves our souls.

*Verse: He rained down manna upon them to eat, and gave them the bread of heaven.*
*Sticheron:*
In the large upper room, the Disciples prepared to eat the Passover; Jesus reclined, and the twelve Apostles with Him, and the Lord said unto them: With desire have I desired to eat this Passover with you; having taken bread, given praise, and broken it, He gave it unto His Disciples, saying: Take, eat, this is My Body, broken for you for the remission of sins.

*Verse: Man ate the bread of Angels, He sent them food in abundance.*
*Sticheron:*
Having taken the cup, He said: This is the cup of the New Covenant in My Blood, which is poured out for you: amen I say unto you, that I shall not drink of the fruit of the vine, until the Kingdom of God comes.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 8:*
*Doxasticon:*
He Whom thou didst bear without seed, O all-pure Virgin, He is seen mysteriously this day upon the throne in festival, and is given unto the faithful for food: Angels with trembling, and men as unto the True God bow down; Whom do thou entreat by thy maternal prayers, that He burn us not because of our sins, but have mercy and save our souls.

*On Saturday evening:*[^2881]
*At "Lord, I have cried", Resurrection Stichera and 3 of the Feast. Tone 6: 1: "A great mystery"; 2: "The shadow uncovered"; 3: "Having blessed the bread". Glory: Tone 4: "Come, ye faithful lovers of the feasts"; all these are written on Wednesday evening at "Lord, I have cried". Both now: Dogmatikon of the tone. At the Aposticha, Stichera from the Oktoechos, Resurrectional. Glory, both now, of the Feast, Tone 2: "Unto the Mystical Supper this day"; written on Wednesday at the Litiya.*
*On Sunday at the Praises: Resurrection Stichera, and 3 of the Feast, Tone 1: 1: "O marvelous wonder, the Fount of life"; 2: "O marvelous wonder, He Who was prefigured in the manna"; 3: "O marvelous wonder, Wisdom hath slain"; Glory, of the Feast; Both now: "Thou art most blessed"; see all these on Thursday at the Praises.*

*On Sunday evening:*[^2882]
*At "Lord, I have cried", Stichera of the Feast, Idiomela, 3.*
*Tone 6:*

*Sticheron:*
On the first day of unleavened bread, the Disciples said unto Jesus: Lord, where wilt Thou that we prepare for Thee to eat the Passover? But He said: Go into the village ahead, and say unto the master of the house: The Teacher saith, With thee I will keep the Passover with My Disciples. And they did as they were commanded, and prepared the Passover: and while they were eating, Jesus, having taken bread, given thanks, blessed, and broken it, gave it unto the Disciples, saying: Take, eat, this is My Body; drink of the cup, this is My Blood, for the remission of sins.[^2883]

*Sticheron:*
David the ancestor of God, bringing the Ark from the house of Obed-edom into his city, played upon trumpets, upon timbrels, and cymbals, and all the people escorted the Ark with joy, and the king playing upon the harp: this Ark was a sign of the Ark of the Holy of Holies, the Body and Blood of Christ our God, Who this day is worshiped by all: unto Whom we also bowing down, pray unto Him as God and Creator of all, that He may save our souls.[^2884]

*Sticheron:*
The Lord fed His Disciples with heavenly food, before the Passion He gave them the Bread of Angels, marvelous beyond manna: Your fathers (said the Lord) ate manna in the wilderness and died; this is the Bread that comes down from heaven: if anyone eat of it, he shall not die, but shall live for ever; whereof vouchsafe us also now to partake, for the sake of the prayers of Thy Mother of God.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 3:*
*Doxasticon:*
O marvelous wisdom! O ineffable mercy of God toward us! How God not only became man for our sake, and concealed the Godhead in humanity, but in bread hid Himself, and gave Himself unto men for food: glory to Thy divine loving-kindness!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ воскре́слъ):*[^2885]

*Sticheron:*
Isaac blessed Jacob, saying: God give thee of the dew of heaven, and of the fatness of the earth abundance of corn and wine: for this was a mystery of the Body and Blood of the Lord, and was foretold.

*Verse: The Lord is my shepherd, and nothing shall I lack:*
*Sticheron:*
Esau also asked a blessing of his father: Is there not a blessing left for me also, father? With corn and wine have I sustained him, and what shall I do for thee, my child? said the father unto his son Esau.

*Verse: Thou hast prepared a table before me:*[^2886]
*Sticheron:*
There is no blessing, save in the Body and Blood of Christ, which Christ the Lord hath given unto us, Himself, for the cleansing of our sins.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 8:*[^2887]
*Doxasticon:*
This is the Bread that came down from heaven, that if anyone eat of it, he shall live, Jesus cried, saying: If anyone thirst, let him come unto Me and drink, and rivers of living water shall flow from his belly: he that eats My Flesh, and drinks My Blood, abides in Me, and I in him.

## ON MONDAY AT MATINS[^2888]
*At the Aposticha, Stichera.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

*Sticheron:*
O Savior Christ, Lover of mankind, Who for the sake of our salvation didst come down from heaven, and wast incarnate of the Virgin Mary, willing voluntarily to go unto death, Thou didst institute this Divine Mystery, giving Thyself for food unto the Disciples, and unto all the faithful: unto whom grant grace and eternal life.

*Verse: And He rained down manna upon them to eat, and gave them the bread of heaven.*
*Sticheron:*
I am the heavenly Bread (said the Lord unto His Disciples), that came down from heaven; I am the living Bread, he that comes to Me shall not hunger, and he that believs in Me shall not thirst: if anyone eat of this Bread, he shall live for ever, and the Bread which I will give is My Flesh, which gives eternal life unto them that partake.

*Verse: Man ate the bread of Angels, He sent them food in abundance.*
*Sticheron:*
Beholding the Mystery of the Divine Flesh, the supernatural Bread: unto God incarnate of the Virgin Mary, with compunction let us bow down: for behold, this is the innocent Lamb, Who takes away the sins of the world, and saves our souls.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 2:*[^2889]
*Doxasticon:*
All creation was amazed at the awesome divine transformation: how God, uncontained by heaven and earth, in a small host and particle of bread, is wholly contained, with Godhead, Flesh, and Divine Soul: Whom thus confessing, let us bow down unto the Divine Body of Christ: that we may obtain from Him the Father's blessing.

## ON MONDAY EVENING

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast on 6, Idiomela:*[^2890]

*Tone 6:*

*Sticheron 1:*
Come, all ye heavenly Angels, and all ye Powers of God, praise with us God Who became man: Who for the sake of the mercy of the human race, by His unsearchable divine wisdom, shut Himself up in the Mysteries of bread, and left Himself as food for men: Whom as God let us celebrate today and hymn, crying: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O God, glory to Thy loving-kindness!

*Sticheron 2:*
Come, all ye ends of the earth, let us bless the Most Holy Body of God, broken for us and for our sins, and given unto death upon the Cross: and after the Resurrection ascended into heaven, seated in glory at the right hand of God the Father, and in these Mysteries left upon earth the same for the faithful: before Whom we, all earthborn, falling down upon the face of the earth, praise with compunction.

*Sticheron 3:*
Come, ye assemblies of feast-lovers, come, let us crown the Church of Christ, festive and adorned today: wherein Christ the supernatural Bread is beheld, and given unto men for food; Whom let all of us, the faithful, worship with fear and gladness, and with the choirs of the heavenly Powers let us praise Him, crying: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Lamb of God, Who takest away the sins of the world!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 6. Doxasticon:*
Come, all ye works of the Lord, hymn the Lord with us, Who is worshipped here: bless Him, sun, moon, and stars: fire, sea, air, clouds, and all creation: for God loved the world and the human race exceedingly and unto the end, for whose sake He emptied Himself, taking the form of a servant; in these Mysteries He concealed not only His Godhead, but also His humanity: which we behold as bread, but believe as God, and bowing down unto Him with fear and trembling, we confess: that He is Christ, the Son of the living God, and the Savior of our souls.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Єѵфра́ѳовъ):*

*Sticheron 1:*
O house of David, hymn after the manner of the psalms: for behold, the Ark of the Most High, in which is contained He that contains all things, Christ the God of Israel.

*Verse: He sent redemption unto His people:*[^2891]

*Sticheron 2:*
He that gave the manna in the wilderness, as food unto His people: today is beheld veiled in bread, and given unto the faithful for food, saving our souls.

*Verse: The Lord hath made a memorial of His wonders, merciful, compassionate, and righteous:*[^2892]

*Sticheron 3:*
Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem, praise thy God, O Sion: for He gives thee His Divine Flesh for food, filling thee with the fat of wheat.[^2893]

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 6. Doxasticon:*
Great is our Lord, and great is His power; Who instead of bread gave His Flesh for food unto the Christian race: that He might unite Himself with them that partake, and raise all that receive Communion unto divine sonship, and save our souls.

## ON TUESDAY AT MATINS

*At the Aposticha.*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нб҃ныхъ чино́въ):*

*Sticheron 1: Rejoice today, O Church of Christ:*

*Sticheron 2: In the great upper room:*

*Sticheron 3: Taking also the cup: with their verses:*

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 8: Whom thou didst bear without seed:*

*All these (see fol. 632)[^2894] on Saturday at Matins.*

## ON TUESDAY EVENING

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera.*

*Tone 3:*

*Sticheron 1:*
He that rained down the manna in the wilderness, and gave the bread of Angels from heaven unto the Israelites: today is beheld shut up in bread, and given unto all Christians for food, for the remission of sins, and unto life eternal.

*Sticheron 2:*
Let us purify our heart and conscience, approaching not unto Mount Sinai, but unto God, saying with Moses unto Christ God and our Redeemer, concealed in these most pure Mysteries, Who saves our souls:

*Sticheron 3:*
Before Whom the Angelic Powers tremble, and the heavenly hosts are struck with awe: falling down before Him, let us worship, and with compunction let us cry aloud: Make us worthy, O Lord, worthily to partake of Thy Flesh and Thy Blood.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 6. Doxasticon:*
Before the shadowy ark, David the ancestor of God leaped, playing: and we today worship this One as God, Whose humanity the ark prefigured: for instead of manna, here is Godhead incarnate; instead of the tablet of the Law, Christ the Lord the Lawgiver is beheld hidden, saving our souls.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Єѵфра́ѳовъ):*

*Sticheron 1:*
This is the Bread of life, that came down from heaven, nourishing the faithful with His Divine Flesh, and granting eternal life.

*Verse: Merciful and compassionate is the Lord, He hath given food unto them that fear Him:*[^2895]

*Sticheron 2:*
Come, ye faithful, let us fall down before Christ God Who is upon the altar, worshipping the Savior of our souls.

*Verse: And He rained down manna upon them to eat, and gave them the bread of heaven:*[^2896]

*Sticheron 3:*
Taste of the manna, of the Flesh of Christ, and with fear receive the Divine Bread, which gives eternal life.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 8. Doxasticon:*
Behold, the Lamb of God, that takes away the sins of the world, giving Himself as food unto men, is beheld festive upon the altar, praised by Angels and worshipped by men; Who comes to judge the living and the dead, and to render unto each according to his works: Whom we also worship with compunction, as our God and Creator.

## ON WEDNESDAY AT MATINS

*At the Aposticha, Stichera.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Єѵфра́ѳовъ):*

*Sticheron 1:*
My Flesh is true food, and My Blood is drink, said the Lord: he that eats My Flesh and drinks My Blood abides in Me and I in him.[^2897]

*Verse: And He rained down manna upon them to eat, and gave them the bread of heaven:*

*Sticheron 2:*
This is the Lamb of God, that takes away sins, Who suffered upon the Cross, and gave His Flesh for food: Whom as God we worship.

*Verse: Taste and see that the Lord is good:*[^2898]

*Sticheron 3:*
I am the Bread of life, that came down from heaven, said the Lord: if any man eat of It, he shall not die, but shall have eternal life in the Kingdom of heaven.[^2899]

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 7. Doxasticon:*
Take up thy harp, O David, and play, rejoicing: for behold, the Manna that came down from heaven is transformed into the Bread of Angels, and given unto men for food: of which all having partaken, receive the True God in supernatural Bread, and are united with Him: let us therefore also draw near, let us taste, and let us behold the immortal Fountain.

*Then: It is good to give praise to the Lord: Trisagion, and after Our Father: Troparion of the Feast.*
*And the Dismissal.*

## ON WEDNESDAY EVENING

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast on 6, written at Vespers on Wednesday:*
*Likewise at the Aposticha, we sing everything of the Feast without change, except for the Paremias, and the Megalynarion at Matins. The service of the saint is sung at Compline, or whenever the superior wills. But if it be a great saint, it is sung together with the Feast: for him with Paremias, Megalynarion, and Polyeleos, and the rest. But if it be not a great saint, the whole service is sung without change, just as on the very day of the Feast.*

*On Thursday at Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast, thrice. Sessional Hymns after the First and Second Kathismata. Then the Canon, Exapostilarion, and at the Praises of the Feast: Great Doxology. Troparion: First Hour, and the Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy we sing everything without change, just as on the very day of the Feast.*

*Be it known, that the Council of Lviv, held in the year 1720, commands to sing the Office of the Most Holy Eucharist solemnly on the Second Sunday after the Descent of the Holy Spirit, according to the general typikon of the Great Feasts of the Lord.*[^2900]

*[Illustration: Icon of the Most Holy Theotokos holding the Christ Child.]*[^2902]

## ORDER OF THE FEAST OF THE COMPASSION OF THE MOST HOLY THEOTOKOS[^2901]

*Celebrated on the Friday of the Second Week after the Sunday of All Saints.*

*On Thursday evening at "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Theotokos on 6.*

*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Unto all men a Protectress, unto all a great Refuge, unto all Christians a Defender did He reveal thee, Who from thee clothed Himself in our mortal clay in His tender compassion, and underwent Crucifixion and death for our sake: Him therefore pray unceasingly, that He send down upon all the cleansing of offenses, O Bride of God.

*Sticheron 2:*
Of old the Virgin and Ewe-lamb, beholding Him that came forth from her womb hanging upon the Tree as one condemned, together with two condemned men, cried aloud, saying: O my Son and my God, strange sight, strange mystery! Yet the abyss of Thy wisdom none can search out: I hymn Thy longsuffering!

*Sticheron 3:*
Where is the majesty of Thy form? Where hath the beauty of Thy countenance hidden itself, O Son? How is it that Thou, Who with ineffable beauty didst fashion all things, Who art fairer than all, for the sake of all men hangest upon the Tree inglorious and dishonored, having neither form nor beauty, O Child? in wonder did the Virgin cry, groaning and weeping.

*Sticheron 4:*
Beholding Thee, O Jesus, lifted up upon the wood of the Cross, the Unwedded Maiden wept and said: O sweet Child, for what cause hast Thou left me alone, who bore Thee? O Light unapproachable of the unoriginate Father,[^2903] hasten and be glorified, that they may obtain divine glory who glorify Thy divine Passions!

*Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*

*Sticheron 5:*
When upon the Tree thou didst behold the Life dying, O Virgin, and painfully pierced with the spear, weeping thou didst cry aloud: O my Son and my God, what hath the ungrateful assembly repaid Thee? Woe is me, I cannot bear the pain, I am consumed in my womb, seeing Thee suffering these things, O Master!

*Sticheron 6:*
When she beheld Him taken down from the Tree, the Maiden that knew not wedlock, laid stretched out upon the earth breathless as a man: embracing Him in her bosom, and kissing His lips and eyes, cried aloud unto Him: Wonderfully, how do I now behold Thee, Who vivifiest all things, speechless and motionless? Truly a great marvel!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 2. Theotokion:*
Having endured many sorrows at the Crucifixion of thy Son and thy God, O Most Pure One, groaning and weeping thou didst cry aloud: Woe is me, O sweet Child! How dost Thou suffer unjustly, willing to deliver the earthborn sons of Adam? Therefore, O most pure Virgin, we pray thee in faith: make Him merciful unto us!

*Entrance, and the Prokeimenon of the day. Then the Paremias.*[^2904]

*Reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah:*[^2905]
*Lesson 1 (Lamentations 1:1-2, 3-4, 9, 11-13, 16-17, 20):*
In those days, Jeremiah wept over Jerusalem, and said: How doth the city sit solitary, that was full of people! How is she become as a widow! She weeps sore in the night, and her tears are on her cheeks: among all her lovers she hath none to comfort her: all her friends have dealt treacherously with her, they are become her enemies. She dwells among the nations, she finds no rest. And from the daughter of Sion all her beauty is departed. Behold, O Lord, my affliction: for the enemy hath magnified himself. All ye that pass by, turn back and see if there be any sorrow like unto my sorrow, which is done unto me, wherewith the Lord hath afflicted me in the day of His fierce anger. He hath called an assembly against me; from above hath He sent fire into my bones, and hath chastened me. My strength is made weak within me; for the Lord hath delivered affliction into my hands. For these things I weep; mine eyes run down with water, because the comforter that should relieve my soul is far from me: my children are desolate, because the enemy prevailed. Sion spreads forth her hands, and there is none to comfort her: the Lord hath commanded concerning Jacob, that his adversaries should be round about him. Behold, O Lord, for I am in distress: my bowels are troubled within me; mine heart is turned within me; for I am filled with bitterness.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah:*[^2906]
*Lesson 2 (Lamentations 2:13, 15-20; 3:7, 12-13, 15, 19, 21):*
In those days, Jeremiah wept over Jerusalem, and said: What thing shall I take to witness for thee, or what shall I liken to thee, O daughter of Jerusalem? Who shall save thee, or who shall comfort thee, O virgin daughter of Sion? All that pass by clap their hands at thee; they hiss and wag their head at the daughter of Jerusalem, saying: Is this the city that men call the perfection of beauty, the joy of the whole earth? The Lord hath done that which He had devised; He hath fulfilled His word that He had commanded in the days of old: give thyself no rest; let not the apple of thine eye cease. Arise, meditate in the night; in the beginning of thy watches pour out thine heart like water before the face of the Lord: lift up thy hands toward Him for the life of thy young children, that faint for hunger. Behold, O Lord, and consider whom Thou hast thus afflicted. He hath hedged me about, and encompassed my head, and I have labored. He hath bent His bow, and set me as a mark for the arrow. He hath caused the arrows of His quiver to enter into my reins. He hath filled me with bitterness, He hath made me drunken with wormwood. Remembering my misery, my bitterness and the gall. This I recall to my heart, therefore will I hope in the Lord.

*Lesson 2 (continued, Lamentations 3:22-26):*
It is of the Lord's mercies that we are not consumed, because His compassions fail not. And I recognized in the morning that great is Thy faithfulness. The Lord is my portion, saith my soul; therefore will I hope in Him. The Lord is good unto them that wait for Him, to the soul that seeks Him. It is good[^2907] that a man should both hope and quietly wait for the salvation of God.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah:*[^2908]
*Lesson 3 (Lamentations 3:40-41, 55-59; 4:1-2, 7, 11, 20; 5:19, 21):*
In those days, Jeremiah wept over Jerusalem, and said: Let us search and try our ways, and turn again to the Lord. Let us lift up our heart with our hands unto God in the heavens. I called upon Thy name, O Lord, out of the lowest pit. Thou hast heard my voice: hide not Thine ear at my breathing, at my cry. Thou drewest near in the day that I called upon Thee: Thou saidst, Fear not. O Lord, Thou hast pleaded the causes of my soul; Thou hast redeemed my life. O Lord, Thou hast seen my wrongs: judge Thou my cause. Thou hast seen all their vengeance and all their imaginations against me. Render unto them a recompence, O Lord, according to the work of their hands. How is the gold become dim! How is the most fine gold changed! The stones of the sanctuary are poured out in the top of every street. The precious sons of Sion, comparable to fine gold. Her Nazarites were purer than snow, they were whiter than milk, their bodies were more ruddy than rubies, their polishing was of sapphire. The Lord hath accomplished His fury; the breath of our nostrils, the anointed of the Lord, was taken in their pits, of whom we said: Under His shadow we shall live among the heathen. Thou, O Lord, remainest for ever; Thy throne from generation to generation. Turn Thou us unto Thee, O Lord, and we shall be turned; renew our days as of old.

*At the Litiya, Stichera of the Most Holy Theotokos.*

*Tone 8:*

*Sticheron 1:*
I cannot bear, O Child, to see Thee asleep upon the Tree, Who givest wakefulness unto all: that unto them who of old fell asleep with a most destructive sleep from the transgression of the fruit, Thou mightest give divine and saving wakefulness: cried the weeping Virgin, whom we magnify.

*Sticheron 2:*
The unjust assembly lifted Thee, the Lamb that takes away the sins of the world, upon the Cross, and with a spear they pierced Thy ribs, O Longsuffering One, and nailed Thy hands and Thy feet: O wicked cruelty! O insolence! cried the Most Pure One, weeping with compunction.

*Sticheron 3:*
What is this sight which I now behold, O Master? He that marvelously holds all creation in His palm hangs unjustly upon the Tree like a lamb, O Word of God, suspended by the hands of the disobedient! O forbearance! O Thy goodness, O All-Merciful One! said the All-Blameless One, weeping.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 6. Doxasticon:*
When thou didst behold nailed upon the Cross the Lamb born of thee, O Ewe-lamb, weeping thou didst cry aloud: Woe is me, O sweetest Child! without sorrow did I bear Thee before, but now my heart is pierced: and I cannot bear to see Thee crucified, O Christ, my most merciful Son!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera.*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы):*

*Sticheron 1:*
Standing by the Cross of her Son and her God, and looking upon His longsuffering, the pure Mother wept and said: Woe is me, O sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mayest save mankind?

*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы):*

*Verse: O God, my God, attend unto me, why hast Thou forsaken me?*[^2909]

*Sticheron 2:*
Standing by the Cross, the unwedded Bride, receiving the wounds of anguish, cried aloud: Leave me not childless, O my Child, who bore Thee, O Word of God: forsake me not, O Only-Begotten, co-eternal with the Father!

*Verse: The troubles of my heart are multiplied:*[^2910]

*Sticheron 3:*
When I gave birth to Thee, I had no sorrow; how then do I now endure pains, seeing Thee, O Word, unjustly crucified?[^2911] bitterly cried the Virgin, weeping: Woe is me! I cannot bear to see Thee naked, lifted up upon the Cross, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Tone 1. Theotokion:*
When the spotless Ewe-lamb beheld her Lamb voluntarily led to the slaughter as a man, weeping she said: Dost Thou now hasten to leave me childless, O Good One, who bore Thee, O Christ? What is this that Thou hast done, O Redeemer of all? Yet I hymn and glorify Thy supreme goodness surpassing mind and word, O Lover of mankind!

*Then: Now lettest Thou: after the Trisagion: Troparion.*

*Tone 6:*
With the virgin Disciple, the Virgin Mother standing by the Cross of Him that came forth from her, unable to bear seeing Him lifted up upon the Cross, was seized with lamentations and cried aloud: How dost Thou suffer, Who art by nature Passionless? I glorify Thine ineffable goodness, O my Son!

*At Matins.*

*At "God is the Lord," Troparion thrice.*

*After the First Kathisma, Sessional Hymn.*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*
O ineffable goodness of Him that was incarnate of thee, O all-hymned Theotokos! For having endured the Cross and death as the Lover of mankind, that He might save the world which He created: pray Him, that He deliver also me, the wretched one, from many sorrows and torment, and establish me where the unwaning light shines.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*After the Second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn.*

*Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ):*
What is this sight which is visible to my eyes, O Master! He that holds all creation, having been lifted up upon the Tree, dost Thou die, Who givest life unto all? the Theotokos weeping cried aloud, when she beheld lifted up upon the Cross God and Man Who shone forth from her ineffably.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Then:*

*MEGALYNARION:*
We magnify thee, O all-blameless Virgin, Mother of Christ our God, and we honor thy most venerable sorrows, which thou didst endure at the Passion of thy Son.

*Verse 1: O God, my God, attend unto me, why hast Thou forsaken me, far from my salvation?*

*Verse 2: The troubles of my heart are multiplied, deliver me from my distresses.*

*Verse 3: Awake, my glory; awake, psaltery and harp; I will awake early.*[^2912]

*Verse 4: I will remember thy name in every generation and generation.*[^2913]

*Verse 5: For it is good before Thy saints.*[^2914]

*Verse 6: In Him hath my heart hoped, and I have been helped: and my flesh hath flourished again, and willingly will I confess Him.*[^2915]

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Alleluia, thrice.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn.*

*Tone 1:*
The spotless Ewe-lamb, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd suspended dead upon the Tree, weeping said, crying aloud in motherly grief: How do I endure Thy condescension surpassing all word, O my Son, and Thy voluntary Passions, O Most Good God?

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Then the Gradual Hymns, the First Antiphon of the Fourth Tone.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
I will remember thy name in every generation and generation.
*Verse: Give ear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear.*

*Let every breath:*
*The Gospel according to John, Pericope 64.*[^2916]

*After the Gospel, Sticheron.*

*Tone 6:*
O sole spotless Ewe-lamb, Virgin Theotokos, beholding Him that was incarnate of thy pure blood hanging voluntarily upon the Cross, with bitter tears thou didst cry aloud, O Maiden: O my Son devoid of malice, I hymn Thine all-awesome dispensation!

*CANON*

*The work of Symeon the Logothete.*[^2917]

*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Having walked as on dry land with their footsteps through the deep, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Refrain: Most Holy Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion 1:*
Beholding her Son and Lord suspended upon the Cross, the pure Virgin, torn with grief, cried aloud bitterly with the other women, and groaning said:

*Troparion 2:*
Now I behold Thee, my beloved and cherished Child, hanging upon the Cross, and I am wounded bitterly in heart, said the Pure One: but give a word, O Good One, unto Thine handmaid!

*Glory:*
Voluntarily, O my Son and Creator, dost Thou endure cruel death upon the Tree, said the Virgin, standing by the Cross with the beloved Disciple.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen:*
*Theotokion:*
Now am I bereft of my hope, joy, and gladness, my Son and Lord: woe is me, grieving in heart! the Pure One weeping said.

*Katavasia:*[^2918]
I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen and Mother, and I shall be seen radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall hymn her wonders.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparion 1:*
For fear of the Jews Peter hid himself, and all the faithful fled away, forsaking Christ, the Virgin said, lamenting.

*Troparion 2:*
At Thy fearful and strange Nativity, O my Son, above all mothers was I magnified: but woe is me, now seeing Thee upon the Tree, I am inflamed in my womb!

*Glory:*
I desired to receive from the Tree into my arms my Fruit, in which I held Thee when Thou wast an Infant, said the Pure One: but none, woe is me, granted me this!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Lo, my sweet Light, my Hope, and my good Life, my God is quenched upon the Cross! I am inflamed in my womb! the Virgin said, groaning.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 6:*
The flock which Thy Son and God acquired, O Most Pure One, sprinkling it with His Blood, deliver Thou from tribulations by thy venerable prayers:[^2919] driving away visible and invisible enemies like wild beasts, and fill their faces with dishonor,[^2920] as David the psalmist proclaimed of old, O Pure One.

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^2921]
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church divinely sings, crying aloud from a pure understanding, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Refrain: Most Holy Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion 1:*
O never-setting Sun, eternal God and Creator of all creatures, O Lord, how dost Thou endure Passion upon the Cross? the Pure One said, weeping.

*Troparion 2:*
Weeping, the unwedded Maiden said unto the noble Joseph:[^2922] Hasten, O Joseph, to go unto Pilate, and ask to take down from the Tree thy Teacher!

*Glory:*
Beholding the Most Pure One bitterly shedding tears, Joseph was troubled, and weeping drew near unto Pilate: Give me, he cried aloud with weeping, the Body of my God!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Beholding Thee wounded and without glory, naked upon the Tree, O my Child, I am inflamed in my womb! the Virgin uttered, lamenting as a Mother.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^2923]
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine, I pray Thee, the souls of them that rise early unto Thee with love, to know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who recallest us from the gloom of sins.

*Refrain: Most Holy Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion 1:*
Torn with grief, and weeping, and marveling together with Nicodemus, Joseph took Him down, and having kissed the most pure Body, he wept and groaned, praising Him as God.

*Troparion 2:*
Receiving Him with weeping, the unwedded Mother laid Him upon her knees, beseeching Him with tears and kissing Him, bitterly lamenting and crying aloud.

*Glory:*
One Hope and Life, O Master, my Son and God, the Light of mine eyes had I, Thine handmaid; but now am I bereft of Thee, my sweet and beloved Child!

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Pains and sorrows and sighings have found me, woe is me! the Pure One said, bitterly lamenting, beholding Thee, my beloved Child, a corpse, naked and alone, and anointed with sweet spices.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^2924]
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly Merciful One!

*Refrain: Most Holy Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion 1:*
Dead do I behold Thee, O Lover of mankind, Who didst make alive the dead and Who upholdest all things, and I am wounded bitterly in my womb: I would that I had died with Thee, the Most Pure One said: for I cannot endure to see Thee dead without breath!

*Troparion 2:*
I marvel, beholding Thee, O all-good God and most compassionate Lord, without glory, without breath, and without form; weeping, holding Thee as I had never expected, woe is me! to see Thee, my Son and God!

*Glory:*
Wilt Thou not speak a word unto Thine handmaid, O Word of God? Wilt Thou not have compassion, O Master, upon her that bare Thee? the Pure One said, weeping and lamenting, kissing the Body of her Lord.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
I consider, O Master, that no more shall I hear Thy sweet voice, nor shall Thine handmaid behold the beauty of Thy countenance as before; for Thou hast set from mine eyes, O my Son!

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^2925]
Beholding Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and voluntarily accepting the Passions, O Master, the Virgin and Thy Mother cried aloud: Woe is me, O sweet Child! How dost Thou endure wounds unjustly, O Physician and Healer of human infirmity, Who by Thy mercy hast delivered all from corruption?

*Ikos:*[^2926]
Beholding her own Lamb drawn unto slaughter, Mary followed with hair disheveled, together with other women, crying aloud these things: Whither goest Thou, Child? For what reason dost Thou complete this swift course? Is there again another wedding in Cana of Galilee, and dost Thou now hasten thither, that Thou mayest make wine for them from water? Shall I go with Thee, Child, or rather wait for Thee? Give me a word, O Word, pass me not by in silence, Thou Who hast preserved me pure: for Thou art my Son and God!

*Synaxarion on the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos:*[^2927]
On this same Friday, we celebrate the memory of the Compassion wherewith the All-Holy and Spotless Mother Virgin Mary suffered together with her Son Jesus Christ at the time of His world-saving Passions. And this Feast in the beginning was instituted by the local Council of Cologne in the year 1423[^2928] (according to the testimony of the most holy Benedict, fourteenth Pope of Rome of that name),[^2929] in rebuttal of the senseless audacity of certain heretics,[^2930] who raged exceedingly against the Icon of the most glorious Mother of God suffering at the Crucifixion. From that time therefore, in the territory only of the Roman Church (as he says), it was venerated on Friday of the sixth week of the holy Great Fast; then in time this pious veneration spread among them also unto other lands. But among us it was initiated from the local Council which was in the city of Zamość,[^2931] for the following reasons: Because of old in many places of the Ruthenian borders the Christ-loving people were accustomed piously to revere the Friday which is the tenth after Pascha;[^2932] therefore unto this their veneration, our Ruthenian Church—which has its entire order from those whom all the Teachers of the Western Church are accustomed to call *Hyperphileotheotokos*,[^2933] that is to say: lovers of the Mother of God above all, or Fathers surpassing in love towards the Mother of God, from the Greeks, I say—out of exceedingly great love toward the Most Holy Theotokos, joined unto that day of theirs, that is, unto the tenth Friday after the Resurrection of Christ, to revere with devotion that which, apart from the daily Services, two days in each week, is manifoldly commemorated generally, but by its own feast was not venerated, although every reason of Christian piety always made it right to anticipate it: namely, the suffering of the All-Praised and Most Blessed Ever-Virgin Mary, which in reality, at the time of the voluntary Passion of her Son Christ, fulfilling the prophecy of the venerable God-receiver Simeon concerning herself: *Yea, a sword shall pierce through thine own soul also;*[^2934] she unspeakably endured. For if the wounds and torments of the martyrs endured in their bodies for Christ, in the tribulations of their salvation, are exceedingly revered by us, how much more is it worthy to revere the grief of her who, together with Christ, for the common redemption of us all, suffered anguish not now in body, but in soul? Therefore rightly, by Saint Augustine[^2935] and the other Fathers, the most holy Mother is accounted to have suffered more than all the martyrs. But in so far as that love which she bare toward her natural Son surpassed the love of the saints who surrendered their lives for Christ—Whom they had not seen nor known so intimately—by so much the Compassion of her who saw and knew Him to be God, so cruelly dishonored and slain for the sins of men, is exalted above those sufferings. This Compassion of the Most Holy and Most Blessed Virgin Mother of God we therefore now revere with such goodwill, as we are bound to be thankful unto her, not only for that she bare for us the Author and Finisher of faith, Jesus,[^2936] but also as it were co-operated with the Son to suffer for us, through which we in every way hope to obtain salvation, in Christ Jesus our Lord. Amen.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^2937]
The Angel made the furnace sprinkle dew for the holy children, but the command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tyrant to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Refrain: Most Holy Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion 1:*
Where, O my Son and God, is the ancient Annunciation which Gabriel spake unto me, calling Thee King and Son of the Most High God?[^2938] But now I behold Thee, my sweet Light, a corpse, naked and wounded!

*Troparion 2:*
O Deliverer from afflictions, now receive me with Thyself, O my Son and God, that I too may descend into Hades with Thee; leave me not alone, for I no longer endure to live, not beholding Thee, my sweet Light!

*Glory:*
With the other Myrrh-bearing women, the Spotless One, bitterly weeping and beholding Christ carried, said: Woe is me, what do I behold! Whither goest Thou now, my Son, and leavest me alone?

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
Fainting and weeping, the Spotless One said unto the Myrrh-bearers: Weep with me, and lament bitterly, for lo, my sweet Light, your Teacher, is delivered unto the tomb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^2939]
From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the holy youths, and with water didst burn up the sacrifice of the righteous man; for Thou dost all things, O Christ, whatsoever Thou desirest: Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Refrain: Most Holy Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion 1:*
Beholding the Virgin weeping, Joseph was all torn with grief, and cried aloud bitterly: How shall I, Thy servant, now bury Thee, O my God? In what winding-sheets shall I wrap Thy Body?

*Troparion 2:*
Beyond understanding surpassed Thy strange sight, O Lord Who upholdest all creation: therefore Joseph, carrying Thee upon his hands as dead, with Nicodemus bears and buris Thee.

*Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord:*
*Troparion:*
A strange and most glorious mystery do I behold! the Virgin cried aloud unto the Son and Lord: How art Thou laid in a poor tomb, Who by Thy command dost raise up the dead in the tombs?

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*

*Theotokion:*
Neither shall I arise from Thy tomb, my Child, nor shall I, Thine handmaid, cease shedding tears, until I too descend into Hades; for I cannot endure separation from Thee, O my Son!

*We do not sing "More honorable," but we sing the Refrains:*

*Refrain 1:*
Magnify, O my soul, the Virgin Mary grieving by the Cross of the Lord.[^2940]

*Another refrain:*
Beholding the affliction of the Most Pure One, the Angels were amazed, how the Virgin suffers with the Son.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^2941]
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of Angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O Most Pure One, the Word incarnate was revealed unto men: Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Refrain: Most Holy Theotokos, save us.*

*Troparion 1:*
Joy shall never henceforth touch me, the Spotless One said, lamenting: My Light and my Joy hath gone down into the tomb; but I will not leave Him alone, here will I die, and be buried together with Him!

*Troparion 2:*
Heal now the wound of my soul, O my Child, the Most Pure One cried aloud with tears: Arise, and quell my pain and sorrow; for Thou art able, O Master, whatsoever Thou desirest, to do, even though Thou hast been buried voluntarily!

*Glory:*
O how was the abyss of compassions concealed from thee? the Lord said secretly unto His Mother: For desiring to save My creation, I willed to die; but I will also arise, and will magnify thee, as God of heaven and earth.

*Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Theotokion:*
I will hymn Thy tender mercy, O Lover of mankind, and I worship the wealth of Thy mercy, O Master: for desiring to save Thy creation, Thou didst undergo death, said the Most Pure One; but by Thy Resurrection, O Savior, have mercy upon us all!

*Exapostilarion:*
O Holy Virgin most pure, glory of the Angels, when thou didst stand by the Cross of thy Son and God, not enduring to behold the reproaches of the enemies, thou didst cry aloud, lamenting motherly: How dost Thou endure threats, O Lover of mankind? I glorify Thy long-suffering!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen. The same.*

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4.*

*Tone 6, Podoben: "The Angelic Powers" (А҆́гг҃лскїѧ предъиди́те Си́лы):*[^2942]

*Sticheron 1:*
Slain by many passions, make alive my soul, O Sovereign Lady, who didst give birth impassibly unto God the Word, the Impassible One Who endured the Passions, and Who put to death death and Hades; by His mortal dying put to death the sin that dwells in me, and direct my deadened thoughts toward divine things. *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*
When Thou wast pleased to be lifted up upon the Cross, O Christ, then beholding the Disciple whom Thou lovedst standing by, and Thy Mother, Thou didst address them:[^2943] Thy Mother is the Virgin, this youth is thy son, O Virgin Mother pure, whom I have loved, the virgin and My Disciple.[^2944]

*Sticheron 3:*
Thenceforth therefore the virgin took the Virgin unto his own home; having been called a son by the all-bountiful Son, he showed toward her as unto a mother that which befits sonship, and warm devotion always; by whose prayers, O Christ God, save our souls!

*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Stavrotheotokion:*
When the Ewe-lamb beheld the Lamb born of thee, O Virgin, nailed upon the Cross, thou didst cry aloud, tearing thy hair: Woe is me, most sweet Child! Without pains did I bear Thee before, but now I suffer pain in heart, and cannot endure to see Thee crucified, O Christ, my most merciful Son!

*After this, the Great Doxology.*
*After the Trisagion, Troparion of the feast: "With the virgin disciple:" (see fol. 638),[^2945] litanies, and dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy: The customary Antiphons of the Theotokos.*[^2946]
*1. "It is good to give praise:" 2. "The Lord is King:" 3. "Come, let us rejoice in the Lord:"*
*Save us, O Son of God, through the prayers of the Theotokos, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia.*
*After the Entrance, Troparion of the feast.*
*Tone 6:*
*With the virgin disciple:*
*Glory... Both now and for ever and unto ages of ages. Amen.*
*Kontakion: "Beholding Thee upon the Cross:" Then after the Trisagion:*

*Prokeimenon. Tone 4:*
Weeping, she wept in the night, and her tears are on her cheeks.[^2947]
*Verse: And there is none to comfort her among all them that love her.*

*Reading from the Book of Judith. Chapter 13:*[^2948]
The Lord God hath blessed thee in His power, Who through thee hath brought our enemies to nought. Blessed art thou, O daughter, by the Lord God Most High, above all the women upon the earth. Blessed be the Lord God, Who created heaven and earth, Who hath directed thee to the cutting off of the head of the chief of our enemies, and Who this day hath so magnified thy name, that thy praise shall not depart from the mouth of men, who remember the power of the Lord for ever; seeing that thou hast not spared thy life for the sorrow and affliction of thy nation, but hast helped against our ruin before the face of the Lord our God.

*Alleluia. Tone 6:*
*Verse 1: My strength hath failed within me, for the Lord hath delivered affliction into my hand.*[^2949]
*Verse 2: He that comforted me, and that refreshed my soul, is far departed from me.*[^2950]

*Gospel according to John, Pericope 61:*[^2951]
At that time, there stood by the Cross of Jesus His Mother, and His Mother's sister, Mary the wife of Cleophas, and Mary Magdalene. When Jesus therefore saw His Mother, and the Disciple standing by, whom He loved, He saith unto His Mother: Woman, behold thy son! Then saith He to the Disciple: Behold thy Mother! And from that hour that Disciple took her unto his own home.

*Communion Hymn:*[^2952]
If there be any sorrow like unto my sorrow; therefore my heart is troubled within me.
Alleluia. *(Thrice)*

*At the refectory, because of the labor of the vigil, great consolation for the brethren: We eat fish with olive oil, and drink wine, unto the glory of God.*

[Engraving: A decorative scroll vignette depicting the Greek letters Alpha and Omega, with the Cross of Christ inscribed between them, amidst foliage.][^2953]

### Notes

[^2267]: Major Part VII Landmark opening the celebration of the Great Feast of Holy Pentecost (Leaves 545-668 / Printed pp. 539-663). The 1907 Lviv edition introduces this solemnity with an elaborate woodcut headpiece depicting the Mother of God and the Holy Apostles in the Cenacle receiving the Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).
[^2268]: Rubric for Little Vespers on Saturday evening before Pentecost. The printed rubrical cinnabar text erroneously specifies "воскрⷭ҇ны" (resurrectional) stichera, an obvious typographical slip by the Stauropegion compositors, as the four stichera that follow are exclusively festal idiomela/prosomoia of Pentecost. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).
[^2269]: First sticheron at *Lord, I have cried* of Little Vespers, Tone 1 (Melodic model: *Joy of the heavenly ranks*): Proclamation of the renewal of all nations through the fiery tongues, empowering the disciples to declare the mighty works of God (Acts 2:11). Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).
[^2270]: Second sticheron at *Lord, I have cried* of Little Vespers, Tone 1 (Melodic model: *Joy of the heavenly ranks*): Christ fulfilling His solemn promise (Lk. 24:49) by sending down power from on high upon the Disciples through the Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).
[^2271]: Third sticheron at *Lord, I have cried* of Little Vespers, Tone 1 (Melodic model: *Joy of the heavenly ranks*): The Comforter sent into the world in tongues of fire to consume human sin and restore genuine mystical communion with God. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).
[^2272]: Doxasticon at *Lord, I have cried* (*Glory, both now*), Tone 7: Doxology venerating the Holy Spirit as "another Comforter" (Jn. 14:16) sent by Christ from the Father unto the earth. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).
[^2273]: First sticheron at the Aposticha of Little Vespers, Tone 2 (Melodic model: *O House of Ephratha*): The minds of the Disciples illuminated by divine radiance as they receive the Holy Spirit essentially into their inner being. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).
[^2274]: Second sticheron at the Aposticha of Little Vespers, Tone 2, with psalm verse Psalm 50:12 LXX: The descent of the divided tongues of fire resting upon the Apostles, enlightening without burning. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).
[^2275]: Third sticheron at the Aposticha of Little Vespers, Tone 2, with psalm verse Psalm 50:13 LXX: Prayer to the heavenly Comforter to sanctify and preserve all who proclaim Him as God. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2276]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha of Little Vespers (*Glory, both now*), Tone 1: The celebrated classical Byzantine pneumatological hymn detailing the gifts of the Holy Spirit: inspiring prophecies, ordaining priests, granting wisdom to fishermen, and convening the Church. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2277]: Troparion (Dismissal Troparion) of the Great Feast of Pentecost in Tone 8 (*Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God*): The central festal hymn glorifying Christ for revealing fishermen as all-wise through the descent of the Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2278]: Service opening of Great Vespers for the Great Feast of Holy Pentecost, celebrated on Saturday evening. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2279]: Rubric governing the stichologia of *Lord, I have cried* at Great Vespers, prescribing 10 stichera: 3 idiomela in Tone 1 repeated, followed by 5 idiomela in Tone 2 with repetitions to complete the decade. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2280]: First festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1 (sung twice): Solemn theological celebration of Pentecost as the realization of divine promise, completion of Christian hope, and revelation of the great Trinitarian mystery. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2281]: Second festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1: Christ renewing the Disciples with the gift of foreign tongues so they may preach the immortal Word throughout all the nations. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2282]: Third festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1: Repetition of the grand dogmatic hymn on the operations of the Holy Spirit in the hierarchy, wisdom, and universal assembly of the Church. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2283]: Fourth festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2 (sung twice): The famous liturgical confession (*We have seen the true Light*), chanted throughout the Byzantine Rite following Holy Communion, originating historically as a Pentecost sticheron. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).
[^2284]: Fifth festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: The historical continuity of divine revelation, from the prefigurations of the Old Testament Prophets to the outpouring of grace upon the Apostles. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).
[^2285]: Sixth festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: The personal offering of prayer and worship with genuflection before the invincible power of Christ at morning, midday, and evening (Ps. 54:18 LXX). Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).
[^2286]: Seventh festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: Corporate kneeling of souls and bodies by the assembly in the temple courts, praising the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit as one co-eternal God. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).
[^2287]: Eighth festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: Confession of the Consubstantial Trinity as proclaimed harmoniously by all Prophets, Apostles, and Holy Martyrs. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).
[^2288]: Festal Doxasticon at *Lord, I have cried* (*Glory, both now*), Tone 8, attributed to the Byzantine Emperor Leo VI the Wise (886–912): The monumental dogmatic anthem proclaiming the Thrice-Hypostatic Godhead and interpreting the Trisagion (*Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal*) in light of the three divine Persons. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).
[^2289]: Rubrics for the solemn Entrance (*Vkhod*) with incense, the ancient evening hymn *Phos Hilaron* (*O Joyful Light*), the Saturday evening Prokeimenon, and the three Old Testament Paroemia readings of Pentecost. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).
[^2290]: First Paroemia of Pentecost: Numbers 11:16-17, 24-29, recounting God placing the prophetic Spirit upon the seventy elders of Israel and Moses' memorable wish that all the Lord's people were prophets. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).
[^2291]: Second Paroemia of Pentecost: Joel 2:23-32, the principal Old Testament prophecy of the outpouring of the Holy Spirit upon all flesh, cited by St. Peter on the morning of Pentecost (Acts 2:16-21). Physical Leaf p548 (printed p. 542).
[^2292]: Third Paroemia of Pentecost: Ezekiel 36:24-28, the prophetic promise of the New Covenant, in which God takes away the heart of stone, bestows a new heart of flesh, and places His Holy Spirit within His people. Physical Leaf p548 (printed p. 542).
[^2293]: Stichera at the Litija of Pentecost, Tone 2: Three solemn idiomela celebrating the manifestation of the Holy Spirit in the Prophets, the bowing of knees in the courts of the Lord, and praise of the Co-unoriginate Trinity. Physical Leaf p548 (printed p. 542).
[^2294]: Doxasticon at the Litija (*Glory, both now*), Tone 8: Graphic hymnographic depiction of the amazement of the crowd in Jerusalem hearing the unlearned Galilean disciples speak mysteries in foreign tongues. Physical Leaf p548 (printed p. 542).
[^2295]: First sticheron at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 6: The astonishment and mockery of the onlookers mistaking the divine intoxication of the Holy Spirit for worldly drunkenness (Acts 2:13), countered by the steadfast prayer of the faithful. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).
[^2296]: Second sticheron at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 6, with psalm verse Psalm 50:12 LXX: The descent of the Spirit as an incomprehensible wonder transforming fishermen into heralds of salvation. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).
[^2297]: Third sticheron at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 6, with psalm verse Psalm 50:13 LXX: The venerable prayer to the Holy Spirit (*O Heavenly King, O Comforter*), integral to the opening prayers of every Byzantine liturgical service, originating as a festal Aposticha sticheron for Pentecost. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).
[^2298]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha of Great Vespers (*Glory, both now*), Tone 8: Masterpiece of Byzantine hymnographic typology contrasting the division and confusion of tongues at the Tower of Babel (Gen. 11:7-9) with the unifying harmony of tongues at Pentecost. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).
[^2299]: Dismissal Troparion of Pentecost, Tone 8 (*Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God*), chanted three times during the Artoklasia (blessing of the five loaves, wheat, wine, and oil) at the conclusion of Great Vespers. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).
[^2300]: Codicological and Typikon rubrics specifying the all-night vigil reading from the Acts of the Apostles, the order for celebrations without a vigil, Compline propers, Midnight Office, and the Sunday Triadicon Canon of the Octoechos. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).
[^2301]: Service opening of Sunday Matins for the Great Feast of Holy Pentecost, introduced with a floral engraved headpiece band. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).
[^2302]: First Sessional Hymn after the first Kathisma stichology at Matins, Tone 4 (Melodic model: *Joseph was amazed*): Veneration of Pentecost as the ultimate feast and fulfillment of divine promises, illuminating the disciples as mystagogues of the heavenly mysteries. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).
[^2303]: Rubric prescribing the patristic reading from St. John Chrysostom's Homilies on the Gospel of John (Homily 50 on John 7:37: "In the last day, that great day of the feast..."), divided into two sections. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).
[^2304]: Second Sessional Hymn after the second Kathisma stichology at Matins, Tone 4 (Melodic model: *Joseph was amazed*): The mystical Fountain of the Spirit descending as rivers of fire and a bedewing cloud upon the Apostles, bestowing regeneration through fire and water. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).
[^2305]: Polyeleos and Megalynarion (Praise) of Holy Pentecost: The assembly's solemn chant honoring Christ the Life-Giver and the Most Holy Spirit sent forth from the Father upon the holy Disciples. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).
[^2306]: Selected Psalm verses chanted during the Megalynarion of Pentecost, drawing upon Psalm 18:2, 32:6, 96:3, 103:30, 142:10, 50:12, 50:13, and 28:11 LXX to glorify the cosmic creative and sanctifying work of the Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).
[^2307]: The rubric *И҆ чте́нїе въ Бг҃осло́вѣ* appoints a patristic reading from the homilies of St. Gregory the Theologian (Nazianzen), specifically Oration 41 (*On Pentecost*), appointed for the festal Matins vigil.
[^2308]: Melodic model heading: *Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded secretly" (Повелѣ́нное та́йно)* matches Byzantine Greek *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς*.
[^2309]: Sessional hymn after the Polyeleos praising Christ's Resurrection, Ascension, and the sending of the Holy Spirit upon the Apostles as the mystical plectrum upon a musical harp (*ꙗ҆́кѡ гꙋ́сль мꙋсікі́йскаѧ*).
[^2310]: Second rubric appointing the continuation of the homily of St. Gregory the Theologian between the Sessional Hymn and the Anabathmoi.
[^2311]: Songs of Ascents (Anabathmoi): The first antiphon of Tone 4 (*Ѿ ю҆́ности моеѧ̀* / *From my youth*), appointed universally on great feasts of the Master.
[^2312]: Festal Matins Prokeimenon: Psalm 142:10 (LXX) (*Дх҃ъ тво́й бл҃гі́й наста́витъ мѧ̀ на зе́млю пра́вꙋ*).
[^2313]: Prokeimenon verse: Psalm 142:1 (LXX).
[^2314]: Pentecost Matins Gospel: John 20:19-23 (pericope 65), recounting Christ's appearance to the disciples in the Upper Room on the evening of the Resurrection, breathing on them and giving the Holy Spirit for the forgiveness of sins.
[^2315]: Liturgical rubric: The resurrectional hymn *Воскресе́нїе Хрїсто́во ви́дѣвше* (*Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ*) is omitted on Pentecost despite it being a Sunday, because the feast commemorates the descent of the Holy Spirit rather than the weekly resurrectional cycle. Psalm 50 is read immediately.
[^2316]: Post-Gospel idiomelon: Tone 6, *Царю́ небе́сный* (*O Heavenly King*), sung in place of the customary Sunday post-Gospel sticheron *Воскре́съ І҆исоу́съ ѿ гро́ба*.
[^2317]: Canon performance rubric: The two festal canons are chanted with their heirmoi doubled (sung twice each) by the alternating right and left choirs, and their troparia repeated to make 12.
[^2318]: Authorship and acrostic of the First Canon: Attributed to St. Cosmas the Melodist (Bishop of Maiuma, c. 675–c. 752); the Greek acrostic *Πεντηκοστὴν ἑορτάζομεν* forms an alphabet of praises for Pentecost.
[^2319]: Canon 1, Ode 1 Heirmos: Tone 7, referencing the defeat of Pharaoh in the Red Sea (Exod. 14:27-28; 15:1-19).
[^2320]: Canon 1, Ode 1, 1st troparion: Christ fulfilling the promise of sending the Comforter unto the Apostles.
[^2321]: Canon 1, Ode 1, 2nd troparion: Fulfillment of the Old Testament prophecies regarding the outpouring of the Holy Spirit (cf. Joel 2:28).
[^2322]: Canon 2 (Iambic) Acrostic: A four-line iambic trimeter dedicatory poem printed in both Greek and Church Slavonic diplomatic verse, dedicating the canon to the Word born of God and the Comforter.
[^2323]: Authorship of the Second Canon: Attributed in the Slavic tradition to *Кѵ́ръ І҆ѡа́ннъ А҆ркалі́йскїй* (St. John of Damascus, c. 676–749), distinguished by its classical Greek iambic meter.
[^2324]: Canon 2, Ode 1 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, referencing Moses veiled in the divine cloud on Mount Sinai receiving the Law (Exod. 19–20; 24:15-18).
[^2325]: Canon 2, Ode 1, 1st troparion: Christ promising that He will pour out ungrudging grace upon His friends from the Father's throne.
[^2326]: Canon 2, Ode 1, 2nd troparion: The descent of the Spirit in rushing wind and tongues of fire fulfilling the promise of Christ.
[^2327]: Katavasia rubric: Both heirmoi of Ode 1 (Cosmas's *He Who broke battles* and John's *Covered by the divine darkness*) are sung by the choirs.
[^2328]: Canon 1, Ode 3 Heirmos: Tone 7, Christ commanding the disciples to abide in Jerusalem until clothed with power from on high (Luke 24:49; Acts 1:4-8).
[^2329]: Canon 1, Ode 3, 1st troparion: The contrast between the division of tongues at Babel (Gen. 11:7-9) and their divine unification at Pentecost.
[^2330]: Canon 2, Ode 3 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, referencing the prayer of the Prophetess Hannah (1 Sam. 2:1-10; 1 Kgs. 2:1-10 LXX).
[^2331]: Canon 2, Ode 3, 1st troparion: Incomprehensible wisdom of God making unlettered fishermen eloquent theologians.
[^2332]: Canon 2, Ode 3, 2nd troparion: The Holy Spirit as Light proceeding from the Unbegotten Light of the Father, revealed through the Son.
[^2333]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3: Tone 8, *Повелѣ́нное та́йно*, praising the descent of the tongues of fire upon the Apostles in the Upper Room.
[^2334]: Canon 1, Ode 4 Heirmos: Tone 7, citing Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX) regarding the saving coming of Christ.
[^2335]: Canon 1, Ode 4, 1st troparion: The Holy Spirit speaking in the Prophets and now revealed to the Apostles.
[^2336]: Canon 1, Ode 4, 2nd troparion: The division of tongues of fire as the sovereign manifestation of the Father's power.
[^2337]: Canon 2, Ode 4 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, addressing Christ as King of kings and the proceeder from the Uncaused Father, sending the equal Spirit.
[^2338]: Canon 2, Ode 4, 1st troparion: The bath of regeneration flowing from Christ's pierced side (John 19:34; Titus 3:5), sealed with the warmth of the Spirit.
[^2339]: Canon 2, Ode 4, 2nd troparion: Worship of the Three Persons in one Essence at the outpouring of the Spirit.
[^2340]: Canon 2, Ode 4, 3rd troparion: Ministers of the Trinity illuminated with divine fire unto salvation.
[^2341]: Canon 1, Ode 5 Heirmos: Tone 7, citing Isaiah 26:18-19 (LXX) regarding the conception of the spirit of salvation.
[^2342]: Canon 1, Ode 5, 1st troparion: The Good Spirit of wisdom proceeding from the Father and revealed through the Son.
[^2343]: Canon 2, Ode 5 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, referencing the fire-inspired dew of the Spirit and the law going forth from Zion (Isa. 2:3).
[^2344]: Canon 2, Ode 5, 1st troparion: The Spirit proceeding unmastered from the Father and sealing the teaching of the Savior.
[^2345]: Canon 2, Ode 5, 2nd troparion: The Word preparing the minds of the Apostles as a clean dwelling for the Light of the Spirit.
[^2346]: Canon 1, Ode 6 Heirmos: Tone 7, the typological prayer of Jonah from the belly of the sea monster (Jonah 2:1-10).
[^2347]: Canon 1, Ode 6, 1st troparion: Outpouring of the Spirit upon all flesh (Joel 2:28; Acts 2:17).
[^2348]: Canon 2, Ode 6 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, Christ rising from the Virgin and snatching fallen Adam from corruption as Jonah was snatched from the sea beast.
[^2349]: Canon 2, Ode 6, 1st troparion: Supplication for the Holy Spirit to burn up the defilements of the adversary and cleanse the mind.
[^2350]: Canon 2, Ode 6, 2nd troparion: The Holy Spirit confirming the Apostles in Zion with fire-breathed inspiration.
[^2351]: Festal Kontakion: Tone 8, *Є҆гда̀ снизше́дъ ꙗ҆зы́ки слїѧ̀* (*When the Most High came down and confused the tongues*), contrasting the Tower of Babel (Gen. 11) with the unity of Pentecost (Acts 2).
[^2352]: Festal Ikos: Prayer unto Jesus for swift consolation and the indwelling presence of the All-Holy Spirit.
[^2353]: Synaxarion of Holy Pentecost: The official liturgical commentary on the historical and theological meaning of the fiftieth day.
[^2354]: Synaxarion commentary on the Jewish origins of the Feast of Weeks (Shavuot, Exod. 23:16; 34:22; Lev. 23:15-21; Deut. 16:9-12).
[^2355]: Synaxarion exposition of the three great pilgrimage feasts of Israel: Passover (Pesach), Pentecost (Shavuot), and the Feast of Booths / Tabernacles (Sukkot / Scenopegia, Lev. 23:33-43; Deut. 16:13-16).
[^2356]: Synaxarion exposition of the mystical significance of the number seven and the Jubilee year (*Їѡвнле́онъ*, Lev. 25:8-55) releasing debts and slaves every 50th year.
[^2357]: Typological interpretation of Scenopegia and the Feast of Tabernacles as an image of the general Resurrection and the reconstructed human tabernacle.
[^2358]: Patristic decision to dedicate the Monday of the Holy Spirit to the Third Person of the Trinity specifically, while Sunday honors the descent of the Spirit in the economy of the Trinity.
[^2359]: Canon 1, Ode 7 Heirmos: Tone 7, citing Daniel 3:26-56 (the Three Holy Youths in the Babylonian furnace).
[^2360]: Canon 1, Ode 7, 1st troparion: The mocking accusation of drunkenness (Acts 2:13) refuted by the preaching of the Trinity.
[^2361]: Canon 1, Ode 7, 2nd troparion: Orthodox theological confession of the Father, Word, and Spirit as one undivided dominion.
[^2362]: Canon 2, Ode 7 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, the music of Nebuchadnezzar's orchestra (Dan. 3:5-7) contrasted with the harmonious praise of the Holy Trinity.
[^2363]: Canon 2, Ode 7, 1st troparion: Refutation of the foolish ones who mistook apostolic ecstasy for wine.
[^2364]: Canon 2, Ode 7, 2nd troparion: The prophecy of Joel 2:28 (*I will pour out of My Spirit*) fulfilled at Pentecost.
[^2365]: Canon 2, Ode 7, 3rd troparion: The threefold grace revealing the Three Hypostases on the Lord's Day.
[^2366]: Canon 1, Ode 8 Heirmos: Tone 7, the unburned bush of Sinai (Exod. 3:2) and the three youths in the furnace (Dan. 3:57).
[^2367]: Canon 1, Ode 8, 1st troparion: The rushing blast from on high inspiring the fishermen to proclaim the wonders of God.
[^2368]: Canon 1, Ode 8, 2nd troparion: Spiritual ascent unto Mount Zion and the city of the Living God (Heb. 12:22).
[^2369]: Canon 2, Ode 8 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, the Threefold image of the Godhead bedewing the flame of the furnace.
[^2370]: Canon 2, Ode 8, 1st troparion: The Holy Spirit bringing to remembrance all the saving words of Christ (John 14:26).
[^2371]: Canon 2, Ode 8, 2nd troparion: Christ as the self-shining Light filling the Apostles with the bountiful Spirit.
[^2372]: Canon 2, Ode 8, 3rd troparion: The Spirit proceeding from the Father's bosom and sharing the eternal throne with the Incarnate Word.
[^2373]: Festal Refrain of Ode 9: *Велича́й дꙋшѐ моѧ̀, ѿ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ и҆ Сн҃а и҆сходѧ́щаго ст҃а́го Дх҃а* (*Magnify, O my soul, the Holy Spirit, Who proceeds from the Father and the Son*). Note the historical Ruthenian / Uniate recension reading incorporating the *Filioque* clause, standard in the 1907 Lviv Stauropegion edition pursuant to the Union of Brest and Roman liturgical confirmations for the Greek Catholic Church.
[^2374]: Canon 1, Ode 9 Heirmos: Tone 7, hymn of praise to the Virgin Theotokos who brought forth the Word without corruption.
[^2375]: Canon 1, Ode 9, 1st troparion: The ascent of Elijah in the fiery chariot (2 Kgs. 2:11; 4 Kgs. 2:11 LXX) prefiguring the descent of the fiery breath upon the Apostles.
[^2376]: Canon 1, Ode 9, 2nd troparion: The miracle of Pentecost where people of diverse nations understood the one apostolic preaching (Acts 2:6-11).
[^2377]: Second Festal Refrain of Ode 9: *Велича́й дꙋшѐ моѧ̀, въ трїе́хъ ли́цѣхъ сꙋ́щее є҆ди́но Бж҃ество̀* (*Magnify, O my soul, the One Godhead existing in three Persons*).
