[^2378]: Canon 2, Ode 9 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, *Ра́дꙋйсѧ Цр҃и́це* (*Rejoice, O Queen*), praising the Mother of God whose childbearing surpasses all human eloquence.
[^2379]: Canon 2, Ode 9, 1st troparion: The Virgin Mary bearing the healing Word Who now, seated at the right hand of the Father, sends the grace of the Spirit.
[^2380]: Canon 2, Ode 9, 2nd troparion: The transformation of believers through the outbreathing of God-flowing grace.
[^2381]: Katavasia rubric: Chanting both Heirmoi of Ode 9 together.
[^2382]: Melodic model heading: *Tone 3, Podoben: "Having adorned the heavens with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми)*.
[^2383]: Festal Exapostilarion 1: Citing the Holy Spirit proceeding from the Father and sent through the Son to the unlettered disciples.
[^2384]: Festal Exapostilarion 2: Trinitarian hymn proclaiming the Father, Word, and Spirit as uncreated Light.
[^2385]: Praises (Lauds) 1st Idiomelon: Tone 2, St. Luke's account of the descent of the Spirit in Acts 2:1-4.
[^2386]: Praises 2nd Idiomelon: Tone 2, dogmatic confession of the eternal, uncreated nature of the Holy Spirit, co-ranked with the Father and the Son.
[^2387]: Praises 3rd Idiomelon: Tone 2, the Holy Spirit as Light, Life, and Noetic Fountain, crowning prophets, apostles, and martyrs.
[^2388]: Post-Praises Doxasticon: Tone 6, the foundational invocation of the Holy Spirit, *Царю́ небе́сный* (*O Heavenly King*).
[^2389]: Festal Matins Dismissal: The proper dismissal formula for Pentecost mentioning the sending of the Holy Spirit in the form of fiery tongues.
[^2390]: First Hour rubric: Appointing the troparion and kontakion of Pentecost, followed by the final dismissal.
[^2391]: Divine Liturgy Typikon rubric: The festal antiphons of Pentecost replace the customary daily or Sunday antiphons.
[^2392]: Liturgy Antiphon 1: Psalm 18:2, 3, 5 (LXX) with the refrain *Through the intercessions of the Theotokos...*
[^2393]: Liturgy Antiphon 2: Psalm 19:2, 3, 5 (LXX) with the proper festal refrain *Save us, O Good Comforter, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia*.
[^2394]: Liturgy Antiphon 2 conclusion: Chanting the Christological hymn *Only-Begotten Son and Immortal Word of God* (*Е҆диноро́дный Сы́не*).
[^2395]: Liturgy Antiphon 3: Psalm 20:2, 3, 4 (LXX) with the festal troparion *Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...*
[^2396]: Festal Troparion of Pentecost: Tone 8, *Бл҃гослове́нъ є҆сѝ Хрⷭ҇тѐ Бж҃е на́шъ* (*Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast revealed the fishermen to be all-wise...*).
[^2397]: Liturgy Little Entrance Verse (Introit / Eisodikon): Psalm 20:14 (LXX) (*Вознеси́сѧ Гдⷭ҇и си́лою твое́ю*).
[^2398]: Liturgy Festal Kontakion: Tone 8, *Є҆гда̀ снизше́дъ ꙗ҆зы́ки слїѧ̀*.
[^2399]: Baptismal Hymn instead of Trisagion: Galatians 3:27 (*Є҆ли́цы во Хрⷭ҇та̀ крⷭ҇ти́стесѧ*), sung on great feasts that were historically baptismal synaxes.
[^2400]: Liturgy Prokeimenon: Tone 8, Psalm 18:5, 2 (LXX), sung daily through the Apodosis of Pentecost.
[^2401]: Liturgy Epistle: Acts 2:1-11 (pericope 3), St. Peter and the Apostles at Pentecost.
[^2402]: Liturgy Alleluia: Tone 1, Psalm 32:6, 13 (LXX).
[^2403]: Liturgy Gospel: John 7:37-52; 8:12 (pericope 27), Christ preaching rivers of living water flowing from the believer's belly on the last great day of the Feast of Tabernacles.
[^2404]: Liturgy Megalynarion replacement: The Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Iambic Canon (*Ра́дꙋйсѧ Цр҃и́це* / *Rejoice, O Queen*) is sung in place of *It is truly meet* (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*) until the Apodosis.
[^2405]: Liturgy Communion Verse (Communion Hymn): Psalm 142:10 (LXX) (*Дх҃ъ тво́й бл҃гі́й наста́витъ мѧ̀ на зе́млю пра́вꙋ*).
[^2406]: Liturgy conclusion rubric: The Ninth Hour is read after the dismissal of the Liturgy, preceding the immediate celebration of Pentecost Kneeling Vespers.
[^2407]: Sunday of Pentecost Kneeling Vespers (*Вечерня с коленопреклонением*): Celebrated immediately after the Divine Liturgy or in the afternoon, during which the Church kneels for the first time since Pascha and offers the seven solemn Kneeling Prayers.
[^2408]: Rubric on the fast ringing of bells: *Зна́менаетъ ско́рѡ* indicates a rapid peal of the church bells to call the faithful to the service of the Kneeling Prayers.
[^2409]: Vespers Opening: The priest intones the initial blessing, the reader chants *O Heavenly King* (solemnly returning to the beginning of services), and the 103rd Psalm (LXX) is read.
[^2410]: Great Litany (*Вели́каѧ є҆кте́нїа*): Intoned with special Pentecostal petitions added after the customary supplications.
[^2411]: Hierarchical commemoration: Standard Ruthenian formulary commemorating the Metropolitan Archbishop of Lviv (Andrey Sheptytsky, 1865–1944) and the local Eparchial Bishop (e.g. Hryhoriy Khomyshyn of Stanyslaviv or Kostiantyn Chekhovych of Peremyshl).
[^2412]: State petition: Diplomatic commemoration for Emperor Franz Joseph I of Austria-Hungary (*Бл҃говѣ́рный І҆мпера́торъ*), King of Galicia and Lodomeria, together with his Imperial Court (*Пала́та*) and the Armed Forces (*Во́и*).
[^2413]: Special Pentecost Vespers Petitions: A series of twelve unique petitions invoking the grace and illumination of the Holy Spirit, preparation for kneeling, deliverance from demonic bonds, and divine sanctification.
[^2414]: Commemoration of the Theotokos concluding the petitions of the Great Litany before the priest's exclamation.
[^2415]: Printed folio 555 (фн҃є). Concluding exclamation of the Great Litany of Pentecost Vespers (*Я́кѡ подоба́етъ тебѣ̀ всѧ́каѧ сла́ва...*), completing the extensive kneeling-eve litany begun on leaf p559 and continued on p560.
[^2416]: Rubric for the Lucernarium psalms (*Го́споди воззва́хъ* / Ps. 140 LXX) with 6 appointed stichos-verses.
[^2417]: Idiomelic stichera (*стїхи̑ры самогла́сны*) for Pentecost Sunday Vespers, Tone 4.
[^2418]: The first idiomelic sticheron (*Пресла́внаѧ дне́сь, ви́дѣша всѝ ꙗ҆зы́цы...*), celebrating the descent of the Holy Spirit upon the Apostles as recorded in Acts 2:1-4.
[^2419]: Incipit of the second idiomelic sticheron (*Дх҃ъ ст҃ы́й бѣ̀ оу҆́бѡ при́снѡ...*), sung to complete the 6 verses by repetition.
[^2420]: Incipit of the third idiomelic sticheron (*Дх҃ъ ст҃ы́й, Свѣ́тъ, и҆ Живо́тъ...*).
[^2421]: Rubric indicating that the three idiomelic stichera are repeated to fill out the 6 stichoi (*Повторя́юще ꙗ҆̀*).
[^2422]: Doxasticon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (*Цр҃ю̀ нбⷭ҇ный, Оу҆тѣ́шителю...*), invoking the Paraclete.
[^2423]: Cross-reference rubric (*Пи́саны вы́ше на хвали́техъ*): this sticheron was previously appointed and printed in full at the Praises of Pentecost Matins (fol. 554 / physical leaf p560).
[^2424]: Rubric for the Little Entrance with the censer (*Вхо́дъ съ кади́ломъ*), customary at Great Vespers.
[^2425]: The ancient Lucernarium hymn *O Gladsome Light* (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй* / Phos Hilaron).
[^2426]: Great Prokeimenon (*Прокі́менъ вели́кїй*), Tone 7.
[^2427]: Psalm 76:14 LXX: *Who is a god great as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders* (*Кто̀ Бг҃ъ ве́лїй ꙗ҆́кѡ Бг҃ъ на́шъ; ты̀ є҆сѝ Бг҃ъ творѧ́й чꙋдеса̀*).
[^2428]: Psalm 76:15 LXX: *Thou hast made Thy power known among the peoples* (*Сказа́лъ є҆сѝ въ лю́дехъ си́лу тво́ю*).
[^2429]: Psalm 76:11 LXX: *And I said: Now have I begun; this is the change of the right hand of the Most High* (*И҆ рѣ́хъ: ны́нѣ нача́хъ, сїѧ̀ и҆змѣ́на десни́цы вы́шнѧгѡ*).
[^2430]: Psalm 76:12 LXX: *I remembered the works of the Lord; for I will remember Thy wonders from the beginning* (*Помѧнꙋ́хъ дѣла̀ Гдⷭ҇нѧ, ꙗ҆́кѡ помѧнꙋ̀ ѿ нача́ла чꙋдеса̀ твоѧ̑*).
[^2431]: Rubric introducing the first station of the Pentecost Kneeling Prayers (*Колѣ́нопрекло́нныѧ моли́твы*).
[^2432]: Diaconal bidding commanding the congregation to kneel (*Па́ки и҆ па́ки прикло́нше колѣ́на...*), marking the solemn return of bodily genuflection after the fifty-day paschal prohibition.
[^2433]: Detailed rubrical instruction specifying that the clergy and people bow their knees to the ground with uncovered heads, while the celebrant reads the prayers aloud from the sanctuary facing the congregation.
[^2434]: Printed folio 556 (фн҃ѕ). Opening apophatic invocations of the First Kneeling Prayer attributed to St. Basil the Great (*Пречи́сте, нескве́рне, безнача́льне...*).
[^2435]: Allusion to 1 Timothy 6:16: *dwelling in the light which no man can approach unto* (*во свѣ́тѣ живы́й непристꙋ́пномъ*).
[^2436]: Scriptural citation of Acts 4:24 (cf. Ps. 145:6 LXX): *Who madest heaven, and earth, and the sea, and all that in them is*.
[^2437]: Liturgical invocation addressing the Eternal Father through His Only-begotten Son Jesus Christ.
[^2438]: Affirmation of the Nicene-Constantinopolitan Creed regarding the Incarnation of the Son of God by the Holy Spirit and the Virgin Mary.
[^2439]: Theological rationale for the posture of kneeling: Christ taught by word and deed, giving an example through His Passion for humble sinners to offer prayer with bowed necks and bended knees.
[^2440]: Commemoration of the historical descent of the Holy Spirit on the fiftieth day (Acts 2:1-4).
[^2441]: Psalm 21:11 LXX: *Unto Thee was I cast from the womb: from my mother's belly Thou art my God* (*къ тебѣ̀ привѣ́ржени є҆смы̀ ѿ оу҆тро́бы, ѿ чре́ва ма́тере на́шеѧ, ты̀ є҆сѝ Бг҃ъ на́шъ*).
[^2442]: Scriptural citations: Psalm 24:7 LXX (*Remember not the sins of my youth, nor my ignorance*) and Psalm 18:13 LXX (*Cleanse Thou me from my secret faults*).
[^2443]: Psalm 70:9 LXX: *Cast me not off in the time of old age; forsake me not when my strength fails*.
[^2444]: Text transitions across the bottom of leaf p562 into the top of leaf p563 via catchword *на предстоѧ́щыѧ*.
[^2445]: Printed folio 557 (фн҃з). Conclusion of the first kneeling prayer, petitioning for angelic protection, remission of sins, and deliverance from demonic snare.
[^2446]: Rubric introducing the supplementary prayer of the first kneeling station (*Присовокупля́етъ же и҆ сїю̀ моли́твꙋ*).
[^2447]: Opening thanksgiving of the second prayer (*Бл҃гослове́нъ є҆сѝ Гдⷭ҇и, Влдко, Вседержи́телю...*), praising God for guiding the faithful through the daylight hours into the evening.
[^2448]: Petitions for a peaceful, sinless night, protection through angelic wardens, and preservation by the prayers of the Theotokos and all the saints.
[^2449]: Rubric for the diaconal litany concluding the first kneeling station.
[^2450]: Little litany petitions with the special addition of *возста́ви* ("raise us up"), signaling the faithful to rise from kneeling.
[^2451]: Traditional Marian commemoration (*Пресвѧтꙋ́ю, пречи́стꙋю...*) concluding the litany.
[^2452]: Rubric for the priestly exclamation.
[^2453]: Sacerdotal exclamation: *Я́кѡ твоѐ є҆́сть, є҆́же ми́ловати, и҆ спаса́ти на́съ...*
[^2454]: Printed folio 558 (фн҃и). Rubric for the Litany of Fervent Supplication (Augmented Litany / *Сꙋгꙋ́баѧ є҆ктенїа̀*).
[^2455]: Opening petition of the Fervent Litany (*Рце́мъ всѝ ѿ всеѧ̀ дꙋшѝ...*).
[^2456]: Codicological abbreviation: *И҆ прѡ́чаѧ* ("And the rest"), directing the deacon to recite the standard petitions of the Augmented Litany.
[^2457]: Rubric for the priestly exclamation of the Augmented Litany.
[^2458]: Priestly doxological exclamation (*Я́кѡ млⷭ҇тивъ и҆ чл҃вѣколю́бецъ Бг҃ъ є҆сѝ...*).
[^2459]: Rubric introducing the second station of kneeling prayers.
[^2460]: Second diaconal call to kneel (*Па́ки и҆ па́ки прикло́нше колѣ̑на...*).
[^2461]: Rubrical instruction reminding the faithful to kneel once more as prescribed for the first station.
[^2462]: Rubric for the reading of the second kneeling prayer by the priest (*Чте́тъ Їере́й в-ю моли́твꙋ во оу҆слы́шанїе всѣ́хъ*).
[^2463]: Opening theological invocation of the Second Kneeling Prayer (*Гдⷭ҇и І҆и҃се Хрⷭ҇тѐ Бж҃е на́шъ...*), addressing the Incarnate Word Who poured out the gift of the Holy Spirit in fiery tongues upon the Apostles.
[^2464]: Allusion to Hebrews 1:3 (*the brightness of His glory, and the express image of His person*); text continues onto leaf p565 via catchword *ми пꙋ́ть,*.
[^2465]: Printed folio 559 (фн҃ѳ). Scriptural allusions to Psalm 142:8 LXX (*Cause me to know the way wherein I should walk*), Psalm 50:12 LXX (*renew a right spirit within me*), and Psalm 50:14 LXX (*uphold me with Thy free/sovereign Spirit*).
[^2466]: Evangelical reference to John 14:13-14 and John 16:23: Christ's promise that whatever is asked in His Name shall be granted by the Father.
[^2467]: Confession of Christ's sinless Incarnation and His condescension toward those who bow before Him.
[^2468]: Scriptural citations: Psalm 16:8 LXX (*Hide me under the shadow of Thy wings*) and Psalm 137:8 LXX (*Forsake not the works of Thine own hands*).
[^2469]: Psalm 43:21 LXX (*If we have stretched out our hands to a strange god*) and Psalm 140:2 LXX (*Let my prayer be set forth before Thee as incense*).
[^2470]: Rubric introducing the supplementary prayer of the second kneeling station (*Присовокупля́етъ же и҆ сїю̀*).
[^2471]: Opening of the supplementary prayer (*Гдⷭ҇и Гдⷭ҇и, и҆збавлѧ́ей на́съ ѿ всѧ́кїѧ стрѣлы̀...*), quoting Psalm 90:5-6 LXX (*the arrow that flies by day, the pestilence that walks in darkness*) and Psalm 140:2 LXX (*the lifting up of my hands as the evening sacrifice*). Text continues onto leaf p566 via catchword *и҆спыта́нїа*.
[^2472]: Printed folio 560 (фѯ). Scriptural citations: Psalm 118:120 LXX (*Nail my flesh with the fear of Thee*) and Colossians 3:5 (*Mortify therefore your members which are upon the earth*).
[^2473]: Rubric for the diaconal litany concluding the second kneeling station.
[^2474]: Abbreviated Marian petition (*Пресвѧтꙋ́ю, пречи́стꙋю, пребл҃гослове́ннꙋю...*).
[^2475]: Rubric for the priestly exclamation over the prayer (*Їере́й возгла́съ моли́твѣ*).
[^2476]: Trinitarian exclamation (*Бл҃говоле́нїемъ и҆ бл҃гостїю є҆диноро́днагѡ Сн҃а твоегѡ̀...*).
[^2477]: The Vespers hymn *Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin* (*Сподо́би Гдⷭ҇и въ ве́черъ се́й...*).
[^2478]: Rubric introducing the third station of kneeling prayers.
[^2479]: Rubrical direction for the faithful to bow their knees for the third time.
[^2480]: Rubric for the celebrant reading the third kneeling prayer (*Їере́й чте́тъ тре́тїю моли́твꙋ*).
[^2481]: Opening invocation of the Third Kneeling Prayer (*Приснотекꙋ́щїй, живо́тный, и҆ просвѣти́тельный И҆сто́чниче...*), addressed to Christ as the Conqueror of Hades and binder of Satan. Text continues onto leaf p567 via catchword *твое́ю*.
[^2482]: Printed folio 561 (фѯа). Solemn supplication for the departed (*оу҆поко́й дꙋ́шы рабѡ́въ твои́хъ...*), remembering parents, siblings, kinsmen, and all the faithful reposed.
[^2483]: Doxology of God's sovereign dominion over life and death, citing 1 Kingdoms (1 Sam.) 2:6 LXX: *The Lord kills, and makes alive: He brings down to Hades, and brings up*.
[^2484]: Commemoration of the Pentecostal descent of the Holy Spirit in fiery tongues, inaugurating the Apostolic preaching of the Orthodox faith.
[^2485]: The unique theological hallmark of the Pentecost Kneeling Prayers: intercession for souls detained in Hades (*ѡ҆ и҆́же во а҆́дѣ держи́мыхъ*), quoting Psalm 22:2 LXX (*in a place of green pasture*) and Isaiah 35:10 LXX (*sorrow and sighing shall flee away*). Text continues onto leaf p568 via catchword *восхва́лѧтъ*.
[^2486]: Printed folio 562 (фѯв). Citation of Psalm 113:25 LXX: *The dead shall not praise Thee, O Lord, nor any that go down into Hades*.
[^2487]: Rubric introducing the second prayer of the third kneeling station (*Присовокупля́етъ и҆ сїю̀ моли́твꙋ*).
[^2488]: Opening of the prayer (*Бж҃е вели́кїй, и҆ вѣ́чный, ст҃ы́й, человѣколюби́вый...*), giving thanks for the gift of standing before God to praise His wonders.
[^2489]: Romans 13:12: *let us put on the armor of light* (*ѡ҆дѣ́явшеся ѻ҆рꙋ́жїемъ свѣ́та*).
[^2490]: Patristic meditation on mortality and resurrection, alluding to Hebrews 2:14-18: Christ partook of human flesh and blood to taste suffering and trial, thereby raising humanity into His divine impassibility.
[^2491]: Petitions for the reposed of every degree of kinship, praying that their names be written in the Book of Life. Text continues onto leaf p569 via catchword *нѣ́дрѣхъ*.
[^2492]: Printed folio 563 (фѯг). Invocation of the Patriarchal resting-place: *in the bosoms of Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob* (cf. Luke 16:22), and the resurrection of bodies at the Last Day.
[^2493]: Scriptural citation of Job 14:4-5 LXX: *Who shall be clean from filth? Not one, even if his life be but one day upon the earth*.
[^2494]: Comprehensive plea for forgiveness of all sins—conscious or forgotten, in word, deed, or thought—and for a peaceful end before the Judgment-seat of Christ.
[^2495]: Rubric introducing the third prayer of the third kneeling station (*Присовокупля́етъ и҆ сїю̀ моли́твꙋ*).
[^2496]: Opening of the evening prayer (*Бж҃е вели́кїй и҆ вы́шнїй...*), citing Genesis 1:4 and Psalm 135:8-9 LXX regarding the creation of sun, moon, and stars; Psalm 140:2 LXX (*incense before Thee*); and Psalm 90:5-6 LXX (*fear of the night*). Text continues onto leaf p570 via catchword *дїа́волскагѡ*.
[^2497]: Printed folio 564 (фѯд). Petitions for peaceful sleep free from diabolical delusion, and for joyful rising to praise God at night.
[^2498]: Rubric for the diaconal litany concluding the third kneeling station.
[^2499]: Little litany with *возста́ви* ("raise us up"), concluding all three stations of kneeling prayers.
[^2500]: Priestly exclamation: *Ты́ бо є҆сѝ оу҆покое́нїе дꙋ́шъ и҆ тѣле́съ на́шихъ...*
[^2501]: Rubric for the Deacon's Evening Litany of Completion (*И҆спо́лнимъ вече́рнюю моли́твꙋ*).
[^2502]: Opening petition of the Evening Litany of Completion (*И҆спо́лнимъ вече́рнюю моли́твꙋ Гдⷭ҇ви*).
[^2503]: Summary incipits of the standard evening petitions: angel of peace, pardon and remission, good and profitable things, peaceful Christian ending.
[^2504]: Concluding Marian commemoration of the evening litany.
[^2505]: Rubric for the priestly exclamation of the evening litany.
[^2506]: Priestly exclamation: *Я́кѡ бл҃гъ и҆ чл҃вѣколю́бецъ Бг҃ъ є҆сѝ...*
[^2507]: Liturgical greeting of peace (*Ми́ръ всѣ̑мъ*).
[^2508]: Bidding to the Prayer at the Bowing of Heads (*Главы̑ на́шѧ Гдⷭ҇ви приклони́те*).
[^2509]: Printed folio 565 (фѯ҃є). Rubric for the priestly Prayer at the Bowing of Heads concluding Great Vespers of Holy Pentecost (*І҆ере́й чте́тъ мл҃твꙋ ѡ҆бы́чнꙋю та́йнѡ*), said secretly while the congregation remains bowed following the diaconal bidding (*Главы̑ на́шѧ Гдⷭ҇ви приклони́те*).
[^2510]: Text of the priestly Prayer at the Bowing of Heads (*Гдⷭ҇и Бж҃е на́шъ, приклони́вый нб҃са̀...*), invoking Psalm 143:5 LXX (*Bow Thy heavens, O Lord, and come down*), petitioning for deliverance from diabolical machinations, vain thoughts, and evil memories during the evening and the approaching night.
[^2511]: Sacerdotal exclamation concluding the Prayer at the Bowing of Heads: *Бꙋ́ди держа́ва ца́рствїѧ твоегѡ̀ бл҃гослове́на и҆ препросла́влена...* (*Blessed and glorified be the majesty of Thy Kingdom...*).
[^2512]: Rubric directing both choirs to assemble together (*соше́дшесѧ ѻ҆́ба ли́ка*) for the chanting of the Aposticha stichera (*На стїхо́внѣ*), idiomela in Tone 3.
[^2513]: First idiomelic sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 3 (*Ны́нѣ въ зна́менїе всѣ̑мъ...*), contrasting the unbelief of the Jews according to the flesh with the calling of the Gentiles who worship the Holy Spirit with bowed hearts and bended knees.
[^2514]: First appointed psalm verse at the Aposticha: Psalm 50:12 LXX (*Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me*).
[^2515]: Second idiomelic sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 3 (*Ны́нѣ оу҆тѣши́тельный Дх҃ъ...*), commemorating the outpouring of the Paraclete upon the Apostles and His descent in the likeness of fire.
[^2516]: Second appointed psalm verse at the Aposticha: Psalm 50:13 LXX (*Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me*).
[^2517]: Third idiomelic sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 3 (*Ны́нѣ ѡ҆блача́тсѧ держа́вою Хрⷭ҇то́вою...*), citing Christ's promise that the Apostles would be clothed with power from on high (Luke 24:49) to proclaim the simple, co-eternal, and three-hypostatic Nature of God.
[^2518]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha, Tone 8 (*Прїиди́те лю́дїе, трїѷпоста́сномꙋ Бж҃ествꙋ̀ поклони́мсѧ...*), a monumental dogmatic hymn of the Byzantine Church attributed to the Emperor Leo the Wise (886–912), developing the theology of the Trisagion in light of the economic mission of the Holy Trinity.
[^2519]: Rubric for the conclusion of Vespers: Canticle of Simeon (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши* / Luke 2:29-32), Trisagion Prayers, and the Dismissal Troparion.
[^2520]: Dismissal Troparion, Tone 8 (*Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ безнача́льна, Сн҃а собезнача́льна...*), daring cherubically to proclaim the Thrice-Holy hymn unto the consubstantial Trinity.
[^2521]: Printed folio 566 (фѯ҃ѕ). Doxasticon troparion of Holy Pentecost, Tone 8 (*Бл҃гослове́нъ є҆сѝ Хрⷭ҇тѐ Бж҃е на́шъ...*), hymning Christ for revealing fishermen as all-wise through the descent of the Holy Spirit.
[^2522]: Solemn Dismissal (*ѿпꙋ́стъ*) of Pentecost Vespers (*И҆́же ѿ Ѻ҆ч҃ихъ и҆ Бж҃твенныхъ нѣ́дръ и҆стощи́вый себѐ...*), recounting Christ's voluntary Kenosis, Incarnation, Deification of human nature, Ascension, and sending of the consubstantial and co-eternal Spirit upon the Apostles.
[^2523]: Headpiece marking the service of Small Compline (*на Повече́рїи*) on the evening of Pentecost Sunday.
[^2524]: Rubric introducing the Canon to the Most Holy and Life-Creating Spirit (*Канѡ́нъ къ прест҃о́мꙋ Дх҃ꙋ*), composed by St. Theophanes the Branded (Graptos, c. 778–845), with the acrostic: *Дх҃а пою̀ Созда́вшаго всю̀ тва́рь* ("I sing the Spirit Who created all creation").
[^2525]: Ode 1 Heirmos, Tone 1 (*Го́рькїѧ рабо́ты и҆зба́вльсѧ І҆и҃ль...*), recalling Israel's deliverance from Egyptian bondage through the Red Sea (Exod. 14:21-31).
[^2526]: Appointed festal refrain for the canon troparia: *Сла́ва тебѣ̀ Бж҃е на́шъ, сла́ва тебѣ̀* ("Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee").
[^2527]: Ode 1, first troparion (*Бж҃е́ственный Дш҃е ст҃ы́й...*), imploring the Holy Spirit for His light-bearing gift to glorify Him together with the Father and the Son.
[^2528]: Ode 1, second troparion (*И҆́же оу҆́мнымъ си́ламъ...*), petitioning the Comforter to cleanse all intellectual defilement and grant holiness.
[^2529]: Ode 1, third troparion (*И҆сто́чниче жи́зни вѣ́рꙋемый...*), invoking the Holy Spirit as the wellspring of divine life and stream of goodness to resurrect the deadened mind.
[^2530]: Printed folio 567 (фѯ҃з). Ode 1, Theotokion (*Хра́мъ ꙗ҆́кѡ была̀ є҆сѝ Бж҃їй...*), addressing the Virgin as the temple of God overshadowed by the Holy Spirit (Luke 1:35).
[^2531]: Ode 3 Heirmos, Tone 1 (*Пре́жде вѣ̑къ ѿ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ рожде́нномꙋ...*), confessing the eternal generation of the Son and His virginal conception in the fullness of time.
[^2532]: Ode 3, first troparion (*И҆́мѧ по є҆стествꙋ̀...*), declaring that the Holy Spirit works by divine nature among the angelic orders, instructing them to chant the Trisagion.
[^2533]: Ode 3, second troparion (*Пѣ́сньми немо́лчными...*), joining with the incorporeal hosts to praise the grace of the Spirit manifested upon the Apostles in the rushing mighty wind (Acts 2:2).
[^2534]: Ode 3, third troparion (*Є҆ди́нꙋ вла́сть, и҆ Бж҃тво̀ є҆ди́но...*), confessing one dominion, power, and kingdom in the Holy Trinity.
[^2535]: Ode 3, Theotokion (*Колеснѝца свѣтоно́снаѧ...*), praising the Theotokos as higher than the Cherubim, bearing the uncircumscribed God upon her arms.
[^2536]: Ode 4 Heirmos, Tone 1 (*Же́злъ и҆з̾ ко́рене І҆ессе́ова...*), drawing upon the messianic prophecies of Isaiah 11:1 and Habakkuk 3:3 regarding the Incarnation of the Word from the Virgin.
[^2537]: Ode 4, first troparion (*Вели́каѧ да́рꙋй на́мъ Дх҃ъ всест҃ы́й...*), praising the Spirit as divine, benevolent, deifying, sanctifying, and self-governing.
[^2538]: Ode 4, second troparion (*На прⷭ҇то́лѣ Хрⷭ҇тѐ Ѻ҆́ч҃ескомъ сѣ́дъ...*), recalling Christ's promise from the Father's right hand to send down the Paraclete, proceeding from the Father.
[^2539]: Ode 4, third troparion (*Гл҃ати бꙋ́дꙋщаѧ ꙗ҆зы́къ Прⷪ҇рѡ́къ...*), uniting the Old Testament prophetic inspiration with the Pentecostal proclamation of the Apostles.
[^2540]: Ode 4, Theotokion (*Врата̀ оу҆́мнагѡ свѣ́та...*), invoking the Theotokos as the gate of spiritual light (cf. Ezek. 44:1-2) through whom Christ appeared in the flesh.
[^2541]: Ode 5 Heirmos, Tone 1 (*Бг҃ъ сы́й ми́ра, Ѻ҆ц҃ъ щедро́тъ...*), quoting Isaiah 9:6 LXX (*Angel of Great Counsel*) and 2 Corinthians 1:3 (*Father of mercies*).
[^2542]: Ode 5, first troparion (*Ѽ Дш҃е премꙋ́дрости, и҆ стра́ха Бж҃їѧ...*), invoking the sevenfold gifts of the Spirit enumerated in Isaiah 11:2-3.
[^2543]: Printed folio 568 (фѯ҃и). Ode 5, second troparion (*И҆́же всѧ̑ содержа́й, и҆ всѣ́хъ сы́й Гдⷭ҇ь...*), praying for divine preservation, holiness, and illumination.
[^2544]: Ode 5, third troparion (*И҆́же дре́вле зако́нъ начерта́вый Мѡѷсе́ю...*), contrasting the Sinaitic Decalogue engraved on stone with the law of the New Covenant inscribed by the Spirit upon human hearts (Jer. 31:33, 2 Cor. 3:3).
[^2545]: Ode 5, Theotokion (*Кла́твꙋ Е҆́ѵинꙋ всѣ́хъ Мт҃ре...*), proclaiming the reversal of Eve's primal curse through the Virgin's virginal childbearing.
[^2546]: Ode 6 Heirmos, Tone 1 (*И҆з̾ оу҆тро́бы І҆ѡ́нꙋ младе́нца...*), interpreting the preservation of Jonah in the whale's belly (Jonah 2:1-10) as a type of the virgin birth of Christ without violation of her virginity.
[^2547]: Ode 6, first troparion (*Твои̑мъ Оу҆чени́кѡмъ и҆сполнѧ́ѧ ѡ҆бѣща́нїе...*), celebrating the fulfillment of Christ's promise through the transmission of fiery tongues to teach the nations.
[^2548]: Ode 6, second troparion (*Прїидѝ къ на́мъ Дш҃е ст҃ы́й...*), addressing the Holy Spirit as the mystical river of the Godhead (cf. Ps. 45:5 LXX, Rev. 22:1) proceeding from the Father through the Son.
[^2549]: Ode 6, third troparion (*Спасѝ Оу҆тѣ́шителю, вѣ́рнѡ пою́щихъ...*), beseeching the Paraclete to make the faithful into unblemished spiritual mirrors of His divine light.
[^2550]: Ode 6, Theotokion (*Ве́сь ли́къ прⷪ҇ро́ческїй...*), hymning the fulfillment of all prophetic shadows in the Incarnation of God the Word.
[^2551]: Sessional Hymn of the Canon, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (*Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва*), meditating on the descent of fiery tongues at Pentecost (Acts 2:1-13).
[^2552]: Ode 7 Heirmos, Tone 1 (*Ѻ҆́троцы бл҃гоче́стїю совоспита́ни...*), invoking the steadfast faith of the Three Holy Children in the furnace of Babylon (Dan. 3:19-25).
[^2553]: Printed folio 569 (фѯ҃ѳ). Ode 7, first troparion (*Ны́нѣ ѡ҆бѣтова́нїа Хрⷭ҇то́ва предложе́нїе...*), proclaiming the fulfillment of Christ's pledge through the manifestation of the Holy Spirit.
[^2554]: Ode 7, second troparion (*Дре́вле оу҆́бѡ раздѣли́сѧ согла́сїе...*), contrasting the confusion and division of tongues at Babel (Gen. 11:7-9) with the holy harmony granted by the Spirit at Pentecost.
[^2555]: Ode 7, third troparion (*Свы́ше носи́мꙋ дохнове́нїю...*), praising the heavenly rushing wind of the Spirit that empowered the Apostles to announce God's wonders.
[^2556]: Ode 7, Theotokion (*Ѻ҆́бразъ твоегѡ̀ Ржⷭ҇тва̀...*), seeing the unconsumed Three Youths in the furnace as a prefiguration of the Virgin bearing the uncontainable fire of Divinity.
[^2557]: Ode 8 Heirmos, Tone 1 (*Чꙋ́да преесте́ственнагѡ росода́тельнаѧ...*), praising the dew-shedding furnace of Babylon (Dan. 3:49-50) as an image of the virginal womb preserving intact the divine fire.
[^2558]: Ode 8, first troparion (*Ст҃ы́й Дш҃е происходѧ́й ѿ Бг҃а...*), confessing the Holy Spirit's eternal procession and invoking His sanctifying illumination.
[^2559]: Ode 8, second troparion (*Дае́ши пода́телю, ꙗ҆́кѡ Бл҃годѣ́тель...*), hymning the Paraclete as the inexhaustible fountain and abyss of divine goodness.
[^2560]: Ode 8, third troparion (*Гдⷭ҇ь животворѧ́щь є҆́сть Дх҃ъ...*), affirming the Nicene-Constantinopolitan confession: the Holy Spirit is Lord and Giver of life, self-moved, self-governing, and unoriginate.
[^2561]: Ode 8, Theotokion (*Кто̀ твоемꙋ̀ премно́жествꙋ...*), marveling at the voluntary poverty and condescension of the pre-eternal Word (2 Cor. 8:9).
[^2562]: Ode 9 Heirmos, Tone 1 (*Ра́дꙋйсѧ дѣ́вственная Похвало̀...*), addressing the Theotokos as the pride of virginity and immaculate Mother magnified by all creation.
[^2563]: Ode 9, first troparion (*Сѐ къ на́мъ Оу҆тѣ́шителѧ и҆но́го низпосла́лъ є҆сѝ...*), citing Christ's promise of "another Comforter" (John 14:16), consubstantial with the Word and co-enthroned with the Father.
[^2564]: Ode 9, second troparion (*Спасѝ ѿ и҆скꙋше́нїа Оу҆тѣ́шителю...*), praying to the Paraclete for deliverance from temptation for all who confess His pre-eternal being.
[^2565]: Printed folio 570 (фѯ҃). Ode 9, third troparion (*Прїидѝ къ на́мъ Оу҆тѣ́шителю...*), invoking the presence of the Comforter to grant fullness of divine consolation.
[^2566]: Ode 9, Theotokion (*Невѣ́сто Бж҃їѧ, Всенепоро́чнаѧ...*), beseeching the Bride of God to deliver her faithful servants from trials through her prayers.
[^2567]: Rubrical directions concluding Compline: singing the ninth heirmos in place of *It is truly meet* (*Достойно есть*), followed by the Trisagion Prayers and the Festal Kontakion.
[^2568]: Headpiece and rubrics for the Midnight Office (*на Полꙋ́нощницѣ*) of Bright Monday of the Holy Spirit (*Въ Понедѣ́лникъ ст҃а́гѡ Дх҃а*), noting that by some traditions the day is dedicated to the Life-Creating Trinity (*Живонача́льныѧ Трⷪ҇цы*); the customary memorial prayers for the departed are omitted throughout Bright Week and the post-festal period of Pentecost.
[^2569]: Service opening for Matins on Bright Monday of the Holy Spirit (*День Свѧта́гw Дх҃а*).
[^2570]: Rubric for the singing of *God is the Lord* (*Бо́гъ Госпо́дь* / Ps. 117:27, 26 LXX) and the threefold repetition of the Pentecost Dismissal Troparion (*Благослове́нъ еси́, Христе́ Бо́же на́шъ*).
[^2571]: Sessional Hymn following the First Psalter Stichology, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (*Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ*), celebrating Pentecost as the post-festal crown and final feast of the liturgical year's paschal cycle.
[^2572]: Sessional Hymn following the Second Kathisma, Tone 4, same Podoben, depicting the outpouring of the Holy Spirit as fiery rivers bedewing the Apostles with grace through fire and water.
[^2573]: Printed folio 571 (фѯ҃а). Rubric appointing both festal canons of Pentecost: the first canon by St. Cosmas of Maiuma in Tone 1 with its heirmos sung to 8, and the second (iambic) canon by St. John of Damascus in Tone 4 with its heirmos sung to 6; Katavasia: *Бж҃е́ственнымъ покрове́нъ* (*Covered by divine cloud*), sung jointly by both choirs.
[^2574]: Special Kontakion to the Most Holy Trinity appointed after Ode 3, Tone 3 (*Трⷪ҇це є҆диносꙋ́щнаѧ и҆ нераздѣ́льнаѧ...*), offering the angelic Trisagion unto the three-hypostatic Unity.
[^2575]: Sessional Hymn following Ode 3, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (*Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва*), repeated from Compline (fol. 568 / leaf p574).
[^2576]: Rubric for the festal Kontakion (*Егда́ снизше́дъ язы́ки слия́...*) and Ikos of Pentecost appointed after Ode 6 of the Canon.
[^2577]: Headpiece for the Synaxarion of Monday after Pentecost, dedicated explicitly to the Third Person of the Holy Trinity, the All-Holy and Life-Creating Spirit (*Сѷнаѯа́рь, въ Понедѣ́лникъ по Пѧтдесѧ́тницѣ, сїе́сть, ст҃а́гѡ Дх҃а*), composed by Nicephorus Callistus Xanthopoulos (c. 1256–1335).
[^2578]: Opening iambic stichoi of the Synaxarion, translating the Greek original: *Πᾶσα πνοὴ δόξαζε Πνεῦμα Κυρίου, / ᾯ τῶν πονηρῶν πνευμάτων φроῦδα θράση* (*Let every breath glorify the Spirit of the Lord, by Whom the audacity of the evil spirits fell*).
[^2579]: Theological opening of the Synaxarion treatise, setting forth the feast of the Holy Spirit as equal in honor, consubstantial, and co-glorious with the Father and the Son, commemorated on Monday to give distinct honor to the Paraclete.
[^2580]: Scriptural references to the historical descent of the Holy Spirit recorded in Acts 2:1-4, noting the fulfillment of Christ's promises in John 14:16, 15:26, 16:7-13, and Luke 24:49, falling upon both the Twelve and the wider body of the Seventy Disciples.
[^2581]: Commentary on the miracle of polyglot speech (glossolalia) and the diverse nationalities gathered in Jerusalem (Acts 2:5-13), refuting the mockery that the Apostles were filled with new wine.
[^2582]: Patristic meditation explaining the ten-day interval between the Ascension of Christ and the descent of the Holy Spirit: during this sacred decade, each of the nine angelic hierarchies sequentially venerated the deified human nature of Christ seated upon the Father's right hand.
[^2583]: Typological synthesis connecting the fifty days of Pascha to the fifty days after the Red Sea crossing, demonstrating that the descent of the Holy Spirit in the upper room is the spiritual fulfillment of the Sinaitic giving of the Law (Exod. 19-20).
[^2584]: Exposition of the mystical symbolism of the fiery tongues: as the pride of Babel resulted in the scattering of mankind through diverse languages (Gen. 11:1-9), so the descent of the fiery tongues at Pentecost united all disparate tongues into the unity of faith.
[^2585]: Printed folio 572 (фѯ҃в). Continuation of the Synaxarion: the dispersion of tongues at Babel is healed and reconciled through the Pentecostal proclamation of the Gospel across all nations.
[^2586]: Practical and providential rationale for the timing of Pentecost: the massive gathering of pilgrims in Jerusalem ensured that the miracle of the Spirit and the fulfillment of the Paschal mystery would be broadcast throughout the Roman and Parthian worlds.
[^2587]: Mystical interpretation of the tongues resting upon the heads of the Apostles: the crown of the head represents the seat of the noetic intellect (*nous*) and the bodily locus of ordination (*cheirotonia*), constituting the Apostles as universal teachers.
[^2588]: Commemoration of St. Peter's first apostolic sermon (Acts 2:14-41), citing the prophecy of Joel 2:28-32 (*I will pour out My Spirit upon all flesh*) and resulting in the baptism of three thousand souls.
[^2589]: Trinitarian linguistic analysis distinguishing between personal identity (*allos kai allos*, "another Person") and essential nature (*allo kai allo*, "another thing/nature"), affirming that the Spirit is another Hypostasis of the same divine Essence as the Father and the Son.
[^2590]: Crucial recensional variant: the 1907 Lviv Stauropegion print explicitly reads *кромѣ̀ нерожде́нїа и҆ рожде́нїа: ѿ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ и҆ Сн҃а и҆сходѧ́й* ("except unbegottenness and begottenness: proceeding from the Father and the Son"). While standard Greek editions read *ἐκ μόνου τοῦ Πατρὸς ἐκπορευόμενον* ("proceeding from the Father alone"), this Ruthenian Greek Catholic edition reflects the theological alignment codified at the Union of Brest (1596) and the Synod of Zamosc (1720).
[^2591]: Patristic doctrine of the threefold giving of the Holy Spirit by Christ: obscurely before His Passion, by the breathing of John 20:22 after the Resurrection, and essentially and hypostatically at Pentecost in Acts 2:1-4.
[^2592]: Concluding devotional prayer of the Synaxarion: *Through the prayers of the Holy Apostles, O Christ our God, have mercy on us. Amen.*
[^2593]: Rubric directing that *More honorable than the Cherubim* (*Честнѣ́йшꙋю*) is omitted in favor of the festal megalynaria of Ode 9, followed by the Exapostilaria of the feast.
[^2594]: Rubric for the Praises (*Хвали́тны* / Pss. 148–150 LXX), with 6 appointed verses and idiomelic stichera in Tone 2.
[^2595]: First sticheron at the Praises, Tone 2 (*Во Прⷪ҇ро́цѣхъ возвѣсти́лъ є҆сѝ на́мъ пꙋ́ть сп҃се́нїа...*), glorifying Christ for revealing the way of salvation through the Prophets and illuminating the Apostles with the grace of the Spirit.
[^2596]: Second sticheron at the Praises, Tone 2 (*Во дво́рѣхъ твои́хъ воспою́ тѧ Сп҃са мі́ра...*), vowing continual prayer with bended knees at evening, morning, and noonday (cf. Ps. 54:18 LXX).
[^2597]: Third sticheron at the Praises, Tone 2 (*Во дво́рѣхъ твои́хъ Гдⷭ҇и, вѣ́рнїи колѣ̑на...*), kneeling with soul and body to praise the unoriginate Father, co-unoriginate Son, and co-eternal Holy Spirit.
[^2598]: Doxasticon at the Praises, Tone 8 (*Ꙗ҆зы́цы и҆ногда̀ размѣси́шасѧ...*), developing the theological antithesis between the division of tongues at the Tower of Babel and their reunification at Pentecost.
[^2599]: Concluding rubrics for Matins: Great Doxology, Festal Troparion, litanies, the proper dismissal of Pentecost (*May He Who in the form of fiery tongues...*), and the First Hour.
[^2600]: Printed folio 573 (фѯ҃г). Rubric for the Divine Liturgy of Bright Monday of the Holy Spirit: the Typika (*И҆зѡбрази́телнаѧ* / Pss. 102, 145 LXX, and the Beatitudes) are appointed, with the Beatitudes troparia taken from Odes 3 and 6 of the Pentecost Canon.
[^2601]: Entrance Hymn (*Входное*) of the Divine Liturgy: Psalm 20:14 LXX (*Be Thou exalted, O Lord, in Thy strength; we will sing and praise Thy mighty acts*).
[^2602]: Troparion of Pentecost sung at the Little Entrance, Tone 8 (*Благослове́нъ еси́, Христе́ Бо́же на́шъ...*).
[^2603]: Festal Kontakion of Pentecost, Tone 8 (*Егда́ снизше́дъ язы́ки слия́...*), sung following *Glory, both now*.
[^2604]: Liturgical appointments for the Epistle: Prokeimenon in Tone 6, citing Psalm 27:9, 1 LXX (*Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance*).
[^2605]: Epistle pericope appointed for Monday of the Holy Spirit: Ephesians 5:8b-19 (section 229), exhorting believers to walk as children of light and be filled with the Spirit.
[^2606]: Alleluia psalmody preceding the Holy Gospel: Tone 2, with stichoi from Psalm 50:3, 13 LXX (*Have mercy on me, O God... Cast me not away from Thy face*).
[^2607]: Gospel pericope appointed for Monday of the Holy Spirit: Matthew 18:10-20 (section 75), warning against despising one of Christ's little ones, and affirming Christ's presence where two or three are gathered in His Name.
[^2608]: Communion Hymn (*Причастный*) of the Liturgy: Psalm 142:10 LXX (*Thy good Spirit shall lead me in the right land*), concluding with the threefold Alleluia.
[^2609]: Universal Typikon rubric governing the post-Pentecostal week: all fasting is suspended (*разрѣша́ема є҆́сть на сы́ръ, ры́бы, вїно̀, и҆ є҆ле́й*), permitting dairy, eggs, fish, wine, and oil on Wednesday and Friday throughout Trinity Week.
[^2610]: Rubrical dispensation omitting the Mid-Hours (*междоча́стїа*) throughout the week, as well as the customary Compline Canon to the Theotokos, since the feast takes precedence.
[^2611]: Printed folio 574 (фѯ҃д). Service opening for Vespers on Monday of Holy Pentecost in the evening (*Въ Понедѣ́лникъ ст҃ы́ѧ Пѧтдесѧ́тницы ве́чера*).
[^2612]: Rubric for the Lucernarium psalms (*Го́споди воззва́хъ* / Ps. 140 LXX) with 6 appointed verses, singing prosomoia stichera in Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (*Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ*).
[^2613]: First prosomion sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1 (*Ны́нѣ ѡ҆бновлѧ́ютсѧ ꙗ҆зы́цы...*), celebrating the renewal of human tongues to proclaim God's wonders in foreign speech.
[^2614]: Second prosomion sticheron, Tone 1 (*Ю҆́же низпосла́ти ѡ҆бѣща́лъ є҆сѝ...*), confessing the fulfillment of Christ's promise to send the Spirit from on high, confirming that He is the Truth.
[^2615]: Third prosomion sticheron, Tone 1 (*Предѡбѣща́нїе и҆спо́лнивый...*), hymning the sending of the Paraclete in fiery tongues to consume sins and grant divine communion.
[^2616]: Rubric directing that the remaining 3 stichera at *Lord, I have cried* are taken from the Menaion of the day (*И҆ Мине́и, г҃*).
[^2617]: Doxasticon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1 (*Пѧтдесѧ́тницꙋ пра́зднꙋемъ...*), summing up the feast of Pentecost as the coming of the Spirit, the pledge of promise, and the fulfillment of hope.
[^2618]: Rubric for the Aposticha stichera (*На стїхо́внѣ стїхи̑ры*), Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (*До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ*).
[^2619]: First sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 2 (*Дѣ́ломъ Оу҆чени́кѡмъ...*), praising Christ for sending the Holy Spirit in actual deed according to His promise.
[^2620]: First psalm verse at the Aposticha: Psalm 50:12 LXX (*Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me*).
[^2621]: Second sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 2 (*Внеза́пꙋ съ нб҃сѐ А҆́пⷭ҇лы всѧ̑...*), describing how the power of the Comforter suddenly revealed the Apostles as all-wise and God-inspired.
[^2622]: Second psalm verse at the Aposticha: Psalm 50:13 LXX (*Cast me not away from Thy face, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me*).
[^2623]: Third sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 2 (*Ро́дъ и҆норо́дныхъ, слы́шаще недоꙋмѣва́хꙋсѧ...*), recounting the astonishment of foreigners hearing the unlettered Galilean fishermen praise the Trinity in native tongues.
[^2624]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha, Tone 1 (*Ꙗ҆зы́ками и҆норо́дныхъ, ѡ҆бнови́лъ є҆сѝ Хрⷭ҇тѐ...*), concluding the evening service with praise of the immortal Word Who renewed the Apostles through diverse languages.
[^2625]: Codicological catchword directing the order of divine services unto Tuesday of Holy Pentecost (*Во вто́рникъ* / Printed folio 574v, physical leaf p581).
[^2626]: Physical leaf p581 corresponds to printed folio 575 (`ф҃ѻє`). The liturgical cycle continues the post-festive celebration of Holy Pentecost on Tuesday at Matins with dedicated Sessional hymns and Aposticha stichera in honor of the Holy Spirit.
[^2627]: The melodic model *Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й)* refers to the canonical Tone 1 resurrectional sessional hymn incipit *Τὸν τάφον σου Σωτήρ* / *Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се*.
[^2628]: The melodic model *Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ)* corresponds to the canonical Tone 4 incipit *Ταχὺ προκατάλαβε* / *Ско́рѡ предварѝ*.
[^2629]: Following Psalm 50, the order prescribes the Canon of the feast in Tone 4 with its heirmoi to 8 troparia, combined with the Canon of the daily saint from the Menaion to 4 troparia.
[^2630]: The melodic model *Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Οἶκος τοῦ Εὐφραθᾶ* / *До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ*.
[^2631]: The imagery of the fiery tongues dividing, illuminating, but not consuming reflects the Patristic interpretation of Pentecost contrasting the consuming fire of Sinai with the life-giving, illuminating fire of the Holy Spirit.
[^2632]: The classic Pentecost sticheron *Всѧ̑ подае́тъ Дх҃а ст҃ы́й* ("The Holy Spirit provides all things"), attributed to the Emperor Leo the Wise or St. Cosmas, articulates the diverse theological operations and charisms of the Third Person of the Holy Trinity.
[^2633]: Physical leaf p582 corresponds to printed folio 576 (`ф҃ѻѕ`). The Tuesday Matins Triodion (*Трипѣ́снецъ*) consists of Odes 2, 8, and 9 in Tone 2, celebrating the descent and hypostatic reality of the Holy Spirit.
[^2634]: The Heirmos of Ode 2 cites the Second Canticle of Moses from Deuteronomy 32:4 (LXX): "God: His works are true, and all His ways are judgments."
[^2635]: The Heirmos of Ode 8 draws upon the Song of the Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:52-88 LXX).
[^2636]: The phrase "another Comforter" (*И҆но́го Оу҆тѣ́шителѧ*) directly cites John 14:16 (*ἄλλον Παράκλητον*), highlighting the distinction of hypostases within the undivided Godhead.
[^2637]: The Heirmos of Ode 9 reflects upon the Incarnation of God the Word from the Holy Virgin as the restoration of fallen Adam.
[^2638]: This troparion invokes the sevenfold gifts of the Spirit enumerated in Isaiah 11:2 (LXX): the spirit of wisdom, understanding, counsel, might, knowledge, and piety.
[^2639]: Physical leaf p583 corresponds to printed folio 577 (`ф҃ѻз`). Tuesday evening begins the liturgical observance of Wednesday with the Vespers office.
[^2640]: The melodic model *Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων* / *Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ*.
[^2641]: The rare metaphor *Невокова̑нныѧ мечѝ* ("unforged swords" / Greek *ἀχαλκεύτους μαχαίρας*) depicts the Apostles as weapons fashioned not by metalcraft or human forge, but wrought spiritually by the Holy Spirit to conquer the world.
[^2642]: The renowned Pentecost troparion *Ви́дѣхомъ свѣ́тъ и҆́стинный* ("We have seen the true Light"), sung also after Holy Communion in the Divine Liturgy, is here appointed as the festive Doxasticon at Vespers.
[^2643]: The melodic model *Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν* / *Да́лъ е҆сѝ зна́меніе*.
[^2644]: This sticheron alludes to the prophecy of Joel 2:28 (LXX) concerning the outpouring of the Spirit upon all flesh in the last days, fulfilled at Pentecost (Acts 2:16-18).
[^2645]: Physical leaf p584 corresponds to printed folio 578 (`ф҃ѻи`). The concluding Aposticha Doxasticon contrasts the spiritual reality of the descent with the mocking accusation of drunkenness recorded in Acts 2:13.
[^2646]: Matins of Wednesday of Holy Pentecost begins with the festive troparion *Благослове́нъ е҆сѝ Хрїстѐ Бо́же на́шъ* sung thrice.
[^2647]: The melodic model *Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Τὸν συνάναρχον Λόγον* / *Собезнача́льное Сло́во*.
[^2648]: The melodic model *Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον* / *Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва*.
[^2649]: The sessional hymn celebrates the paradox of simple, unlettered fishermen (*лове́цъ*) transformed by divine grace into princes of the Church and universal theologians.
[^2650]: The Aposticha stichera in Tone 2 reuse the melodic model *До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ* to articulate the ongoing Pentecostal sanctification.
[^2651]: Physical leaf p585 corresponds to printed folio 579 (`ф҃ѳ` / `ф҃оѳ`). The solemn Doxasticon *Цр҃ю̀ нбⷭ҇ный* ("O Heavenly King"), the universal invocation of the Holy Spirit, concludes the Aposticha before the First Hour.
[^2652]: The Wednesday Triodion begins with Ode 3, whose Heirmos draws upon the Canticle of Hannah (1 Kingdoms / 1 Samuel 2:2 LXX) and Acts 20:28.
[^2653]: The Greek text underlying *показа̀ дне́сь А҆́пⷭ҇толы нѣ́ма* contrasts the prior silence of the disciples with their miraculous fluency in all languages through the Spirit.
[^2654]: The Heirmos of Ode 8 echoes the Song of the Three Holy Children, praising God Who preserves His servants amid fire.
[^2655]: Physical leaf p586 corresponds to printed folio 580 (`ф҃п`). The phrase "beauty of Jacob" (*І҆а́кѡвлю добро́тꙋ*) alludes to Psalm 46:5 (LXX) and Amos 8:7, applied typologically to the Mother of God.
[^2656]: The Heirmos of Ode 9 cites the legal statute of the firstborn opening the womb (Exodus 13:2, Luke 2:23 LXX), fulfilled archetypally by the Incarnate Word.
[^2657]: The epithet *многочⷭ҇тна́го* ("most honorable" / "greatly valued") describes the precious gift of the Spirit given freely to the Church.
[^2658]: Wednesday evening commences the liturgical service of Thursday with Great Vespers.
[^2659]: The melodic model *Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Ὁ ἐξ ὑψίστου κληθείς* / *Зва́нный свы́ше*.
[^2660]: The text references the Holy Evangelist Luke's narrative in the Acts of the Apostles (Acts 2:1-4) concerning the violent wind and the sudden enlightenment of the fishermen.
[^2661]: Physical leaf p587 corresponds to printed folio 581 (`ф҃па`). The sticheron supplicates the Savior not to take away the Holy Spirit from the faithful, echoing Psalm 50:13 (LXX).
[^2662]: The Doxasticon pairs Christ as Advocate (*Παράκλητος* / 1 John 2:1) with the Holy Spirit as the other Advocate sent to the Church on earth.
[^2663]: The melodic model *Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες* / *Прехва́льніи мꙋ́ченицы*.
[^2664]: The sticheron affirms the eternal procession of the Holy Spirit from the Father (*ѿ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ и҆сходѧ́щаго*, John 15:26) and His co-equal glory in the Son.
[^2665]: The Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 8 recounts the astonishment of the Jewish onlookers at the multilingual eloquence of the unlearned Galilean disciples.
[^2666]: Physical leaf p588 corresponds to printed folio 582 (`ф҃пв`). Matins of Thursday after Pentecost opens with the festive troparion and sessional hymns.
[^2667]: Sessional hymn after the 1st Kathisma in Tone 1, Podoben *Гро́бъ тво́й*, explaining the fulfillment of prophetic types by the outpoured Spirit.
[^2668]: Sessional hymn after the 2nd Kathisma in Tone 8, Podoben *Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ* (*Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς*), describing the bold witness of the disciples upon receiving the Spirit.
[^2669]: The rubric prescribes the Canon of the feast in Tone 7 with the heirmos to 8, paired with the daily saint from the Menaion to 4.
[^2670]: The Aposticha stichera are sung to *Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́внѡ)* (*Τριήμερος ἀνέστης Χριστέ*), here uniquely set to celebrate the fire-wrought transformation of the disciples' earthen tongues.
[^2671]: Physical leaf p589 corresponds to printed folio 583 (`ф҃пг`). The Aposticha Doxasticon *Во дво́рѣхъ твои́хъ Гдⷭ҇и* draws upon Psalm 134:2 (LXX), bowing the knees of soul and body in adoration of the Holy Trinity.
[^2672]: The Thursday Triodion features Odes 4, 8, and 9 in Tone 4. The Heirmos of Ode 4 draws upon Habakkuk 3:2-3 (LXX) and Isaiah 19:1.
[^2673]: The troparion emphasizes the divine transcendence of the Holy Spirit, Who surpasses all human intellect and swiftness of thought.
[^2674]: The Heirmos of Ode 8 echoes the Song of the Youths in the furnace, praising the Almighty Redeemer.
[^2675]: Physical leaf p590 corresponds to printed folio 584 (`ф҃пд`). The Heirmos of Ode 9 contrasts Eve's introduction of the curse through disobedience with Mary's blossoming of the universal blessing through the Incarnation.
[^2676]: The troparion directly references the Prophet Joel's vision of the Spirit poured out upon all flesh (Joel 2:28 LXX, Acts 2:17).
[^2677]: Thursday evening marks the celebration of Friday with the Vespers office.
[^2678]: The stichera at "Lord, I have cried" are repeated in Tone 4 to the Podoben *Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе*, emphasizing the divine and consubstantial nature of the Spirit.
[^2679]: The concluding Doxasticon in Tone 2 summarizes the theological foundation of the feast: the consubstantial Trinity proclaimed in unison by Prophets, Apostles, and Martyrs.
[^2680]: Physical leaf p591 corresponds to printed folio 585 (`ф҃пє`). The post-festive celebration of Holy Pentecost continues on Thursday evening at Vespers (the liturgical start of Friday) with Aposticha stichera praising the Holy Spirit, followed by the headpiece for Friday Matins.
[^2681]: The melodic model *Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ)* corresponds to the canonical Byzantine automelon incipit *Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων* / *Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ*.
[^2682]: The hymnographic contrast between Moses beholding God in fire upon Mount Sinai (Exodus 19:18, Deuteronomy 4:12) and the Apostles receiving the fiery descent of the Holy Spirit (Acts 2:3) emphasizes the identity of the divine nature in both dispensations.
[^2683]: The Church Slavonic phrase *дебе́лость вещества̀ неизрече́ннѡ* ("the coarseness of matter ineffably") describes human physical corporeality sanctified, lightened, and renewed by divine spiritual fire.
[^2684]: This Typikon rubric manages the hymnographic density of the post-festive period by anticipating the commemorations of certain daily saints at Compline when primary weekend offices (Saturday of Apodosis and Sunday of All Saints) supersede them.
[^2685]: The melodic model *Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й)* corresponds to the canonical resurrectional sessional incipit *Τὸν τάφον σου Σωτήρ* / *Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се*.
[^2686]: Physical leaf p592 corresponds to printed folio 586 (`ф҃пѕ`). Friday Matins of Holy Pentecost continues with the sessional hymn after the 2nd Kathisma, followed by the Aposticha in Tone 6 and the First Hour.
[^2687]: The melodic model *Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον* / *Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва*.
[^2688]: The sessional hymn alludes to Acts 2:13, where cynical onlookers mocked the multilingual ecstasy of the Apostles as intoxication with new wine (*пїѧ́нство* / *γλεύκους μεμεστωμένοι*), whereas the faithful recognized the fulfillment of divine prophecy.
[^2689]: The melodic model *Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Τριήμερος ἀνέστης Χριστέ* / *Тридне́внѡ воскре́слъ е҆сѝ*.
[^2690]: The reference to the "third hour" (*въ тре́тїй бо ча́съ*) anchors the liturgical remembrance to the scriptural hour of the Spirit's descent recorded in Acts 2:15.
[^2691]: The Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 8 (*Є҆гда̀ дх҃а твоегѡ̀ посла́лъ є҆сѝ гдⷭ҇и*) celebrates the supernatural wisdom granted to unlettered Galilean fishermen (*невѣ́жди бо сꙋ́ще оу҆мꙋдри́шасѧ*).
[^2692]: Physical leaf p593 corresponds to printed folio 587 (`ф҃пз`). Friday Matins Triodion (*Трипѣ́снецъ*) consists of Odes 5, 8, and 9 in Tone 5, dedicated to the adoration and hypostatic reality of the Holy Spirit.
[^2693]: The Heirmos of Ode 5 draws directly upon the creation psalmody of Psalm 103:2 (LXX): "Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment."
[^2694]: The theological formula *ѿ безна́чална Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ неслїѧ́ннѡ* ("unconfusedly from the unoriginate Father") articulates the eternal, hypostatic procession of the Third Person of the Holy Trinity from the Father alone, resting co-eternally upon the Son.
[^2695]: The Marian titles *го́ро присѣ́ннаѧ, тꙋ́чнаѧ го́ро и҆ оу҆сыре́ннаѧ* ("mountain overshadowed, mountain fat and curdled") cite the typological prophecies of Psalm 67:16-17 and Habakkuk 3:3 (LXX).
[^2696]: The Heirmos of Ode 8 draws upon the Canticle of the Three Holy Youths in the furnace of Babylon (Daniel 3:52-88 LXX).
[^2697]: The Heirmos of Ode 9 cites the Messianic Emmanuel prophecy of Isaiah 7:14 and the prophetic title "Dayspring" (*Восто́къ* / *Ἀνατολή*) from Zechariah 6:12 and Luke 1:78 (LXX).
[^2698]: Physical leaf p594 corresponds to printed folio 588 (`ф҃пи`). Ode 9 of the Friday Triodion concludes, followed by comprehensive rubrics governing the Apodosis of Holy Pentecost on Friday evening and Saturday morning.
[^2699]: The troparion articulates high Pneumatological dogma: the Spirit is *є҆диноесте́ственъ, и҆ є҆диносꙋ́щенъ, и҆ є҆диноче́стенъ, и҆ сопресто́ленъ* ("one in nature, consubstantial, of equal honor, and co-enthroned") with the Father and the Only-begotten Son.
[^2700]: The rubrics explicitly order Friday Vespers and Saturday Matins according to the liturgical pattern of the Apodosis of the Ascension, prescribing festive stichera and canons while omitting Polyeleos, Megalynarion, and Gospel.
[^2701]: The Katavasia heirmos *Бж҃е́ственнымъ покрове́нъ* ("Covered by the divine darkness") is the celebrated opening of the iambic Pentecost canon composed by St. John of Damascus.
[^2702]: Physical leaf p595 corresponds to printed folio 589 (`ф҃пѳ`). Saturday of Holy Pentecost Matins features the Four-Ode Canon (*Четверопѣ́снецъ*) in Tone 6, comprising Odes 6, 7, 8, and 9.
[^2703]: The Heirmos of Ode 6 draws upon the prayer of the Prophet Jonah from the depths of the sea (Jonah 2:3-10 LXX), symbolizing the soul's deliverance from worldly tempests.
[^2704]: This troparion explicitly affirms that the Holy Spirit operated as the divine illuminator and sanctifier of the angelic celestial hosts before the creation of the material world.
[^2705]: The Heirmos of Ode 7 recalls the miraculous Angel of the Lord who cooled the fiery Babylonian furnace with dew for Ananias, Azarias, and Misael (Daniel 3:49-50 LXX).
[^2706]: The theological term *сво́йство бж҃тва̀* refers to the personal hypostatic property of the Holy Spirit, manifested in the fiery tongues at Pentecost.
[^2707]: The Heirmos of Ode 8 references both the cooling of the Babylonian furnace and the miraculous sacrifice of the Prophet Elijah upon Mount Carmel, which was consumed by fire from heaven despite being drenched with water (3 Kingdoms / 1 Kings 18:33-38 LXX).
[^2708]: Physical leaf p596 corresponds to printed folio 590 (`ф҃ч`). The Four-Ode Canon of Saturday Matins concludes with Odes 8 and 9, followed by the rubrics and scriptural pericopes for the Divine Liturgy of the Apodosis of Pentecost.
[^2709]: The troparion invokes Psalm 50:12 (LXX): "Renew a right spirit within me."
[^2710]: The Heirmos of Ode 9 meditates on the paradox of the invisible and unapproachable Godhead becoming visible to humanity through the Incarnation of the Word from the Virgin.
[^2711]: The Divine Liturgy of the Apodosis of Pentecost prescribes Romans 1:7-12 (Pericope 74) as the Epistle and Matthew 5:42-48 (Pericope 5, the command to love enemies and be perfect as the Heavenly Father is perfect) as the Gospel.
[^2712]: The Pentecost Communion Verse (*Прича́стенъ*) is taken from Psalm 142:10 (LXX): "Thy good Spirit shall guide me in the right land. Alleluia."
[^2713]: Physical leaf p597 corresponds to printed folio 591 (`ф҃ча`). The liturgical book opens the cycle of the Sunday of All Saints with Small Vespers celebrated on Saturday afternoon.
[^2714]: At Small Vespers, the resurrectional stichera of Tone 8 and the Doxasticon of the Martyrs in Tone 6 are cited by their incipits, with rubrical reference to their full appearance at Great Vespers.
[^2715]: The Tone 8 Dogmatikon *Ка́кѡ тѧ̀ оу҆блажи́мъ Бцⷣе* ("How shall we call thee blessed, O Theotokos?") constitutes an elaborate theological exposition of the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III, 680-681), defining Christ's two natural wills (*два̀ хотѣ́нїѧ*) and two natural operations (*два̀ дѣ́йства*) corresponding to His two undivided natures.
[^2716]: Physical leaf p598 corresponds to printed folio 592 (`ф҃чв`). Small Vespers concludes with the Aposticha Theotokia in Tone 8 and the dismissal.
[^2717]: The melodic model *Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ)* corresponds to the canonical automelon incipit *Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος* / *Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ*.
[^2718]: The Aposticha stichera comprise an extensive litany of classical biblical and liturgical epithets for the Mother of God, invoking her as Wellspring, Palace, Divine Tabernacle, Candlestick, and Living Paradise.
[^2719]: The epithet "slumberless Eye" (*недре́млющее Ѻ҆́ко*) applied to the Christ Child in the arms of the Virgin alludes to Psalm 120:4 (LXX): "Behold, He that keeps Israel shall neither slumber nor sleep."
[^2720]: Physical leaf p599 corresponds to printed folio 593 (`ф҃чг`). Great Vespers of the Sunday of All Saints begins with the full first Kathisma of the Psalter ("Blessed is the man") and 10 stichera at "Lord, I have cried."
[^2721]: The resurrectional stichera of Tone 8 address Holy Zion as the "Mother of the Churches," honoring the Jerusalem Church as the birthplace of Christian worship and the site of the Upper Room.
[^2722]: The Eastern stichera (*Восто́чны* / *Anatolika*) represent a distinguished stratum of Octoechos resurrectional hymnography, traditionally attributed to Anatolius, Patriarch of Constantinople (5th century).
[^2723]: The melodic model *Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all earthly hope" (Всѐ ѿло́жше)* corresponds to the canonical Byzantine automelon incipit *Ὅλην ἀποθέμενοι* / *Всѐ ѿло́жше*.
[^2724]: The stichera of All Saints graphically enumerate the historical torments endured by the early Christian martyrs: exposure to wild beasts, beheading by the sword, laceration by iron claws, amputation of limbs, and burning with material fire.
[^2725]: Physical leaf p600 corresponds to printed folio 594 (`ф҃чд`). Great Vespers of All Saints continues with the 4th sticheron of the saints, the Doxasticon, the classical Tone 8 Dogmatikon, and the Old Testament Paremias.
[^2726]: The Doxasticon in Tone 6 (*Мꙋ́ченикъ Бж҃твенный ли́къ*) celebrates the martyrs as the living fulfillment of the Gospel, whose bloodshed permanently quenched the sacrificial fires of pagan polytheism.
[^2727]: The renowned Tone 8 Dogmatikon *Цр҃ь нбⷭ҇ный* ("The King of heaven"), authored by St. John of Damascus, articulates the Chalcedonian dogma of the Incarnation: Christ is one Son, twofold in nature (*сꙋгꙋ́бъ є҆стество́мъ*), but single in hypostasis (*но не ѵ҆поста́сїю*).
[^2728]: The first Old Testament reading is Isaiah 43:9-14 (LXX), the solemn courtroom narrative in which the Lord summons the nations and establishes Israel and His Servant as His witnesses to His eternal, unoriginate Godhead.
[^2729]: The second Old Testament reading is Wisdom of Solomon 3:1-9, the preeminent patristic and liturgical reading for martyrs and saints, declaring that "the souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them."
[^2730]: Physical leaf p601 corresponds to printed folio 595 (`ф҃чє`). At Great Vespers of the Sunday of All Saints, the third Old Testament Paremia is Wisdom 5:15 - 6:3, followed by the Litiya stichera in Tone 1.
[^2731]: The Litiya idiomel stichera in Tone 1 catalog the entire panoply of saints from all ages: Patriarchs, Prophets, Apostles, Martyrs, Hierarchs, Teachers, Ascetics, and holy Women.
[^2732]: The prayer for the "Christ-loving Emperor" (*Хрⷭ҇толюби́вомꙋ Їмпера́торꙋ*) reflects the historical imperial Ruthenian recension of the 1907 Lviv Stauropegion edition under the Austro-Hungarian crown (Emperor Franz Joseph I).
[^2733]: The Litiya sticheron invokes the Apostles as "net-casters of the nations and fishers of men" (*ꙗ҆зы́кѡвъ мре́жницы, и҆ ловцы̀ человѣ́кѡвъ*), echoing Matthew 4:19 and Mark 1:17.
[^2734]: Physical leaf p602 corresponds to printed folio 596 (`ф҃чѕ`). The Litiya concludes with the Tone 5 Doxasticon graphically depicting the tortures of the martyrs, followed by the Aposticha stichera of Tone 8.
[^2735]: The Aposticha Eastern resurrectional stichera (*восто́чны*) in Tone 8 follow an alphabetical Greek acrostic in the original Byzantine hymnography.
[^2736]: The Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 6 summons the faithful to honor all the saints together, praying again for victory unto the Emperor over barbarians.
[^2737]: The Tone 6 Aposticha Theotokion *Творе́цъ и҆ И҆зба́витель мо́й* ("My Creator and Redeemer") is the classical Dogmatikon/Theotokion of Tone 6 by St. John of Damascus, meditating on Christ assuming human nature to undo Adam's curse.
[^2738]: Physical leaf p603 corresponds to printed folio 597 (`ф҃чз`). Vespers concludes with the blessing of the loaves and the reading from the General Epistle of James, followed by the Midnight Office rubric and the headpiece for Sunday Matins.
[^2739]: The Typikon prescribes the reading of the General Epistle of James during the Paschaltide/Pentecost post-Vespers vigil period, following ancient monastic practice.
[^2740]: The resurrectional Troparion of Tone 8 *Съ высоты̀ снизше́лъ є҆сѝ Бл҃гоꙋтро́бне* ("Thou didst come down from on high, O Compassionate One") highlights Christ's three-day burial delivering mankind from passions.
[^2741]: The celebrated Troparion of All Saints in Tone 4 *И҆́же во все́мъ мі́рѣ Мч҃нкъ твои́хъ* ("Thy Church, adorned with the blood of Thy Martyrs throughout the whole world as with purple and fine linen") represents the primary dismissal hymn of the feast.
[^2742]: The Tone 4 Theotokion *Є҆́же ѿ вѣ́ка оу҆тае́ное* ("The mystery hidden from all ages") articulates the Incarnation as an unconfused union (*въ несли́тномъ соедине́нїи*) preserving both divine and human natures.
[^2743]: Physical leaf p604 corresponds to printed folio 598 (`ф҃чи`). Matins continues after the Kathisma readings with resurrectional Sessional Hymns in Tone 8 and the standing Theotokion *Ѡ҆ тебѣ̀ ра́дꙋетсѧ*.
[^2744]: The melodic model *Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ)* corresponds to the canonical Greek automelon incipit *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς* / *Повелѣ́нное та́йнѡ*.
[^2745]: The melodic model *Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word of God" (Премꙋ́дрости Сло́ва)* corresponds to the canonical incipit *Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον* / *Премꙋ́дрости сло́ва*.
[^2746]: The rubric explicitly directs that *Ѡ҆ тебѣ̀ ра́дꙋетсѧ* ("All creation rejoices in thee"), composed by St. John of Damascus, be sung standing (*не сѣда́юще пое́мъ, но стоя́ще*), with fear and reverence, honoring its exalted status.
[^2747]: The Tone 8 Hypakoe *Мѵроно́сицы Жизнода́вца* recounts the encounter of the Myrrh-bearing women with the angel at the empty tomb.
[^2748]: Physical leaf p605 corresponds to printed folio 599 (`ф҃чѳ`). The complete four Antiphons of the Hymns of Ascents (Anabathmoi) in Tone 8 are chanted. Tone 8 uniquely contains four Antiphons instead of the customary three found in Tones 1 through 7.
[^2749]: The first Antiphon of Tone 8 draws upon Psalm 128:1-2 (LXX), likening the haters of Zion to withered grass.
[^2750]: Antiphon 2 meditates on holy fear guarding against pride, and the final judgment by fire and torment.
[^2751]: Antiphon 3 invokes the tripartite theology of the Trinity (*Бг҃осло́вїе є҆ди́ница трист҃а́ѧ*): the unoriginate Father, the timelessly begotten Son, and the co-enthroned Holy Spirit.
[^2752]: Antiphon 4 cites Psalm 132:1 (LXX) ("Behold now, what is good or what is pleasant? But that brethren dwell together") and Christ's words regarding the lilies of the field (Matthew 6:28).
[^2753]: Physical leaf p606 corresponds to printed folio 600 (`х`). Sunday Matins proceeds with the Prokeimenon, the 1st Resurrection Gospel (Matthew 28:16-20, Pericope 116), and the canon complex comprising 4 canons.
[^2754]: The rubric notes that from this Sunday of All Saints, the eleven weekly Resurrection Matins Gospels (*Є҆ѵⷢлїа воскр҃сна*) begin to be read in regular sequential cycle (*по ря́дꙋ*).
[^2755]: The heirmos of Ode 1 in Tone 8 *Колесницегони́телѧ фараѡ́нѧ* ("Pharaoh the charioteer") is the classical Resurrection heirmos based on the Song of Moses (Exodus 15:1-19).
[^2756]: The Cross-Resurrectional Canon (*Канѡ́нъ Крⷭ҇товоскре́сенъ*) uses the heirmos *Во́дꙋ проше́дъ* ("Having traversed the water"), focusing on Christ's harrowing of Hades and the exaltation of human nature upon the Father's throne.
[^2757]: Physical leaf p607 corresponds to printed folio 601 (`ха`). Ode 1 concludes with the Canon of All Saints, carrying the Greek alphabetical acrostic: *Τῶν ἁγίων πάντων πολυώνυμα τάγματα μέλπω* ("I sing the multifold ranks of all the saints").
[^2758]: The Katavasia for the Canons is taken from the Canon of the Annunciation/Akathist: *Ѡ҆тве́рзꙋ оу҆ста̀ моѧ̑* ("I shall open my mouth"), which serves as the general Katavasia throughout the liturgical year outside festive periods.
[^2759]: The heirmos of Ode 3 in Tone 8 *Оу҆твержде́й въ нача́лѣ нб҃са̀* ("Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens") is based on the Canticle of Hannah (1 Kingdoms / 1 Samuel 2:1-10 LXX).
[^2760]: Physical leaf p608 corresponds to printed folio 602 (`хв`). Ode 3 of the canons concludes, followed by the Sessional Hymns of All Saints in Tone 8.
[^2761]: The Cross-Resurrectional heirmos *Нбⷭ҇нагѡ крꙋ́га верхотво́рче* ("Fashioner of the vault of heaven") and Theotokion heirmos *Ты̀ є҆сѝ оу҆твержде́нїе* ("Thou art the establishment") complete the intermediate canons of Ode 3.
[^2762]: In the Canon of All Saints, Ode 3 continues the acrostic with letters I, Ω, N, and Π, praising the holy pastors, teachers, and assemblies of righteous virgins.
[^2763]: The Sessional Hymn in Tone 8 *Пра́ѻтецъ, Ѻ҆тє́цъ, Патрїа́рхъ* ("Celebrating the holy memory of Thy Forefathers, Fathers, Patriarchs...") serves as a comprehensive liturgical litany of all orders of sanctity.
[^2764]: Physical leaf p609 corresponds to printed folio 603 (`хг`). Ode 4 of the canons begins with the Resurrection heirmos *Ты̀ моѧ̀ крѣ́пость Гдⷭ҇и* ("Thou art my strength, O Lord"), based on the Canticle of Habakkuk (Habakkuk 3:1-19 LXX).
[^2765]: The Marian troparion invokes the Virgin as the "untilled field" (*неѡра́ннаѧ Ни́во*) that brought forth the life-bearing ear of wheat (*кла́съ животво́рный*), echoing patristic Eucharistic typology.
[^2766]: In the Canon of All Saints, Ode 4 incorporates the letters Α, N, and T of the acrostic, praising the hieromartyrs and ascetic fathers who overcame carnal impulses.
[^2767]: Physical leaf p610 corresponds to printed folio 604 (`хд`). Ode 4 concludes with the letters Ω and N, followed by Ode 5 of the four canons.
[^2768]: The heirmos of Ode 5 in Tone 8 *Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ* ("Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face") is based on the Canticle of Isaiah (Isaiah 26:9-20).
[^2769]: The Cross-Resurrectional troparion highlights Christ being clothed in mocking purple before His Passion, thereby clothing the shameful nakedness of fallen Adam.
[^2770]: In the Canon of All Saints, Ode 5 begins with the letters Π and O of the acrostic word *ΠΟΛΥΩΝΥΜΑ*, commemorating the Old Testament patriarchs and prophets who foresaw Christ before the giving of the Mosaic Law.
[^2771]: Physical leaf p611 corresponds to printed folio 605 (`х҃є`). Ode 5 of the Canon of All Saints concludes with the letters Λ and Y of the Greek acrostic, followed by Ode 6 of the four canons.
[^2772]: The Resurrectional heirmos of Ode 6 in Tone 8 *Ѡ҆чи́сти мѧ̀ Сп҃се* ("Cleanse me, O Savior") reflects Jonah's prayer from the belly of the sea monster (Jonah 2:3-10 LXX).
[^2773]: The Cross-Resurrectional troparion highlights Christ stretching out His hands upon the Cross to heal the unrestrained reaching forth of Adam's hand in Eden (Genesis 3:6).
[^2774]: In the Canon of All Saints, Ode 6 begins with the letter Ω of the acrostic word *ΠΟΛΥΩΝΥΜΑ*, meditating on Christ as the precious cornerstone laid in Sion (Isaiah 28:16, 1 Peter 2:6).
[^2775]: Physical leaf p612 corresponds to printed folio 606 (`х҃ѕ`). Ode 6 of All Saints concludes with the letters N, Y, and M, followed by the Kontakion and Ikos of All Saints in Tone 8 and the reading of the Synaxarion.
[^2776]: The Kontakion of All Saints in Tone 8 *Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки є҆стества̀* ("As the first-fruits of nature, the universe offers unto Thee, O Lord, the God-bearing Martyrs") is attributed to St. Romanos the Melodist, serving as one of the most famous kontakia of the Byzantine liturgical year.
[^2777]: The Synaxarion for the Sunday of All Saints expounds three theological reasons why the Church established this feast immediately following Holy Pentecost: first, to demonstrate the universal fruit of the Holy Spirit's descent; second, to honor the countless anonymous saints whose names are known only to God across all lands; and third, to unite all the righteous into one common celebration since they struggled in one Christ.
[^2778]: Physical leaf p613 corresponds to printed folio 607 (`х҃з`). The Synaxarion recounts the historical dedication of the Church of All Saints in Constantinople by Emperor Leo VI the Wise (886–912) and provides an overview of the Triodion's liturgical journey from the fall of Adam to the heavenly gathering of all the saints.
[^2779]: Emperor Leo VI originally intended to dedicate the grand church to his pious first wife, Empress Theophano (d. 893/897), but when the Church hierarchy objected to immediate imperial canonization, Leo wisely dedicated the temple to All Saints of the whole world, noting: "Even if Theophano is holy, let her be numbered with all the saints."
[^2780]: The nine ranks of sanctity enumerated in the Synaxarion mirror the nine celestial angelic choirs: Forefathers, Patriarchs, Prophets, Apostles, Martyrs, Hierarchs, Hieromartyrs and Venerable Martyrs, Venerables and Righteous, and holy Women, with the Most Holy Theotokos exalted above all creation.
[^2781]: The Resurrectional heirmos of Ode 7 in Tone 8 *Бж҃їѧ снизхожде́нїа* ("God's condescension") is based on the Canticle of the Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:26-56 LXX).
[^2782]: Physical leaf p614 corresponds to printed folio 608 (`х҃и`). Ode 7 of the Canon of All Saints incorporates the letters T, A, Γ, M, A, T, A of the acrostic phrase *ΤΑΓΜΑΤΑ ΜΕΛΠΩ* ("I sing the ranks"), followed by Ode 8 of the four canons.
[^2783]: The Resurrectional heirmos of Ode 8 in Tone 8 *Седмери́цею пе́щь* ("The Chaldean tyrant furiously heated the furnace sevenfold") continues the hymnography of Daniel 3:57-88 LXX (The Song of the Three Children).
[^2784]: The Trinitarian troparion of Ode 8 articulates the orthodox dogma of the one uncreated essence (*є҆диноесте́ственно*) and three distinct Hypostases (*трїѵпоста́снагѡ є҆стества̀*): the unoriginate Father, the co-essential Word, and the co-reigning Spirit of one essence.
[^2785]: The Marian troparion implores the Virgin to quench the fiery darts of the adversary (Ephesians 6:16).
[^2786]: Physical leaf p615 corresponds to printed folio 609 (`х҃ѳ`). Ode 8 of All Saints concludes with the letters M, E, Λ, and Π, followed by the Magnificat rubric and Ode 9 of the Resurrectional and festive canons.
[^2787]: The troparion of All Saints in Ode 8 affirms that by communion with God's deifying energies and illumination by His uncreated light, the saints become "gods by grace" through drawing near unto God (*бо́зи быва́ете, къ Бг҃ꙋ приближе́нїемъ*), reflecting classical patristic theosis (divinization; cf. Psalm 81:6 LXX, John 10:34-35).
[^2788]: The Resurrectional heirmos of Ode 9 in Tone 8 *Оу҆жасе́ся ѡ҆ се́мъ нб҃о* ("Heaven was dismayed thereat") is based on the Canticle of the Theotokos (Magnificat, Luke 1:46-55) and Jeremiah 2:12 LXX.
[^2789]: The Christological troparion of Ode 9 confesses Christ in two natures unconfusedly and indivisibly united (*во двою̀ сꙋщество̀ нераздѣ́льнѡ и҆ неслїѧ́ннѡ*), directly expressing the Chalcedonian dogmatic definition.
[^2790]: Physical leaf p616 corresponds to printed folio 610 (`х҃і`). Ode 9 concludes with the final troparia of All Saints (letters E, Λ, Π, and Ω, completing the acrostic *Τῶν ἁγίων πάντων πολυώνυμα τάγματα μέλπω*), the Katavasia of the Akathist, and the first Sunday Resurrectional Exapostilarion.
[^2791]: The Katavasia of Ode 9 *Вся́къ земноро́дный* ("Let every earth-born mortal leap for joy, enlightened in spirit") is taken from the Canon of the Annunciation / Akathist Hymn (Tone 4).
[^2792]: The 1st Resurrectional Exapostilarion *Со оу҆чени́ки взы́демъ на го́рꙋ Галїле́йскꙋю* ("Let us go up with the disciples into the mountain of Galilee") corresponds to the 1st Matins Gospel reading (Matthew 28:16-20), recalling Christ's Great Commission to baptize all nations in the name of the Holy Trinity.
[^2793]: Physical leaf p617 corresponds to printed folio 611 (`х҃аі`). Matins proceeds with the Exapostilarion of All Saints, its Theotokion, and the Praises (Lauds, *На хвали́техъ*), comprising 4 Resurrectional stichera in Tone 8, 1 Anatolian sticheron in Tone 8, and 3 stichera of All Saints in Tone 4.
[^2794]: The Exapostilarion of All Saints *Крⷭ҇ти́телѧ и҆ Предте́чꙋ* ("The Baptist and Forerunner, the Apostles, Prophets...") is chanted to the melodic model *Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите)*, although the incipit of the automelon is omitted in the printed heading of the 1907 edition.
[^2795]: The melodic model *Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ)* corresponds to the canonical Greek automelon *Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν μάρτυσιν*, sung for the praises of the holy martyrs and righteous ones.
[^2796]: Physical leaf p618 corresponds to printed folio 612 (`х҃в`). Matins concludes with the final sticheron of All Saints, the 1st Matins Gospel Doxasticon in Tone 1 *На го́рꙋ Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ и҆дꙋ́щымъ*, the Great Doxology, and the First Hour with the Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite; followed by the opening of the Divine Liturgy.
[^2797]: The 1st Matins Gospel Doxasticon in Tone 1 *На го́рꙋ Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ и҆дꙋ́щымъ* ("Unto the disciples going into the mountain...") is the work of Emperor Leo VI the Wise, meditating on Christ's Ascension and the promise of His perpetual presence.
[^2798]: At the Divine Liturgy of All Saints, the Beatitudes (Makarismoi) are taken from Tone 8 (4 troparia) and from Ode 6 of the Canon of All Saints (4 troparia).
[^2799]: The Epistle for the Sunday of All Saints is Hebrews 11:33–12:2 (Pericope 330), recounting the heroic faith and sufferings of the Old Testament saints and martyrs who "through faith subdued kingdoms, wrought righteousness, obtained promises."
[^2800]: Physical leaf p619 corresponds to printed folio 613 (`х҃гї`). The Divine Liturgy concludes with the Gospel according to Matthew 10:32-33, 37-38; 19:27-30 (Pericope 38), the Communion Hymn (LXX Ps 148:1 and 32:1), and a rubric governing the celebration when a temple is dedicated to All Saints, featuring a woodcut engraving of the Mother of God and Child.
[^2801]: The rubric notes that at the agape meal (*на трапе́зѣ*), consolation is given to the brethren in anticipation of the Apostles' Fast (*гото́вѧщымся къ по́стномꙋ по́двигꙋ*), which customarily begins on the Monday following All Saints Sunday (or the second Monday after Pentecost in ancient Ruthenian monastic Typika).
[^2802]: For a parish or monastery dedicated to All Saints, the Typikon directs a full Vigil service with Polyeleos, Megalynarion (*Велича́емъ ва́съ А҆́пⷭ҇ли*), and Selected Psalm (LXX Ps 111:1 *Бл҃же́нъ мꙋ́жъ боя́йся Гдⷭ҇а*).
[^2803]: The woodcut engraving depicts the Most Holy Mother of God holding the divine Infant Christ in an oval mandorla of glory, symbolizing the Church of the Firstborn who surround the Queen of Heaven.
[^2804]: Physical leaf p620 corresponds to printed folio 614 (`х҃ді`). Opening of the Office of the Feast of the Most Holy Mystery of the Eucharist (Corpus Christi), celebrated on Thursday after the Sunday of All Saints, adorned with an elaborate headpiece engraving of the Eucharistic mystery.
[^2805]: The celebration of the Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist (Corpus Christi / *Боже́ственнаго Тѣ́ла*) on the Thursday following All Saints Sunday is a distinctive monument of the Ruthenian Greco-Catholic liturgical recension, formally confirmed for the Ruthenian Church at the Synod of Zamość (1720) and preserved in the 1907 Lviv Stauropegion Pentecostarion.
[^2806]: The opening stichera at "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 6 meditate on the institution of the Holy Mystery of the Eucharist at the Mystical Supper (Matthew 26:26-28; 1 Corinthians 11:23-26), calling Christ the "Living Bread" (*Хлѣ́бъ живо́тный*) and "Heavenly Bread" (*Хлѣ́бъ сы́й нбⷭ҇ный*).
[^2807]: The fourth sticheron juxtaposes the earthly manna eaten by the Israelites in the wilderness with the true Bread from Heaven (John 6:49-51, 58), the Angelic Bread (*Хлѣ́бъ А҆́гг҃льскїй*) that imparts everlasting life.
[^2808]: The Doxasticon in Tone 6 summons all lovers of the feasts to rejoice without the "old leaven of malice" (1 Corinthians 5:7-8), partaking of the Lamb of God Who takes away the sins of the world (John 1:29).
[^2809]: Physical leaf p621 corresponds to printed folio 615 (`х҃єі`). Conclusion of the Doxasticon at Great Vespers for the Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist (Corpus Christi), followed by the Entrance and the Old Testament Readings (Paroimiai).
