[^2810]: The first Old Testament Reading is Exodus 19:10–19, recounting the Lord's descent upon Mount Sinai in fire and smoke to sanctify Israel, prefiguring the awe and moral purification required before partaking of the Holy Mysteries.
[^2811]: The second Old Testament Reading is 1 Kingdoms (1 Samuel) 21:1–6, in which Ahimelech the priest gives the holy Showbread (Bread of Presentation) to David and his companions after ascertaining their ritual purity, prefiguring the Eucharist as the Holy Bread reserved for the faithful.
[^2812]: The third Old Testament Reading is 3 Kingdoms (1 Kings) 17:1–9, wherein ravens miraculously nourish the Prophet Elijah at the brook Cherith with bread and meat, pointing forward to the miraculous food from heaven.
[^2813]: Physical leaf p622 corresponds to printed folio 616 (`х҃ѕі`). Conclusion of the third reading (3 Kingdoms 17:10–16, Elijah and the widow of Zarephath with the unfailing jar of meal and cruse of oil), followed by the Litiya stichera in Tone 6, the Doxasticon in Tone 2, and the opening of the Aposticha in Tone 1.
[^2814]: The first Litiya sticheron in Tone 6 evokes King David leaping and dancing before the Ark of the Covenant (2 Kingdoms / 2 Samuel 6:14–16), contrasting the shadow of the old Ark (which contained the manna and tables of stone) with Christ Himself, the living Lawgiver and incarnate God concealed in the Eucharistic bread.
[^2815]: The second Litiya sticheron recalls the Ark of the Covenant captured by the Philistines and set in the temple of Dagon (1 Kingdoms / 1 Samuel 5:2–4), where the idol fell prostrate and was shattered before the glory of the Lord.
[^2816]: The third and fourth Litiya stichera beseech Christ, the Lamb of God and supernatural Bread, not to consume the communicant as an idol or sinner, but to deify and enlighten through His Body and Blood.
[^2817]: Physical leaf p623 corresponds to printed folio 617 (`х҃зі`). Stichera at the Aposticha (with psalm verses LXX Ps. 77:25 and 77:24), Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 8, Festal Troparion in Tone 7, Dismissal of Vespers, and the opening of Matins.
[^2818]: The Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 8 *Ѡ҆ пресла́вное чꙋ́до* ("O marvelous wonder! The Living and Divine Bread...") cites St. Paul's injunction that each examine himself before eating and drinking (1 Corinthians 11:28).
[^2819]: The Festal Troparion in Tone 7 *Хлѣ́бъ преесте́ственный* ("The supernatural Bread, which Angels and Archangels desire to behold with trembling...") is the primary troparion of the Feast of Corpus Christi in the Ruthenian recension.
[^2820]: The Dismissal of Vespers commemorates the institution of the Eucharist at the Mystical Supper ("giving His own Body to eat and His true Blood to drink for our salvation") alongside the twelve holy Apostles.
[^2821]: Physical leaf p624 corresponds to printed folio 618 (`х҃иі`). Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma in Tone 4, Polyeleos with Megalynarion and Selected Psalm verses, Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, and the 1st Antiphon of the Songs of Ascents in Tone 4.
[^2822]: The Sessional Hymn after the 2nd Kathisma is sung to the melodic model *Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ)*, quoting Christ's Eucharistic discourse in John 6:54: "Whoso eats My Flesh, and drinks My Blood, hath eternal life; and I will raise him up at the last day."
[^2823]: The Megalynarion for the Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist: *Велича́емъ тѧ̀ Живодв҃че Хрⷭ҇тѐ* ("We magnify Thee, O Christ Giver of life, Who for our sake didst change bread into Thy Body and wine into Thy Blood...").
[^2824]: The Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos is chanted to the melodic model *Tone 4, Podoben: "The beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства)*, meditating on the transition from the Old Passover to the New Mystical Passover in the Upper Room.
[^2825]: Physical leaf p625 corresponds to printed folio 619 (`х҃ѳі`). Matins Prokeimenon in Tone 4 (quoting John 6:54 and LXX Ps. 77:25), Matins Gospel (John 6:55–58, Pericope 24), Psalm 50, Post-Gospel Sticheron in Tone 6, and Ode 1 of the Festive Canon in Tone 2.
[^2826]: The Matins Gospel pericope is John 6:55–58: "For My Flesh is food indeed, and My Blood is drink indeed..."
[^2827]: The Post-Gospel Sticheron in Tone 6 *Дне́сь А҆́ггли оу҆жаса́ются* describes the angels filled with awe beholding the incarnate God hidden within the Eucharistic species and received by mortals.
[^2828]: The Canon of the Feast is composed in Tone 2 to the heirmos *Грядѣ́те лю́дїе* ("Come, O ye peoples, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea..."), with the festal refrain: *Сла́ва Хрⷭ҇тѐ Пло́ти твое́й и҆ Кро́ви* ("Glory, O Christ, to Thy Body and Blood!").
[^2829]: Physical leaf p626 corresponds to printed folio 620 (`х҃к`). Ode 3 of the Canon, Sessional Hymn in Tone 1 with its Theotokion, and Ode 4 (opening).
[^2830]: The Sessional Hymn after Ode 3 is sung to the melodic model *Tone 1, Podoben: "The stone being sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ)*, praying that communion of the Holy Mysteries be not unto judgment or condemnation (cf. 1 Corinthians 11:29).
[^2831]: Ode 4 begins with the heirmos *Прише́лъ є҆сѝ ѿ Дв҃ы* ("Thou didst come forth from the Virgin, neither an envoy nor an angel..."), based on the Canticle of Habakkuk (Habakkuk 3).
[^2832]: In the third troparion of Ode 4, the communicant is portrayed standing with fear and trembling, eyes fixed on the Crucified Savior Whose outstretched hands bestow divine communion.
[^2833]: Physical leaf p627 corresponds to printed folio 621 (`х҃ка`). Conclusion of Ode 4, Ode 5 with heirmos *Просвѣще́нїе во тмѣ̀ лежа́щихъ*, and Ode 6 with heirmos *Въ бе́зднѣ грѣхо́внѣй валя́ѧся*.
[^2834]: The heirmos of Ode 5 *Просвѣще́нїе во тмѣ̀ лежа́щихъ* ("O illumination of them that lie in darkness...") is based on the Canticle of Isaiah (Isaiah 26:9–20). The second troparion invokes the burning coal of Isaiah 6:6–7 (*оу҆́гль*) to purge the darkened soul.
[^2835]: The heirmos of Ode 6 *Въ бе́зднѣ грѣхо́внѣй валя́ѧся* ("Wallowing in the abyss of sin...") reflects the Canticle of Jonah (Jonah 2).
[^2836]: The third troparion of Ode 6 implores Christ to grant the communicant to approach His Divine Mysteries with fear and trembling, receiving His true and holy Body and His true, holy, and precious Blood.
[^2837]: Physical leaf p628 corresponds to printed folio 622 (`х҃кв`). Ode 6 Theotokion, Katavasia, Kontakion and Ikos in Tone 2, and the beginning of the historical Synaxarion for the Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist.
[^2838]: The Kontakion in Tone 2 *Сѐ предлежи́тъ Хрⷭ҇то́съ на пи́щꙋ всѣ̑мъ* ("Behold, Christ is set forth as food for all...") implores the Lord that the reception of the Mysteries not burn the unworthy, but consume sins and purify souls.
[^2839]: The Synaxarion for Corpus Christi is a major historical and canonical monument of the Ruthenian Greek Catholic Church, detailing how the local Synod of Zamość (1720) formally adopted this solemn feast from the Roman Church to glorify the Holy Eucharist and confirm the faithful in Catholic dogma. Pope Urban IV instituted the universal feast of Corpus Christi in 1264 through the bull *Transiturus de hoc mundo*.
[^2840]: The Synaxarion cites St. John Chrysostom's Eucharistic homilies (on 1 Corinthians 10 and Matthew 26) regarding how Christ feeds believers with His own Body and Blood like a mother nursing her infant, and references the Council of Trent's decrees (Session XIII, Decree on the Eucharist, Canon 6) approving Eucharistic processions (*літі́а* / *processio*) through public squares and streets.
[^2841]: Physical leaf p629 corresponds to printed folio 623 (`х҃кг`). Conclusion of the Synaxarion, followed by Ode 7 and Ode 8 of the Festive Canon.
[^2842]: The heirmos of Ode 7 *Тѣ́лꙋ злато́мꙋ на по́ли Деи́рѣ слꙋжи́мꙋ* ("When the golden image was worshiped on the plain of Dura...") is based on the Canticle of the Three Holy Youths (Daniel 3:1–23).
[^2843]: The heirmos of Ode 8 *Ѽ подо́бїи зла́тѣ небре́гше* ("Disregarding the golden image, the thrice-blessed youths...") continues the theme of the Three Holy Children in the furnace (Daniel 3:57–88).
[^2844]: The third troparion of Ode 8 expresses the tremulous mystery of communion: "I shudder receiving Fire, lest I be burned as wax and as grass... How do I, who am clay, partake of the Divine Body and Blood, and am made incorruptible?"
[^2845]: Physical leaf p630 corresponds to printed folio 624 (`х҃кд`). Katavasia of Ode 8, special Festal Megalynaria (refrains) for Ode 9, Ode 9 Heirmos and troparia, Katavasia of Ode 9, and the Exapostilarion (Photogogic) with its Theotokion.
[^2846]: Special refrains are prescribed for Ode 9: *Велича́й дꙋшѐ моѧ̀ Гдⷭ҇а...* and *Велича́й дꙋшѐ моѧ̀ Хрⷭ҇та̀ Бг҃а на́шего...* The heirmos of Ode 9 in Tone 2 *Ве́сь є҆сѝ жела́нїе, ве́сь сла́дость* ("Thou art all desire, Thou art all sweetness, O Word of God...") is based on the Song of Songs (Canticle of Canticles 5:16).
[^2847]: The Exapostilarion *Оу҆чени́кѡмъ сѣдѧ́щымъ на Та́йнѣй Ве́чери* ("While the disciples were sitting at the Mystical Supper...") recalls Christ lifting His eyes to heaven to God the Father, blessing, breaking, and giving the bread to His disciples.
[^2848]: The Theotokion of the Exapostilarion meditates on the incarnational continuity of the Eucharist: the very same Christ now worshiped and received in the Holy Mysteries is He Whom the Virgin Mary bore in her maternal womb and held in her arms.
[^2849]: Physical leaf p631 corresponds to printed folio 625 (`х҃кє`). Stichera at the Praises (Lauds) in Tone 1, Doxasticon in Tone 6, Dismissal of Matins, and the Opening of the Divine Liturgy with the Festal Antiphons.
[^2850]: The Praises are sung to the melodic model *Tone 1, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ ди́вное чꙋ́до)*, beginning with an incipit evoking the Prologue of St. John's Gospel (John 1:14): "The Word was made Flesh, and dwelt among us, and we beheld His glory, the glory as of the Only-begotten of the Father."
[^2851]: The third Sticheron at the Praises quotes the invitation of Divine Wisdom from Proverbs 9:1–5: "Wisdom hath builded her house... she hath slain her beasts, she hath mingled her wine; she hath also furnished her table... Come, eat of my bread, and drink of the wine which I have mingled."
[^2852]: The Doxasticon at the Praises in Tone 6 quotes Christ's Eucharistic discourse in John 6:51, 53–54: "I am the living Bread which came down from heaven... Except ye eat the Flesh of the Son of man, and drink His Blood, ye have no life in you; whoso eats My Flesh, and drinks My Blood, hath eternal life; and I will raise him up at the last day."
[^2853]: The Festal Antiphons at the Divine Liturgy are taken from Psalm 22 (LXX Ps. 22): "The Lord is my shepherd..." (Antiphon 1).
[^2854]: Physical leaf p632 corresponds to printed folio 626 (`х҃кѕ`). Conclusion of the First Antiphon, Second Antiphon (Psalm 115 / LXX Ps. 115), Third Antiphon (Psalm 4 / LXX Ps. 4 and Psalm 106 / LXX Ps. 106), and the Festal Entrance Verse (Introit).
[^2855]: The refrains of the Second Antiphon interpolate a proper festal response: "Save us, O Son of God, Who didst feed us with Thy Flesh, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia!"
[^2856]: The Festal Entrance Verse is taken from Psalm 115:8 (LXX Ps. 115:8): "I will sacrifice unto Thee a sacrifice of praise, and will call upon the name of the Lord."
[^2857]: Physical leaf p633 corresponds to printed folio 627 (`х҃кз`). Conclusion of the Liturgy of the Feast (Troparion, Kontakion, Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, Heirmos of Ode 9 as Zadostoinik, Communion Hymn, Dismissal), followed by Great Vespers on Thursday Evening (First Day of the Octave).
[^2858]: The Epistle pericope is 1 Corinthians 11:23–32 (Pericope 149), recounting the Apostle Paul's tradition of the institution of the Holy Eucharist at the Mystical Supper and the necessity of self-examination before partaking.
[^2859]: The Gospel pericope is John 6:48–54 (Pericope 23), concluding Christ's discourse on the Bread of Life.
[^2860]: In place of "It is truly meet" (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), the Church prescribes the Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Festal Canon with its Refrain: *Ве́сь є҆сѝ жела́нїе* ("Thou art all desire...").
[^2861]: The Communion Verse (Communion Hymn) is John 6:56: "He that eats My Flesh, and drinks My Blood, abides in Me, and I in him. Alleluia."
[^2862]: The Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" on Thursday Evening recall the eternal Priesthood of Christ after the order of Melchizedek (LXX Ps. 109:4, Hebrews 5–7), Who offered bread and wine to the Father.
[^2863]: Physical leaf p634 corresponds to printed folio 628 (`х҃ки`). Conclusion of "Lord, I have cried", Doxasticon in Tone 8, Entrance with the censer, Great Prokeimenon in Tone 7, and Stichera at the Aposticha in Tone 2.
[^2864]: The Sticheron on fol. 628 weaves together LXX Ps. 110:4 ("He hath made a remembrance of His wonders"), LXX Ps. 110:2 ("The works of the Lord are great, sought out of all them that have pleasure therein"), and Philippians 2:9–10 ("Therefore God also hath highly exalted Him, and given Him a name which is above every name: That at the name of Jesus every knee should bow, of things in heaven, and things on earth, and things under the earth").
[^2865]: The Great Prokeimenon in Tone 7 is LXX Ps. 76:14, 15, 11, 12: "Who is so great a God as our God? Thou art the God Who doest wonders."
[^2866]: The Stichera at the Aposticha are sung to the melodic model *Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ)*.
[^2867]: Physical leaf p635 corresponds to printed folio 629 (`х҃кѳ`). Conclusion of the Aposticha on Thursday Evening, Dismissal, and Matins on Friday (Day 2 of the Octave) with Stichera at the Aposticha.
[^2868]: For the Sessional Hymns at Matins on Friday, the rubric directs the reader back to printed folio 617 (`х҃зi`, physical leaf p623).
[^2869]: The Stichera at the Aposticha at Friday Matins are sung to the melodic model *Tone 6, Podoben: "Having risen on the third day" (Тридне́венъ воскре́слъ)*.
[^2870]: The second Sticheron contrasts the Virgin Mary's womb, which contained the uncontainable God, with the Eucharistic bread, wherein the same God is enclosed in a small crumb (*въ крꙋпи́цы ма́лой хлѣ́ба затворе́нный*).
[^2871]: Physical leaf p636 corresponds to printed folio 630 (`х҃л`). Conclusion of the Aposticha of Friday Matins, Doxasticon in Tone 4, and Great Vespers on Friday Evening with Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 5.
[^2872]: The Doxasticon in Tone 4 cites 1 Corinthians 5:8: "Therefore let us keep the feast, not with old leaven, neither with the leaven of malice and wickedness; but with the unleavened bread of sincerity and truth."
[^2873]: Great Vespers on Friday Evening begins the liturgical commemoration of Saturday within the Octave.
[^2874]: The first Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" on Friday Evening expounds Isaac's blessing of Jacob (Genesis 27:28: "God give thee of the dew of heaven, and the fatness of the earth, and plenty of corn and wine") as an Old Testament type of the Eucharist.
[^2875]: The second Sticheron opens with the imagery of Psalm 41:2 (LXX Ps. 41:2): "As the hart pants after the water brooks, so pants my soul after Thee, O God."
[^2876]: Physical leaf p637 corresponds to printed folio 631 (`х҃ла`). Conclusion of "Lord, I have cried" on Friday Evening, Doxasticon in Tone 3, Prokeimenon of the day, Stichera at the Aposticha in Tone 2, and Doxasticon in Tone 6.
[^2877]: The Doxasticon in Tone 3 evokes the apocalyptic vision of Revelation 4:4, 10–11 and 5:6–14, comparing the faithful falling prostrate before the Eucharist to the twenty-four elders falling down before the Lamb upon the throne.
[^2878]: The Stichera at the Aposticha in Tone 2 employ Psalm verses from LXX Ps. 110:4 ("He hath made a remembrance of His wonders") and LXX Ps. 110:9 ("He sent redemption unto His people"), along with LXX Ps. 33:9 ("Taste and see that the Lord is good").
[^2879]: Physical leaf p638 corresponds to printed folio 632 (`х҃лв`). Matins on Saturday (Day 3 of the Octave) with Stichera at the Aposticha in Tone 1, Great Vespers on Saturday Evening, and Sunday Matins rubrics.
[^2880]: The Stichera at the Aposticha on Saturday morning are sung to the melodic model *Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly hosts" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ)*, meditating on the Upper Room and Christ's words in Luke 22:15–18.
[^2881]: The rubric for Saturday Vespers and Sunday Matins provides the Typikon order for combining the Sunday of the Octave of Corpus Christi with the Resurrectional Octoechos (Tone 6).
[^2882]: Physical leaf p639 corresponds to printed folio 633 (`х҃лг`). Great Vespers on Sunday Evening (Octave Day 4) with Stichera at "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 6, Doxasticon in Tone 3, and Stichera at the Aposticha in Tone 6.
[^2883]: The first Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" recounts the preparation of the Passover in Matthew 26:17–28 and Luke 22:7–20.
[^2884]: The second Sticheron recalls King David bringing the Ark of the Covenant from the house of Obed-edom to Jerusalem with dancing and instruments (2 Kingdoms / 2 Samuel 6:10–15), identifying the Ark as a prefiguration of the Holy of Holies of the Eucharistic Body and Blood. The Church Slavonic reads *А҆веда́рова* (representing Obed-edom, Hebrew *‘Ōḇēḏ-’Ĕḏōm*).
[^2885]: The Stichera at the Aposticha on Sunday Evening return to the type of Isaac blessing Jacob and Esau (Genesis 27:28–37).
[^2886]: Physical leaf p640 corresponds to printed folio 634 (`х҃лд`). Conclusion of Sunday Evening Aposticha, Doxasticon in Tone 8, and Matins on Monday (Day 5 of the Octave) with Stichera at the Aposticha in Tone 4 and Doxasticon in Tone 2.
[^2887]: The Aposticha Doxasticon in Tone 8 weaves together Christ's invitation in John 7:37–38 ("If any man thirst, let him come unto Me, and drink. He that believs on Me, as the scripture hath said, out of his belly shall flow rivers of living water") with John 6:56.
[^2888]: The Stichera at the Aposticha at Monday Matins are sung to the melodic model *Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ)*.
[^2889]: The Doxasticon in Tone 2 marvels at the Eucharistic presence: how He Whom heaven and earth cannot contain is wholly present in a small host (*въ ма́ницѣ*) and particle of bread, with His Godhead, Flesh, and Divine Soul. The Slavonic *ма́ницѣ* is the locative of *ма́ница* (from Latin *mica* / *manna*, here referring to the consecrated Host / particle).
[^2890]: Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist (Corpus Christi) Octave. The feast of the Most Holy Eucharist was instituted in the Ruthenian Church by the Provincial Council of Zamość (1720), with an eight-day post-festive octave concluding on the following Thursday.
[^2891]: Ps. 110:9 LXX: *He sent redemption unto His people: He hath commanded His covenant for ever.*
[^2892]: Ps. 110:4 LXX: *The Lord hath made a memorial of His wonders: merciful, compassionate, and righteous.*
[^2893]: Ps. 147:1-3 LXX: *Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem; praise thy God, O Sion. For He hath strengthened the bars of thy gates; He hath blessed thy children within thee... He fills thee with the fat of wheat.*
[^2894]: Internal cross-reference: *(see fol. 632)*. Physical leaf coordinate: Physical Leaf p638 = Printed Folio 632 + 6.
[^2895]: Ps. 110:5 LXX: *He hath given food unto them that fear Him: He will ever be mindful of His covenant.*
[^2896]: Ps. 77:24 LXX: *And He rained down manna upon them to eat, and gave them the bread of heaven.*
[^2897]: John 6:55-56: *For My Flesh is meat indeed, and My Blood is drink indeed. He that eats My Flesh, and drinks My Blood, dwells in Me, and I in him.*
[^2898]: Ps. 33:9 LXX: *Taste and see that the Lord is good: blessed is the man that hopes in Him.*
[^2899]: John 6:51: *I am the living Bread which came down from heaven: if any man eat of this Bread, he shall live for ever.*
[^2900]: Rubrical decree citing the Provincial Council of Lviv (1720): The Church Slavonic text explicitly references *Собо́ръ Львóвскїй бы́вшїй въ лѣ́то ҂аѱч҃а* (The Council of Lviv held in the year 1720), which designates the landmark Provincial Synod of Zamość (1720) celebrated under Metropolitan Leo Kiška and confirmed for the Metropolitan province of Kyiv, Halych, and All Rus' centered in Lviv. The synod made the Feast of the Most Holy Eucharist an obligatory Great Feast of the Lord, celebrated on the Thursday after All Saints Sunday with solemn commemoration on the Second Sunday after Pentecost.
[^2902]: Graphic woodcut headpiece depicting the Most Holy Theotokos holding the Christ Child in Her arms, flanked by floral festoons, with traditional Greek monograms MP OY and IC XC.
[^2901]: Feast of the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos (*Сострада́нїе Престѧ́тыѧ Богоро́дицы*, *Compassio Beatae Mariae Virginis*). In the liturgical usage of the Ukrainian Greco-Catholic Church following the Synod of Zamość, this feast is appointed for the Friday of the Second Week after All Saints Sunday, immediately following the Octave of Corpus Christi.
[^2903]: Typographical variant in the 1907 Lviv print witness: printed *прекезнача́льнагѡ* (with Cyrillic *k* instead of *b*), emended in translation to *пребезнача́льнагѡ* ("unoriginate" / "pre-eternal"). The leaf also exhibits *Ст҃рти* for *Стра̑сти* ("Passions").
[^2904]: Typographical error in rubric heading: printed *Глака̀ а҃* with Cyrillic *k* instead of *v* for *Глава̀ а҃* ("Chapter 1").
[^2905]: First Old Testament Reading (Paremia I) at Vespers: composite reading from the Lamentations of Jeremiah: Lam. 1:1-2, 1:3-4 (excerpt), 1:9, 1:11-12, 1:13, 1:16-17, 1:20.
[^2906]: Second Old Testament Reading (Paremia II) at Vespers: composite reading from the Lamentations of Jeremiah: Lam. 2:13, 2:15-17, 2:18-20; 3:7, 3:12-13, 3:15, 3:19, 3:21.
[^2907]: The word *[є҆́сть]* is set within typographical square brackets directly in the 1907 Church Slavonic print witness (Lam. 3:26: *бла́го [є҆́сть] и҆ надѣ́ющейсѧ съ молча́нїемъ спасе́нїѧ Бж҃їѧ*), indicating an editorial grammatical copula restoration in the biblical text.
[^2908]: Third Old Testament Reading (Paremia III) at Vespers: composite reading from the Lamentations of Jeremiah: Lam. 3:40-41, 3:55-59; 4:1-2, 4:7, 4:11, 4:20; 5:19, 5:21.
[^2909]: Ps. 21:2 LXX: *O God, my God, attend to me: why hast Thou forsaken me? The words of my transgressions are far from my salvation.*
[^2910]: Ps. 24:17 LXX: *The troubles of my heart are multiplied: bring Thou me out of my distresses.*
[^2911]: Typographical error in the 1907 Lviv print witness: printed *кѡлѣ́зни* with Cyrillic *k* instead of *b* (*болѣ́зни*, "sorrows" / "pangs"), recurring also in the Megalynarion on physical leaf p649.
[^2912]: Ps. 56:9 LXX (and Ps. 107:3 LXX): *Awake, my glory; awake, psaltery and harp: I myself will awake early.*
[^2913]: Ps. 44:18 LXX: *They shall remember Thy name in every generation: therefore shall the peoples praise Thee for ever, and unto the age of the age.*
[^2914]: Ps. 51:11 LXX: *I will praise Thee for ever, because Thou hast done it: and I will wait on Thy name; for it is good before Thy saints.*
[^2915]: Ps. 27:7 LXX: *The Lord is my helper and my defender: my heart hoped in Him, and I have been helped; and my flesh hath flourished again, and willingly will I confess Him.*
[^2916]: Gospel Reading at Matins: John pericope 64 (John 19:25-37), the Crucifixion narrative of Christ with His Mother and the Beloved Disciple at the foot of the Cross.
[^2917]: Canon of the Compassion (Lamentation of the Theotokos at the Passion of Christ) attributed to Saint Symeon the Metaphrast / the Logothete (*Творе́нїе Сѷмеѡ́на Логоѳе́та*).
[^2918]: Typographical error in Katavasia text: printed *Ѡ҆тке́рзꙋ* with Cyrillic *k* instead of *v* (*Ѡ҆тве́рзꙋ*, "I shall open").
[^2919]: Sessional Hymn: petition for deliverance from visible and invisible enemies through the intercession of the Theotokos.
[^2920]: Psalm 82:17 [83:16]: "Fill their faces with dishonor; and they shall seek Thy name, O Lord" (Πλήρωσον τὰ πρόσωπα αὐτῶν ἀτιμίας / И҆спо́лни ли́ца и҆́хъ безче́стїѧ).
[^2921]: Ode 4 Heirmos: Tone 6, based on Habakkuk 3:2.
[^2922]: "the noble Joseph": Saint Joseph of Arimathea (ὁ εὐσχήμων Ἰωσήφ / бл҃гоѡбра́зный І҆ѡ́сифъ, Mark 15:43).
[^2923]: Ode 5 Heirmos: Tone 6, based on Isaiah 26:9.
[^2924]: Ode 6 Heirmos: Tone 6, based on Jonah 2:3-7.
[^2925]: Kontakion, Tone 8: Attributed to Saint Romanos the Melodist (fl. 6th c.), from his famous Kontakion *On the Crucifixion* (*Mary at the Cross*).
[^2926]: Ikos: Continuation of Saint Romanos the Melodist's dialogue between the Virgin Mary and Christ on the way to Golgotha, alluding to the first miracle at Cana of Galilee (John 2:1-11).
[^2927]: The Synaxarion for the Feast of the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos (*Сострада́нїе Прест҃ы́ѧ Бцⷣы* / *Compassio Beatae Mariae Virginis*), detailing its liturgical history and theological foundation.
[^2928]: The Provincial Council of Cologne (*Concilium Coloniense*), convoked in 1423 under Archbishop-Elector Dietrich II von Moers, instituted the Feast of the Sorrows/Compassion of Our Lady (*Commemoratio angustiae et doloris B. Mariae V.*) to counter the iconoclastic sacrileges of the Hussites.
[^2929]: Pope Benedict XIV (Prospero Lorenzo Lambertini, 1675–1758; reigned 1740–1758), eminent canonist and liturgical scholar, author of the definitive treatise *De Festis Domini Nostri Jesu Christi et Beatae Mariae Virginis* (1748).
[^2930]: The Hussite iconoclasts in Bohemia and the Rhineland in the early 15th century, who attacked devotion to sacred images, particularly representations of the suffering Mother of God at the Foot of the Cross.
[^2931]: The Provincial Synod of Zamość (*Synodus Provincialis Zamoscena*), convoked in 1720 by Metropolitan Lev Kyshka of Kyiv and the Ruthenian Greek Catholic hierarchy, formally incorporated the Feast of the Compassion of the Theotokos into the liturgical calendar of the Ruthenian Church (Titulus III, *De festis*).
[^2932]: The Tenth Friday after Pascha (*Десята п'ятниця по Пасці*), an ancient popular Ruthenian votive observance dating back to the medieval period, reverenced throughout Galicia, Volhynia, and Belarus. The Synod of Zamość purposefully consecrated this existing folk devotion to the liturgical feast of the Compassion of the Mother of God.
[^2933]: *Hyperphileotheotokos* (Greek: Ὑπερφιλοθεοτόκος, "surpassing in love toward the Mother of God"): A scholarly title used in Western theological and liturgical literature to describe the Greek Patristic tradition's sublime Marian veneration.
[^2934]: Luke 2:35: The prophecy of Saint Simeon at the Presentation: "Yea, a sword shall pierce through thine own soul also" (καὶ σοῦ δὲ αὐτῆς τὴν ψυχὴν διελεύσεται ῥομφαία / И҆ тебѣ́ же само́й дꙋ́шꙋ про́йдетъ ѻ҆рꙋ́жїе).
[^2935]: Saint Augustine of Hippo (354–430), whose sermons on the Passion emphasize the spiritual martyrdom of Mary at the Cross (*passio cordis* / *martyrium spiritus*).
[^2936]: Hebrews 12:2: "Looking unto Jesus the author and finisher of our faith" (ἀφορῶντες εἰς τὸν τῆς πίστεως ἀρχηγὸν καὶ τελειωτὴν Ἰησοῦν).
[^2937]: Ode 7 Heirmos: Tone 6, based on the Prayer of the Three Holy Children in the Babylonian furnace (Daniel 3:49-50 [LXX]).
[^2938]: Luke 1:31-33: The Annunciation of the Archangel Gabriel to the Virgin Mary.
[^2939]: Ode 8 Heirmos: Tone 6, based on the three youths in the furnace (Daniel 3) and the sacrifice of Elijah consumed with water upon Mount Carmel (1 Kings 18:38 [LXX 3 Kings 18:38]).
[^2940]: The printed Slavonic witness reads `волѧ́щꙋю`, an epigraphic error or misprint for `болѧ́щꙋю` ("grieving" / "in sorrow").
[^2941]: Ode 9 Heirmos: Tone 6, canonical Byzantine Marian theophany heirmos.
[^2942]: Melodic model heading: Tone 6, Podoben: "The Angelic Powers" (А҆́гг҃лскїѧ предъиди́те Си́лы). Canonical incipit matching `data/liturgical_primitives_registry.json` (`POD.T6.ANGELSKIYA_SILY`).
[^2943]: John 19:26-27: Christ speaking from the Cross to His Mother and to Saint John the Evangelist.
[^2944]: Saint John the Evangelist and Theologian, honored in Byzantine hymnography under the title of "the virgin disciple" (*дѣ́вственникъ* / παρθένος).
[^2945]: Internal codicological cross-reference: Troparion "With the virgin disciple" (see fol. 638). Physical Leaf coordinate: `Physical Leaf p644 = Printed Folio 638 + 6`.
[^2946]: The customary Marian Antiphons at the Divine Liturgy: Antiphon 1: Psalm 91:2 [92:1]; Antiphon 2: Psalm 92:1 [93:1]; Antiphon 3: Psalm 94:1 [95:1].
[^2947]: Lamentations 1:2: "Weeping, she hath wept in the night, and her tears are on her cheeks" (Κλαίουσα ἔκλαυσεν ἐν νυκτί, καὶ τὰ δάκρυα αὐτῆς ἐπὶ τῶν σιαγόνων αὐτῆς / Пла́ча пла́касѧ въ нощѝ, и҆ сле́зы є҆ѧ̀ на лани́тꙋ є҆ѧ̀).
[^2948]: Judith 13:18-20 (LXX Judith 13:18-20): The praise of Judith by Ozias prince of Judah after the slaying of Holofernes, interpreted typologically of the Mother of God crushing the serpent's head.
[^2949]: Lamentations 1:14: "My strength hath failed within me; for the Lord hath delivered affliction into my hand."
[^2950]: Lamentations 1:16: "He that comforted me is far from me, and he that relieved my soul."
[^2951]: John 19:25-27: Liturgical Gospel pericope 61 of Saint John, read for feasts of the Holy Cross and Compassion of the Mother of God.
[^2952]: Lamentations 1:12, 1:20: The liturgical Communion Hymn for the Compassion of the Theotokos.
[^2953]: Codicological tailpiece: A fine decorative woodcut scroll engraving depicting the Greek letters Alpha and Omega with the Cross of Christ, marking the liturgical conclusion of the Office of the Compassion of the Most Holy Theotokos.
